# Bug round, 2026-10-03 (evening) — continuing the count from 150

Owner's brief: Embarcadero Center (buildings that belong ON TOP of others drawn INSIDE them — the condos and the brick
building on One Maritime Plaza; the decks not navigable, walls blocking the way under them; wrong footprints?); the
hotel by Yerba Buena Center; Union Square's glassy custom buildings z-fighting; trainer mode must NEVER need the
controls (gridlocked junctions must clear themselves); and the recent BUGS.md entries. Target: 100 more (151–250).

- [x] (#507) (owner, 2026-10-06) THE LEGION OF HONOR faces the wrong way: the entrance must face the round fountain (NE, across Legion of Honor Dr), not have it at its side. ## Done

151. **The welcome card no longer holds a hands-off rider** (trainer modes): it waited for a key or a click, which a
     trainer rider without controls can never give — the new trainer soak (tools/trainer-soak.ts, 180 W, no input)
     stood behind it for six minutes. With slow input it now closes itself after two seconds of pedalling or twenty
     seconds up (ui/loading.ts `closeWelcome`, game.ts).
152. **The squeeze comes sooner hands-off**: with the welcome fixed, the soak's stalls were 5–8 s at junctions behind
     buses and cars standing in the box. In the trainer modes the rider's stall valve now opens at 3 s (was 5/8) and the
     per-vehicle valve behind a standing vehicle at 2.5 s (was 5): `traffic.squeezeAfter`, game.ts `handsOff`.
153. **The Fidelity cube is free of 44 Montgomery** (owner): the tower model was fitted to the hull of every part of
     44 Montgomery's outline, which runs 82 m south over the cube on the Market corner. It is now fitted to the tower
     part alone (way/690644023, 49 × 50 m); the outline and podium parts are a separate placeholder (market-east.json).
     (Owner called it McKesson; McKesson's outline is 45 m west — the white-finned tower is 44 Montgomery.)
154. **Overdraw after the sf10 bake**: a scan of 580 placed models downtown for unclaimed baked buildings inside them
     found 34 candidates; checked against each model's TRUE outline (not its fitted rectangle), 32 were neighbours
     outside it — left alone — and two were real: inside California Bank and Trust and the Palace Hotel (placeholders).
155. **Golden Gateway Commons stand ON their podiums** (owner: "the condos … supposed to be ON TOP of buildings are
     appearing INSIDE them"): OSM maps the 21 townhouses as `layer=1` buildings with their own 12–13 m over garage
     podiums of 12 and 13 m (way/588365127, way/588363943), and drawn from the ground each stood wholly inside its
     podium, roofs flush with the deck. A restyle can now give a `base` (render/prepare.ts, landmarks.ts): the building
     keeps its own height and stands that high up. Both groups restyled brick on their podiums (prominent.json).
     Systemic follow-up for the next bake: a `layer>=1` building inside a lower one's footprint should be based on it.
156. **Union Square's glassy towers no longer z-fight their podiums** (owner): a new cross-mesh coplanar detector
     (scratchpad crossplanar.mjs: same-facing triangles within 3 cm in one plane, overlapping, between two meshes)
     found the Four Seasons tower × its 757 Market podium (291 m²) and the Westin St. Francis tower × the 1904 hotel
     (789 m²) — each tower is a part of its podium's outline and shared its walls from the ground. A manifest `inset`
     (render/landmarks.ts, winding-proof) draws a model's footprint in all round; at 0.4 m both are clean (at 0.12 the
     Westin's wall landed on the hotel's recessed window glass and still fought). Nothing is left within 300 m.
157. **One Maritime Plaza's podium is back, and the brick building stands ON it** (owner, #180): yesterday's overdraw
     scan placeholdered the Golden Gateway Garage (way/1177704958) — the 8 m podium the tower's own note says is "left
     to the city" — so the plaza had nothing under it. Restored; the tower no longer claims Bar Sprezzatura
     (way/1391009943, level 2), which had widened its fit over the plaza; that is now a 6 m brick building 8 m up.
158. **No walls across Embarcadero Center's walks** (owner: "walls block the way"): the podium walks are several OSM
     pedestrian ways side by side and crossing, each drawn as its own deck with parapets down both sides — and every
     parapet ran across a neighbour's walking surface, low concrete walls the rider rode through. A deck's parapet is
     now drawn only along the stretches where that side is open, with no other raised walk beside it within 1.5 m of
     height (render/bridges.ts `openRuns`, city.ts `besideOf`). Applies to every raised walk in every city.
159. **Market Street is car-free** (owner): from 10th Street to the Embarcadero only buses and taxis spawn on it, at
     40% of a normal street's density, and a private car never turns onto it while any other exit is legal
     (sim/traffic.ts `carFree`/`mayUse`, region test in game.ts). Taxis are now the sim's own (`taxi`, yellow), and
     the renderer draws exactly those in a taxi livery (street-fleet.ts). Measured over a minute at Powell: 14–19 buses
     and the taxis on car-free Market, 0–2 private cars (ones with no other way out). Streetcars and the rider as ever.
160. **Junction gridlock and cars inside cars (#244, and the owner's bus with a car in it)** — four changes, measured
     with the headless soak (tools/traffic-soak.ts, 600 s) at Pine/Bush and at Union Square:
     - a car that has stopped LOOKING ahead (the 10 s patience valve) still never drives into another vehicle: it looks
       a contact distance, 1.2 m, so a ring of waiting still breaks but nothing is driven through;
     - no traffic spawns in, or turns into, a service alley that leads nowhere (Quincy Street off Pine filled both
       ways and locked for good; `blindAlley`);
     - a refused car is never taken into the junction box anyway: where the line is a metre short of a crosswalk node
       it used to roll in and eighteen cars piled on each other at the start of Geary's next block;
     - refused for want of room beyond for eight seconds, a car picks another exit that has room (`rechoose`; on in the
       game, off in the unit crossroads tests whose exact frames it perturbs).
     Pine/Bush: stuck > 60 s 21 → 0, deep overlaps 434 → 314 pair-seconds. Union Square: stuck 60 → 31, overlaps
     11 090 → 310. (`DEBUG_EDGE=<id>` on the soak prints every vehicle on one street every 5 s.) Remaining overlap:
     a long bus stopped on an 8 m connector inside a junction cluster with its tail over the car behind it.
161. **Trainer mode, hands off, verified**: after #151–152 and #160 the hands-off soak (180 W, no input of any kind,
     8 min from Union Square) rode 2 386 m with one stall — the welcome card's own 2 s. Before: six minutes behind the
     welcome card, then 5–8 s stalls behind buses standing in junction boxes.
162. **The Marriott Marquis by Yerba Buena** (owner: "the hotel around YB Center is still pretty bad"): the model was
     fitted to the hotel's whole relation — the tower AND four 24 m wings round a court — so a 133 m ziggurat 160 ×
     76 m filled the block, with neighbours standing in a "courtyard" inside it. Fitted now to the tower part alone
     (way/436648569, 89 × 106 m); the wings are the city's. Repainted from Mapillary: pale cream precast and dark glass,
     where it was light grey-blue stripes.
163. **Every Market Street intersection photographed** (owner: "take a picture of EVERY intersection on Market Street
     … and verify ALL of them"): 30 junctions from Van Ness to Steuart, from the ride graph's own nodes, each shot
     looking up and down Market (scratchpad mkt_sheet0–5). Defects found: the Path of Gold standards standing in the
     MEDIAN where Market is mapped as two one-way carriageways (Van Ness, 10th, 7th, 2nd …) and corner news racks in
     the roadway. Not found anywhere: missing buildings, broken junctions, floating objects. Note: Market's ride
     graph ends at Steuart — The Embarcadero junction itself is a plaza in OSM.
164. **No lamps or racks in Market's median**: a pavement point is skipped when another way of the SAME name runs
     nearer to it than its own — the inside of a street mapped as two ways (render/city.ts `insideTwin`, from the
     tile and its neighbours: the twin is often in the next tile). Applies to every dual-carriageway street.
165. **Ferries that work** (owner): at the Ferry Building, Sausalito or Treasure Island a pill offers the trip (T, or
     tap); "Where do you want to go?" lists the line's in-box terminals with the $5 fare; picked, the screen fades to
     black, the rider arrives on the nearest street at the other terminal, and the fare comes off the delivery cash
     (it may go below zero: travel is never refused). game/stations.ts, ui/travel.ts. Tested: Ferry Building →
     Sausalito, Ferry Building → Treasure Island.
166. **Muni Metro, select stops** (owner: "maybe just do select ones … the rider mostly wants to avoid hills"): ten
     stops for $1 — the Market Street subway (Embarcadero, Montgomery, Powell, Civic Center), Church & Market, Castro
     (under Twin Peaks' shoulder), Carl & Cole and 9th & Irving (the N's Sunset Tunnel under Buena Vista), 4th & King
     and Chase Center — every one inside the box. Tested Powell → Carl & Cole. The card stays open when the rider rolls
     on past the stop; its number keys are its own while open.
167. **The cable-car turntables turn the cars** (owner: "we should make the turntables function!"): a cable car at
     the end of its line was reversed in one frame (heading 171° → −9°); it now stands nine seconds and is pushed
     round half a turn on the spot, starting from the way it arrived (render/life.ts `spin`); one standing at the end
     of a one-way track in sight turns the same way. Measured at Powell & Market: the car stops at the line end, stands
     9 s, leaves the other way. Caveat: OSM's Powell track ends ~18 m short of the turntable's own node, so the car
     turns just north of the drawn turntable rather than on it — to align next.
168. **The Embarcadero piers stop z-fighting along their waterline**: the pier kit's dark substructure rose 0.4 m into
     the deck and was flush with its sides, and the shed's walls ran down through the deck — the largest coplanar
     surfaces in the city by the self-coplanar scan (Pier 23: 35 282 m²; Piers 1–48, Fort Mason). Substructure
     now stops 2 cm under the deck, 5 cm in; the shed starts on the deck. Pier 23 after: 3 m² (pile caps).
169. **Mid-Market's dressed towers stop z-fighting**: market-west.ts's `specPlus` handed its overlays (fins,
     mullions, frames) the building's own ring, so each was a second wall in the walls' plane. They now get a ring 3 cm
     proud (`nudge`). NEMA North 17 808 → 693 m², NEMA South 12 830 → 470, Market Square 16 708 → 999, the Warfield
     3 811 → 0 (residue: fins meeting at corners).
170. **1145 Market Street**: its mullion overlay doubled the fin wall top to bottom (4 230 m²) — now proud of it (5 m²).
171. **The War Memorial Opera House**: the stage house shared the hall's back walls for 27 m (6 481 m²) — drawn 5 cm
     inside them now (3 m²).
172. **Union Square's flights stop flickering**: the Powell and Geary flights and the four Stockton/Post stairs were
     treads NESTED back to the deck, so every tread shared its cheeks and ends with the others, in alternating granite
     colours; each tread is now only its own step. The lawn panels lifted off the paving bands (1 cm apart before).
     Coplanar scan, now colour-aware (same-colour overlaps are invisible and no longer counted): 2 992 → 800 m², the
     rest buried at the garage's base.
173. **Powell Station's stairs are one flight, not a bank of four** (owner): Hallidie Plaza's stairs and escalators are
     several OSM `steps` ways a few metres apart, each drawn with its own cheeks and rails — a block of white walls.
     A flight running the same way within 4 m of one already drawn is now the same stair; flights underground
     (tunnel, layer < 0) are not drawn at all (render/stairs.ts). Applies to every city.
174. **The Furniture Mart's tower bay no longer stands in Market Street** (owner, Market at 10th): its 48 m tower bay,
     a strip off the Market frontage, ran out past the building into the road. The bay is now clipped to the
     building's own outline (and tried on the other side of the frontage where that side was the street); where it
     cannot fit the bay is left off — to model properly next.
175. **The "square over the water" by the Ferry Building** (owner, 37.793672, −122.389667): a Pier 14 entry drew a
     172 × 126 m railing round the bounding box of the breakwater with no deck. Pier 14 is the long walkway OSM draws
     itself; the entry is removed (s1.json, batches/s1.ts).
176. **The Exploratorium's footbridge is a light steel bridge**, not a Seine bridge: outside Paris an unnamed bridge
     over water now takes a steel girder with an iron railing (`STEEL_OVER_WATER`, bridge-structures.ts) instead of
     two great stone arches (owner, 37.802649, −122.396803).
177. **Pier 45's sheds have roofs their own size** (owner: "weird red roofs that are way too big"): Sheds A, B and D
     roofed the footprint's axis-aligned box plus 15% — on sheds turned to the shore, red slabs twice their size
     overhanging the road. Now a parapet and a flat grey roof on the outline itself.
178. **A different rider every load** (owner): the player is an avatar-kit body from a random seed, dressed by the
     crowd's own city wardrobe (trousers on the bike; sports kit on foot) — two loads, two riders. Frozen shots keep
     the preset so they repeat; `?rider=<n>` picks a seed (game.ts, crowd-baked.ts `cityClothes` exported).
179. **Eat the food — the pass** (owner): carrying an order, E eats it. Once very late (past the one-star mark) a
     banner offers it for five seconds every minute ("Running very late… press E to eat the oysters on ice — full
     energy, no pay"). Eaten: the energy bar is full, the receipt is one star, $0 fee and tip, and a complaint ("Refund
     requested. Courier reported."); the next offer comes as after a delivery (game/delivery.ts `eat`, `ATE_LINES`,
     game.ts; unit test). Tested in game.
180. **Never stuck on a hill**: a rider still pedalling up a slope too steep to ride gets off and pushes — never
     slower than 1.2 m/s (sim/ride.ts `PUSH_SPEED`). The owner's Sutro Baths path (a 67% bank in the raw-relief zone)
     made zero progress; the trainer is still asked for the real grade.
181. **Paths that stop short are carried on** (owner, twice: Sutro Baths 37.778852, −122.514159 and Lands End
     37.783244, −122.510211): OSM's paths end 5–12 m from the path they meet, never joined at a node, and the rider
     had to turn round. A path's dead end within 12 m of another way's node, within 2.5 m of its height and with
     nothing built between, now runs on to that node (city/ridegraph.ts `closePathGaps`). Bake-time: lands with the
     next bake.
182. **A playground kit** (owner: "reusable procedural models for playground equipment"): render/playground.ts —
     soft surfacing in park colours, a play structure (deck, posts, a pyramid roof, ladder, slide), a swing set (A-frames,
     two to four seats on chains), a climbing net (mast and rope rings), spring riders and benches, laid out along the
     playground as room allows, the choice and colours fixed by its position. Every OSM `leisure=playground` is fitted
     at bake as a box and travels with the sports courts as sport `playground` (city/sports.ts, tools/bake.ts,
     render/sports.ts) — lands with the next bake; Civic Center's two already use it.
183. **Civic Center Plaza** (owner: "playground and sport courts and a little restaurant … large silver towers"): the
     promenade from City Hall to Larkin — tan granite between paved edges, paved over the lawn; the Avenue of Flags at
     OSM's 18 poles; double rows of clipped planes; the garage's brushed-steel vent stacks and stair kiosks; the two
     Helen Diller playgrounds on OSM's rectangles from the kit (the south one with the spiderweb net); two basketball
     half-courts by McAllister (by eye: OSM has none; the café is OSM's own, at Grove). Park trees are cleared from
     the promenade and the playgrounds (a new `Vegetation.clear(ring)`, fed by every landmark ground). Draped on the plaza's slope
     (sf/civic-plaza.ts, sights.json).
184. **Coplanar audit counted slabs that never overlap** (and court lines shimmered): `coplanarSlabs` bucketed
     horizontal faces by height alone, so Civic Center's courts at one end flagged against the promenade paving 60 m
     away. A pair must now also share 2 m cells in plan (tools/nonbuildings.ts) — one Bay Bridge false pair dropped
     out, nothing real did. Court lines raised from 1.5 to 2.5 cm over the surface city-wide (render/sports.ts); the
     garage kiosk roofs no longer sit on their glass boxes.
185. **Presidio Parkway over Lincoln Blvd: a wedge of ground up to the deck, and hanging fins** (owner's two
     screenshots, 37.806467, −122.474234). OSM's bridge ways are only 11 m — just Lincoln's width — and the Parkway
     carries on as `ramp`s on embankments. The street grade (city/grade.ts) files a ramp's corridor as its fill, with
     the round end cap every street has; at the abutment that cap spilled onto Lincoln, lifting 3 m of its carriageway
     to the deck. A ramp's corridor now ends SQUARE at any end no other ramp continues from. The retaining walls then
     dropped to the road at the end frame as triangular fins: they are taken down only to the fill (sampled 1.5 m back),
     and a vertical abutment face closes the end where it stands 1.2 m+ over the ground beyond (render/bridges.ts).
186. **Embarcadero Center's podium level is one rideable level, 8 m up** (owner: "the embarcadero decks are not
     navigable, and walls block the way under them … somewhat the wrong footprints"). A plan of OSM against the models
     showed the four `embarcaderoUnit` plaza decks standing ACROSS Battery, Front, Davis and Drumm — "the gap between
     two towers" is a street — with their piers and well walls in the roadways; and OSM's real promenade (layer 1,
     along the blocks' south side) baked at street level, its skybridges guessed 5.7 m up: a person-high step at
     every bridge. Now: the four entries are gone (DISABLED MODELS above); `embarcaderoPodium` places decks the ways
     take their height from (src/city/sf/bridges.ts `EMBARCADERO_PODIUM_DECKS`): a strip along the promenade and the
     Golden Gateway Garage's roof as an AREA deck (new `DeckDecl.ring`), foot ways on layer 1 only (Washington Street
     is layer 1 too), `vertical` with a new `joins`: the stairs and paths up from the street are graded from the deck
     to whatever they meet below (src/city/bridge-decks.ts step 7). Junction check over the area: 8 jumps → 3, all
     three older than this (the Washington Street bridge north to Golden Gateway Center; a 2 m hump by Drumm). The
     garage roof is brick (the podium model draws it; the garage restyled to 7.4 m under it). Ridden: the promenade
     at a steady 7.8 m over Front Street. A `trails: 'bike'` zone opens the podium's footways to bikes (next bake).
187. **The brick building on One Maritime Plaza renders** (owner): it is the Punch Line (way/104682197, OSM
     `location=roof`, min_height 3, height 6 — inside the garage, so never seen). Restyled brick, 7 m on the 8 m
     podium; a restyle's `height` is now the building's own on its `base` even where OSM gave a min_height
     (render/prepare.ts). The restyle earlier this round had gone to Bar Sprezzatura, which is inside the tower's
     footprint.
188. **Cupid's Span stood in The Embarcadero's roadway** (owner, 37.790952, −122.389875; the earlier "sculpture by the
     Ferry Building in the road"): its lat/lon was typed, 60 m south of the sculpture's own OSM outline
     (way/175010375) in Rincon Park. Moved to that outline's centre (art.json).
189. **A ten-storey building with no roof** (owner, 37.792719, −122.391949: the Army and Navy YMCA, Steuart Street): OSM
     `roof:shape=pyramidal` on an outline wrapped round a light court. The pyramid is a fan from the centroid, and on a
     ring that is not star-shaped about it the triangles fold back and face down — culled, so no roof at all. Such a
     pyramid is now capped flat (render/roofs.ts), with a test on a notched ring.
190. **The Ferry Terminal's South Basin plaza lay under the bay** (owner: "pier near ferry building needs tons of work
     (this is where the ferries actually go)", 37.794588, −122.392631): OSM maps it as a pedestrian area (way/979811996,
     2020) with ~17 benches; drawn on the flattened sea bed it was under the water, its benches standing on the bay.
     A `plazaDeck` on piles fitted to its minimum rectangle, like the Ferry Plaza's (marinas.json). The rest of that
     report — the gangways, the floats, the gates' canopies — stays on the queue.
191. **Models fitted to an OUTLINE were drawn over the models of its parts** (owner's reports B, D, F and more; owner:
     "we are probably replacing a whole building and keeping the buildingparts, OR maybe the other way around" — yes).
     The bake drops an outline that has `building:part`s and keeps the parts; an entry naming the outline is fitted
     to the hull of ALL its parts — including one another entry models. 22 such claims in the manifests; the
     model × model ones (Paramount/St. Regis, Westin St. Francis/its tower, MIRA/its Folsom wing, One Sansome/its
     banking hall, Warfield/its auditorium, NEMA towers/podiums, One Market Plaza/Spear and Steuart) are fixed in
     render/landmarks.ts: a part another model names for itself (`ownedBy`) is left out of the outline's union.
     Drawn overlap after: Westin 100% → 16%, One Sansome 99% → 13%, Warfield 94% → 27%, MIRA 90% → 28%; the Paramount
     (75% left — its other part sits by the St. Regis) now claims its own 148 m tower part.
192. **The Market & Montgomery glass cube stood 2.5 m into the Hobart Building** (owner: z-fighting on the first floor,
     37.789374, −122.401760) and 1 m into 44 Montgomery: a standalone box, trimmed to the gap between them.
193. **Audit tools for buildings drawn into each other**: tools/building-overlaps.ts (claimed rings and baked
     buildings, from the package alone — finds OSM duplicates: 118 generic × generic pairs) and tools/drawn-overlaps.ts
     (from what the page DRAWS: each landmark's plan rasterised from its mesh, then model × model and model × city;
     report in docs/review/san-francisco-drawn-overlaps.txt). A first version filled the bounding box of every wall
     seen edge-on, which turned every diagonal wall into a rectangle — 36 placeholders written from it were removed
     before anything used them. Owner's points A (Mutual Savings), E (Parc 55), I (Apple) are not explained by either
     list: still open (E, Parc 55: #287).
194. **"The Paramount" was the St. Regis's tower** (owner's report B, z-fighting at 37.786342, −122.401152): OSM's 148 m
     part way/436648187 lies wholly inside the St. Regis Museum Tower's part way/79377186 — they are one building, the
     St. Regis (148 m, 42 storeys). A 2026-10-02 entry modelled it as "the Paramount" from a Mapillary frame taken
     there, and its builder shared the registry key `paramount` with the real Paramount's recipe (680 Mission,
     way/160673823, fidi-3), so both towers were drawn as the pale one and the St. Regis had a second tower inside it.
     Renamed `stRegisTower`, entry renamed and pointed at its part; the Paramount keeps its terracotta Deco recipe.
     The drawn-overlap audit (docs/review/san-francisco-drawn-overlaps.txt) lists 108 model × model and 107 model ×
     city overlaps after the fixes above; the top of each list is the worklist.
195. **The Marriott Marquis, rebuilt as the jukebox** (owner: "the extremely unique hotel near YB Center needs a custom
     model, the kit model looks nothing like it … that one will take internet research"). Researched from two Commons
     photographs (Yerba Buena Gardens; Mission Street with St. Patrick's) and SF YIMBY / Wikidata: Zeidler with DMJM,
     1989, 133 m, "ziggurat step profile", "dramatic arched windows inspired by Art Deco". The tower's OSM part is a
     CROSS of diagonal arms round a core; the old builder drew a box ziggurat with dark slits over its bounding box
     (it also covered the 24 m wings: 88–95% in the drawn-overlap audit). Now (sf/marquis.ts): the cross itself, clipped
     to a square shrinking with height so every arm steps down; cream precast with punched windows; at every step a
     half-round funnel of blue glass on the arm's end over a blue strip — the jukebox lights; the core's top storeys in
     blue glass with a great half-round arch on each face.
196. **The Wave Organ, recognisable** (owner: "there, but not recognizable"): it was four rows of boxes. Now a stepped
     half-round amphitheatre of salvaged granite and marble opening to the bay, mouldings left on some blocks, column
     drums and a fallen fluted shaft lying about, steps down to the water, and 26 organ pipes in clusters through the
     stone, each with a flared mouth (sf/park-sights.ts).
197. **No trees inside models**: the park scatter planted Rincon Park's trees through Cupid's Span. Every placed model's
     footprint is now a tree clearing (render/landmarks.ts `onFootprint` → Vegetation.clear), except areas — plazas,
     parks, gardens, marinas — whose own trees stand in them. `clear()` now tests only its new ring, with a bounding-
     box pass, since hundreds of footprints arrive one at a time.
198. **The Sutro Baths and Lands End paths join** (owner's two gap reports): `closePathGaps` (this round, bake-time)
     had not closed them. At Sutro the footway's end also carried a flight of steps, which bikes do not ride — and
     `joinDeadEnds`, run first, rescued the dead end through those steps, so `closePathGaps` saw no dead end; the
     steps went later and the dead end came back, 5 m short of the path on. It now runs before the rescue as well as
     after. A second run exposed a swap: two dead ends facing each other each picked the other and both moved (the
     contraction then failed); a node joined TO now stays put. Checked in the ride graph: both points are now on
     through edges. (Lands with the decks bake, already run.)
199. **A podium raised to the height of the buildings on it** (owner, 37.798421, −122.399213: "intersections that can't
     possibly be right"): Golden Gateway Commons' podium (way/941869556, `building=yes`, no height) holds 14 m
     townhouse parts covering less than half of it, so the bake kept it — and raised it to the parts' 14 m, a rule
     meant for towers whose only mapped part is a floating crown. Identical blocks stood one inside the other. Now
     only floating parts raise an outline; grounded parts on an outline with no height of its own hold it UNDER them
     (0.45 of the lowest, at least 3.5 m) — a podium (tools/bake.ts `capTo`, logged with a count). Bake-time.
200. **Apple Union Square's glass front z-fought** (owner, 37.788715, −122.407202: "major z fighting"): its walls were
     drawn edge by edge as `faceBand(out, [a, b], …)` — and faceBand closes whatever ring it is given, so a two-point
     ring was the wall twice, back to back. Landmarks are drawn without back-face culling, so the pair fought across
     the whole storefront. faceBand now treats two points as one wall (render/landmarks/tower.ts); the same pattern is
     in three market-east.ts walls, fixed with it. Neither coplanar probe could see it: one compares a mesh with
     itself only where normals agree, the other between meshes.
201. **Golden Gateway Center's garden podium joins the aerial network**: its two garages' roofs (way/588365127 west,
     12 m; way/588363943 east, 13 m — the heights the townhouses already stood on) are area decks of the
     Embarcadero podium (src/city/sf/bridges.ts); the bridges over Washington Street now climb to them from Maritime
     Plaza's 8 m instead of hanging at the bake's guessed 5.7 m. Left at its edges: a pedestrian bridge off the east
     podium drops 13 m in its last 8 m to the street it meets (way 67549), and the bridge north over Jackson
     (68081) still at 5.7 m — the Commons beyond is the next podium to declare.
202. **The green public toilets** (owner: "the automated public pay toilets — iconic green. there are not many of
     them"): JCDecaux's self-cleaning kiosks, the 1995 Parisian design — dark green cast panels on a box with half-
     round ends, moulded ribs, a low dome with a finial, a brushed-steel door under a sign (sf/pitstop.ts). The 25
     sites are SF Public Works' Pit Stop list (sfpublicworks.org/pitstop, via search); OSM tags none of them. The 21
     inside the box are in public-toilets.json, each placed by tool: the corner's OSM node (the nearest node pair
     where the streets share none; Boeddeker Park's own outline for Eddy & Jones), then the first point 8–18 m out
     2.2–6.5 m clear of every carriageway and in no building, the door turned to the nearest road. Union Square's by
     hand onto the square (the list says "Union Square"). Twin Peaks (two), Mission & 24th and South Van Ness at
     Cesar Chavez are outside the box.
203. **Roads ahead on phones** (owner: "on mobile … roads don't render at all until you are on top of them"): the
     phone tiers build only the rider's own tile in full; the rings beyond had building massing and no streets, so
     the road ahead began at the tile's edge. The mid ring now draws each street as a plain asphalt ribbon at its
     width and graded heights (render/fringe.ts `midRoads`, a few hundred triangles a tile), from a slim copy of the
     streets kept beside the mid tile's (emptied) edges — or its full edges, for a tile the memory governor pulled
     in from the full ring. Seen at `?tier=ultra`: the street runs on up the hill past the tile.
204. **No keyboard needed** (owner: "no way to board transit on mobile"; "many of the messages say 'press X key'"):
     the HUD's transit line is now a button — a tap boards the cable car or streetcar it names, or gets you off (the
     same `transitAction` as B; when lost, the escape hatch) — and on a touch screen it says "Tap · board …" /
     "tap to get off". The pass has a button too: "🍽 Eat the …" while you are very late (the same condition as the
     hint, which on a touch screen says "tap 🍽 below"). Banners that named B say "tap" there (ui/touch.ts
     `touchScreen`/`keyed`, the controls' own `wantsTouch` rule). Not yet seen boarding in a probe: a stationary
     rider kept being placed on a street just off the cable car line.
205. **Civic Center's playgrounds were drawn twice** (owner: "looks good but has a lot of z fighting"): the plaza model
     furnished OSM's two playground rectangles with the kit, and since this round's bake the city's courts layer
     furnishes every OSM playground — the same ones. The model now keeps only the spiderweb net and the clearings.
206. **Pause, on P** (owner: "move freeze to pause and make it a P button … show a message that you can navigate with
     wasd (on keyboard) or show arrow buttons (on mobile)"): the look-around freeze is the pause now — P (F still works);
     its message names W A S D, Q/E, drag and wheel on a keyboard. On a touch screen a ⏸ button is always in the corner
     and, paused, an arrow pad (▲◀▶▼, ⤒⤓ for up and down) flies the camera — held buttons are held keys to the orbit
     look (ui/debug.ts `LookPad`). P had been the controls' bare pause (the rider held, no look) — that stays on the
     Pause key; photo mode's fog toggle moved from P to O. Checked in the page both ways: the message, and the camera
     moving under a held ▲.
207. **The inspector, for editing** (owner: "press i, select a building and see the address, lat/long, source of the
     building data, as much internal info as possible. this is just for me"): `?edit=1` makes the buildings pickable and
     puts I on the inspector (the player's landmark card keeps I otherwise). Armed, a click fills a box (ui/inspector.ts):
     for a building — the point's lat/lon and world position, Street View and OSM links, the mesh and tile, its OSM
     element(s) (an odd id may be a way or a relation), every baked field, every manifest entry that names it with the
     FILE it is in (entries now carry `_file`), and its OSM tags fetched live from the OSM API — the address among them;
     for a model — its manifest entry whole and its placement. Checked on 347–373 Geary: address, tenement restyle,
     union-square.json.
208. **The Hilton Union Square was one tower on a lawn** (owner: "a custom building that looks totally wrong",
     37.785399, −122.410764): a placeholder (2026-10-02, for z-fighting) named the site's outline way/700791868 — and a
     claim on an outline takes every part in it: Tower 1, Tower 3 (106 m) and the 9-storey wing were hidden, the
     modelled Tower 2 left alone on the block's lawn. The outline is out of the placeholder (the bake drops it for its
     parts anyway); the block is built again.
209. **Escape leaves the big map** (owner: "hard to get out of map mode because you need to press m, and focus is on the
     landmark input box"): the search field has the focus while the map is open, so M there is a letter. Escape now
     closes the map from anywhere, the field included (game.ts).
210. **St. Mary's Cathedral on its podium** (owner, with a Street View from Gough: "not terrible, but not great. the real
     building has a large concrete podium and is mostly concrete on top"): the four saddle shells sprang from a 3 m
     plinth at the full width, their gables all glass. Now a 13.5 m podium — a dark glass core behind travertine piers,
     glass bays at its corners, a deep overhanging roof slab — with the cupola set in to 0.6 of its width rising from
     the podium's roof to 58 m; each gable travertine, with a narrow dark slot of glass up its middle to the ridge
     (sf/st-marys.ts).
211. **The Hearts in San Francisco, all of OSM's** (owner: "union square itself has the heart sculpture, neat. we should
     add all the hearts"): two hand-typed placements of one flat red cut-out (one called "Ferry Building", by Maiden
     Lane). Now ten hearts at OSM's own artworks — Labyrinthine Heart, Butterflight and a third at Union Square, Simon
     Norris's My Heart, Patrick Dintino's glass Open Heart, three in the western neighbourhoods, Jim Dine's bronze Big
     Heart on the Rock and a giant metal heart — each a ROUNDED heart (the heart curve lofted front to back, so it is
     puffy) on a granite pedestal, painted by its own design and colours (stripes, dots, bands, rainbow, splotches, a
     labyrinth; bronze and steel plain) as stand-ins for the artists' (sf/art.ts `paintedHeart`). Not yet looked at
     in the renderer (probes held off while the owner's game was slow).
212. **The game got slow and washed out** (owner, mid-afternoon: "suddenly got much slower … fog/brightness changed …
     everything look washed out"; rAF handlers of 180 ms): two causes found. (1) #203's road ribbons were being built
     over the WHOLE mid ring on a desktop too — a hundred-odd more meshes, pale grey under the fog across the distance:
     now on the phone tiers only, where the full ring is the rider's own tile. (2) This session's own probes — headless
     copies of the game run back to back, each rendering the whole city on the same machine — beside an hour-long bake;
     the probes are held off now. A CPU profile of the current code (headless, desktop tier): the steady cost is the
     street grade's `nearest`, called per wheel per vehicle per frame (vehicle poses), and Babylon re-marking
     materials as tiles arrive. A baseline from the round's first commit could not be measured: that code no longer
     loads today's data. And `nearest` itself made cheaper (city/grade.ts): its 10 m cells keyed by number instead of a
     string built on every query (the garbage-collector's share of the profile), squared distances instead of
     `Math.hypot`, one result object per query instead of one per closer segment.
213. **The Westin St. Francis tower** (owner: "a unique old building with then a unique modernist building behind it.
     look at commons images"): the 1972 tower was a pale horizontally-banded preset. From Commons ("Westin St Francis,
     San Francisco 2023-07-13.jpg", "Union Square and the Westin seen from Macy's"): taupe precast in a close grid of
     punched windows over a dark granite base; a full-height slot of dark glass standing out of the middle of each long
     face — the outside glass lifts, two lit cabs in it; a crown of tan finned storeys under a projecting cap
     (districts/union-square.ts `westinTower`). The old hotel's model (three wings, two light courts, copper cornices)
     is unchanged — since #191 it no longer swallows the tower. Not yet looked at in the renderer.
214. **Flags, from OSM's 237 flagpoles** (owner: "SF is festooned with many flags … not just US but city, county,
     state, other countries"; "flags will be important when we add other cities"): tools/flags.ts reads every
     `man_made=flagpole` in the cut, sorts it into a design by its tags (143 US — an untagged pole flies the country's
     — 25 Pride, 23 California, 10 San Francisco, 6 Taiwan, 3 China, a dozen other countries, 13 team and trade
     banners) and stands it on the roof of the building it is inside (74 are), writing public/flags/san-francisco.json.
     render/flags.ts draws them: a pole with a gilt ball, the flag flying east on the prevailing west wind with a wave in
     the cloth, the designs drawn once on a canvas atlas — the Stars and Stripes, the California bear flag, San
     Francisco's phoenix with its motto, the rainbow, and the national flags — no image fetched. A tile layer (range 3).
     The old crude civic flags (render/quickmarks.ts) stay for buildings OSM gives no pole.
215. **Golden Gate Park's tropical planting** (owner: "golden gate park is a unique mix of tropical and north american
     plants"): the park was planted with cypress, pine, gum, oak and laurel only. A TREE FERN now (render/palms.ts
     `treefern`: a short shaggy trunk in the windmill palm's fibre, a wide umbrella of the queen palm's feather fronds
     held out flat and tinted bright; a new tree form, appended so no baked code moves), and the park's east and middle
     zones plant Canary palms, tree ferns, cabbage trees and queen palms among the natives; a Tree Fern Dell zone below
     the Conservatory (by eye — OSM does not map it) is mostly ferns. Lands with `--replant`.
216. **Rocky cliffs at Lands End and the Sutro Baths** (owner: "it is rocky cliffs, not grass. it's the ONLY area of rocky
     cliffs in the game"): a zone property `cliffs` (docs/MAP-FORMAT.md §7, map/format.ts), on the Lands End and Sutro
     Baths zones; render/cliffs.ts draws the steep ground there (over ~37°, a little ragged at the edge) as bare rock in
     weathered greys and browns, shaded by slope, lifted over the grass, with boulders scattered on the ledges and at the
     feet. The gentle ground stays grass.
217. **The box reaches Sutro Tower** (owner: "still no close-up view of sutro tower"; "a ride up to the tower … it's not
     quite all in the box"): the city's south edge was 37.7607, 470 m short of the tower (37.7552) — its near model
     (`sutroTowerNear`) waited for a tile that never came. South 4500 m (37.7526) in both the box and the detail box
     (tools/cities.ts); baked. The ride up still to check.
218. **WITHDRAWN — the de Young was already modelled.** I wrote a "new" de Young (copper fins, a separate twisting
     tower, two sights.json entries) believing the city drew it as boxes; src/render/landmarks/sf/de-young.ts already
     held the appearance pass's measured model (main block, the turned NE wing, planted courtyards on decks, the
     entrance canopy, the Hamon tower in the museum's frame), placed by parks.json — and my file had replaced it, so
     the museum was being drawn twice. Restored from HEAD; my two sights.json entries and the extra registry spread
     removed (my version kept in the session scratchpad only). Lesson: grep the manifests and `ls` the builder's
     file before writing a landmark.
219. **The replant hung on a reserve's boundary**: growing the box south (#217) brought in three `leisure=nature_reserve`
     relations tens of kilometres across — one runs 228 km down the coast — packed as "park". The park planting walked
     a 13 m grid over the whole bounding box: 45 million candidates, each a point-in-ring test on 6 663 points, so the
     replant never finished. Found by pausing the process under the inspector (`plantParks` → `inside`). A green area
     over 40 km across is now a boundary, not a park: the bake drops it (tools/bake.ts, logged), `--replant` drops it
     from an older package, and `plantParks` skips it (park-planting.ts `tooBigForAnArea`). Replanted: 127 051 trees,
     the tropical planting (#215) and the tree fern dell in; map-v3 kept the cliffs zones (#216).
220. **The Sutro Baths cliffs, second go** (#216 drew nothing you could see): measured in the page, only 2–4 % of the
     two cliffs zones is steeper than 37°, and most of that is the sea floor — the elevation model smooths the real
     cliffs to twenty or thirty degrees. render/cliffs.ts now: rock over ~20°; a ragged strip 11–25 m wide along the
     water's edge, measured against the WATER AS DRAWN (the tile's sea areas), not the terrain under sea level — the
     DEM's coast and OSM's disagree, and a terrain band came out as a pale line in the water with the lawn running to
     the real edge (and a height band rocked the whole floor of the Baths' basin); nothing under water. Colour a dark
     cool grey varying in patches metres across (a colour per 2 m cell was a beige chequerboard). Looked at: grey rock
     along the shore below the ruins with the surf on it, outcrops on the steep hillside; the hillside rock is
     stair-edged at its 2 m cells. Also looked at this pass: Sutro Tower's near model from its summit (#217), the
     Westin tower behind the old hotel (#213), City Hall's flags (#214), the Union
     Square heart (#211), Golden Gate Park's palms (#215).
221. **The Golden Fire Hydrant** (visitor joys): the hydrant at 20th & Church that kept running in 1906 and saved the
     Mission, repainted gold every April 18th. Our hydrants are procedural along the pavements (OSM's are not drawn), so
     it is a landmark at OSM's node 3989015890 (landmarks/sf/golden-hydrant.ts `goldenHydrant`): an old Greenberg
     pattern — flanged foot, stout barrel with a band, domed bonnet and operating nut, side outlets, the pumper outlet
     to the corner — gold all over, a bronze plaque in the pavement before it. Looked at from the corner.
222. **The Seward Street Slides** (visitor joys): the two concrete slides of Seward Mini Park (1973), ridden on cardboard.
     Not in OSM (the park, way/138662530, and the Acme Alley steps beside it are). landmarks/sf/seward-slides.ts
     `sewardSlides`: a 24 m trough of polished concrete split down the middle, kerbed walls, a pad and a rail at the top,
     a run-out and a rubber mat at the bottom, cardboard lying about — built a metre at a time and DRAPED, so it follows
     the hill from the Corwin end (24.6 m in game) down toward Seward Street (4 m; measured on the baked terrain).
     Looked at: beside the steps and the playground, the right way up after one turn round (front 42° → 222°).
223. **Union Square: grass blotched through the granite** (owner: "refine the appearance of union square"): from above
     the square was a scatter of irregular green patches on grey paving. OSM maps no grass there — it was the park's
     own ground: the terrain under the square falls 5.7 m from Powell & Post down to Stockton & Geary (measured on
     the baked terrain), and `unionSquarePlaza`'s granite deck was ONE level, 1.5 m over the lowest corner, so over
     the upper half the park's lawn came up through it. The entry is now DRAPED (union-square.json): the deck, its
     paving bands, lawn panels, flights and pavilions ride 1.5 m over the ground everywhere, as the real square's
     terraces follow the hill. Looked at from above: clean banded granite, the two lawn panels, the column, the steps.
224. **Golden Gateway Commons, modelled** (owner: the walkway condos "render, but just with generic buildings"; then
     the owner's photographs — aerial, the Jackson front from Sydney Walton Square, a corner, the block plan): the Commons
     (Fisher-Friedman, 1978–85, Jackson to Pacific) are maroon brick townhouses and flats on two-storey bases of shops
     round the parking, gardens on the podium. landmarks/sf/gg-commons.ts:
     - `ggCommonsHouse`, fitted to each of OSM's 14 m `building:part`s (20 entries, prominent.json): an arcade of dark
       round-arched openings to 6.4 m, square windows with dark sloped awnings, a top storey of half-round windows, a
       pale pink-buff roof deck with a dark glass greenhouse skylight along the longest side and two tall chimneys;
     - `ggCommonsPodium` on the two block outlines (way/941869555, way/941869556; inset 0.4 m so the houses' shared
       walls hide it): the brick arcade base, its roof the podium garden;
     - `ggCommonsGateway` on the Jackson front of the Front–Davis block (front 171°): the brick arch rising above the
       roofline over the way in, the flight and landing and the two flights off it (the city's OSM steps stand in it).
     First tried as a `brick` restyle (replaced). The two earlier `base` entries were misnamed "Commons": they are
     Golden Gateway CENTER's townhouses — renamed. Five of the houses are multipolygon parts the bake never fetched
     (#225): their entries wait for the bake. Looked at from Walton Square, from above and along Drumm.
225. **Every `building:part` mapped as a relation was missing** (found on the Commons): the main query asks for
     `relation["building"]` only, so a multipolygon part never reached any city. A separate query
     (overpass.ts `partRelationsQuery`, its own cache key so the main query is not re-fetched) brings them in, and
     the bake turns each outer ring into a part way (packed `rel.id * 2 + 1`), so coverage, `partOf` and dressing
     treat it exactly like a part way. Lands with the full bake running now.
226. **The South Basin at the ferry gates, part two** (owner: "pier near ferry building needs tons of work"; the
     screenshot's "white boxes"): (a) the AGRICULTURE BUILDING (1915, 101 The Embarcadero — OSM way/104599975,
     "Ferry Station Post Office Building", old name AG Building) was a generic grey office block over the water; from two
     Mapillary frames it is red brick framed in buff rusticated stone — quoins up every corner, rusticated window
     surrounds — over a concrete base, under a deep eave and a low hipped clay-tile roof (landmarks/sf/ag-building.ts).
     (b) `plazaDeck` (the South Basin plaza and Ferry Plaza decks, #190 and earlier) stood on a SOLID dark block that
     rose 3.5 m out of the water — a building on the bay, the "white box" — and its top was one flat pale colour. Now
     paving in two greys in broad bands, an edge beam, and a grid of piles down into the water (marina.ts). Still to
     do: the gates' own gangways and canopies (no usable photograph yet; OSM's two `building=roof` canopies,
     way/979811981 and way/979811987), and joining the basin deck to the promenade.
227. **The full bake landed** (#199 podium caps, #225 relation parts, #219 reserve boundaries, the tropical planting):
     69 building parts from multipolygon relations; 8 podium outlines held under the buildings on them; 3 reserve-sized
     green relations dropped. The Commons' five relation houses are in and the blocks are whole — and with them the two
     block outlines are now covered by their parts and superseded, so the Commons stand on the ground with their
     gardens between at grade (the `ggCommonsPodium` entries now reach only the leftover low parts). Stage 3 of the
     bake audit first FAILED: a second instance of #218's mistake — `ferryStationPostOffice` (a one-line recipe in
     batches/h2.ts, h2.json) already claimed the AG Building, and my new entry was shadowed by it. Fixed by pointing
     the h2 entry at `agricultureBuilding` and deleting the dead recipe key (DISABLED MODELS table); the audit re-run
     passes at its baseline (4), map-v3 rewritten, `npx vitest run` 1582 passed.
228. **SFMOMA's Snøhetta wing through the W hotel** (owner: "another intersecting buildings issue here 37.785513,
     -122.400479"; drawn-overlap audit: 1147 m², 52 % of the W): the OSM parts do not overlap — the museum's
     expansion is an L wrapping the W's corner lot — but a model fitted to its parts was given their CONVEX HULL as its
     ring, which takes the W's lot in, and the wing (raised on the rear of that ring) stood through the hotel. New
     opt-in `"plan": "union"` (render/landmarks.ts `partsOutline`: the grounded parts rasterised at 0.5 m, the outer
     boundary traced edge by edge, simplified to its corners; test: an L keeps its notch) hands the builder the true
     outline; set on SFMOMA. Opt-in so models already checked against photographs keep their hull. Looked at from
     straight above: the wing steps round the W and still reaches Howard beside it.
229. **Golden Gateway Commons after the re-bake** (owner: "it doesn't look perfect"): with the block outlines superseded
     by their parts (#225/#227) the brick base and the podium garden had gone — the houses stood on bare ground —
     and the roof decks read a bright peach, the greenhouse skylights pale sky-blue (a glass surface reflecting the
     sky). The base is now drawn at the two blocks' real outlines, copied from OSM into gg-commons.ts
     (`ggCommonsPodiumDrumm`, `ggCommonsPodiumFront`, placed by their centres so a bake cannot take them away again;
     0.4 m in so the houses' shared walls win); roofs a muted pink-buff; skylights matte dark grey. Looked at from above,
     from Walton Square and along Drumm: arcaded base, garden at podium level between the houses, gateway.
230. **Trainer hands-off: the stall valve never opened face to face with a car in the box** (owner: "it is very
     important that the user never has to touch the controls"): the hands-off soak (tools/trainer-soak.ts, 8 min from
     Union Square) stood 9.6 s against a car standing in the junction box, though the valve (game.ts `STALL_LIMIT`,
     3 s hands-off) should have let the rider through at 3. Its clock reset whenever the rider moved a metre in ANY
     direction — and a standing rider is nudged sideways by the navigator's sidestep looking for a way round. Now only
     progress along the heading (forward or back) resets it. Two 8-minute soaks after: Union Square — 1995 m, longest
     stand 2.5 s (the welcome card); Chinatown (the thickest traffic) — 2048 m, longest 4.0 s while tumbling after a
     crash, every traffic stand ≤ 2.5 s.
231. **Civic Center's flags each had a coloured square in front of them, both sides** (owner's screenshot): the plaza
     builder (civic-plaza.ts) still drew its own Avenue of Flags — a second white pole beside each of OSM's 26 poles
     with a plain red/blue/white/yellow/green square on it — and since #214 the flag layer flies a real flag on each
     of those same poles. The builder's flags are gone; the layer's stay. Looked at down the avenue: clean flags.
232. **Square blocks as trees at Civic Center** (owner: "we don't need to use square blocks as trees, we have real
     trees"): the clipped London planes were flat green boxes on sticks. Now the library's real trees, planted along
     each of the four rows (a deck strip a metre wide, `trees: true`, one every 7 m). Looked at.
233. **The 1200 Gough footbridge drawn as a raised skinny house** (owner, with a Street View frame: "in reality it is a
     plain concrete walk … it goes to the roof of the parking structure"): OSM way/256487839 is `building=bridge`,
     levels 2, min_level 1 — the bake made it a 4 m storey on stilts in a Victorian style. New `concreteFootbridge`
     (landmarks/sf/footbridges.ts): a pale concrete deck level with the garage roof it leads onto (4 m), a fascia, a
     thin steel picket railing along its two sides and the ends left open. Looked at from the drive and from above.
234. **A striped green-and-white quarter-circle across the street by the ice rink** (owner, twice): picked from above, it
     is `msThirdStreetEsplanade` (sf/ms-edges.ts) — its `arcStair` with the `esplanade` preset: 8 m of rise in 0.2 m
     risers is 40 treads, at the preset's 1.2 m each a 48 m flight, anchored at the wall top and climbing OUT — its
     alternating green and pale treads laid over the roadway to the far pavement. The flight is taken out (DISABLED
     MODELS); the canopy, beds and rail along the arc stay. Looked at from above: the street is clear.
235. **The Ghirardelli sign faced away from the bay and floated** (owner): the entry stood 14 m north of the clock-tower
     block it belongs on (z 1429 against the block's north edge at 1415, measured from the package) — so its lattice hung
     in the air past the roof — and its `front: 0` (a point entry turns by front + 180) put the letters' faces inland:
     from the bay they read mirrored. Moved onto the block's north edge, `front: 180`, and its foot set absolutely at
     the block's roof (`baseY` −46.4: the block's lowest ground −67.86, less the 0.5 sink, plus its 22 m) instead of a
     24 m lift off the slope. Looked at from the Aquatic Park lawn and from the west.
236. **Sutro Tower's base: an apartment block, and OSM's tower parts hanging in the air** (owner, with a photograph: "a huge
     concrete structure … an equipment bunker and maybe broadcast offices … i want to add a fictional 'ride to the top'
     later"; "osm has some simple building parts representing the sutro tower that appear to hang in the air"):
     - the "apartment building" round the tower's foot was OSM's way/581120610, tagged `building:part=no` — the bake's
       truthy test took "no" for a part. Fixed in the bake (`isPart`), and placeholdered now;
     - the five crossbar-level relations (#225 brought relation parts in) hung through `sutroTowerNear`: placeholdered,
       with the tower relation itself;
     - the bunker: 17 OSM concrete parts, each now `sutroBunker` (raw grey concrete, a dark window slot, plain coping)
       or `sutroBunkerPanels` (the white ribbed fibreglass band from 13 to 15 m) — landmarks/sf/sutro-base.ts — and the
       four small Victorian-styled service sheds on the compound are concrete blocks. Looked at from above the compound.
       Ready for a "ride to the top" later: the tower's legs stand clear of the bunker roofs.
237. **Far too many parking meters** (owner: "WAY too many … most of the city has no parking meters"): every main street
     (primary to tertiary) outside the brick-paved names had a row down 65 % of its kerbs, city-wide. Now only a
     COMMERCIAL street gets them: the city files each tile's shop and food POIs (`shopsIn`), and a street takes meters
     only with at least three within 20 m of it and one per 40 m of its length (render/city.ts). The outer avenues,
     the park edges and the residential hills lose them; downtown and the neighbourhood shopping streets keep them.
238. **The Japanese Tea Garden** (owner: "really bad looking. i think maybe the custom model isn't getting used"): the
     models WERE used — they were placeholders: the pagoda a two-tier red box, the South Gate a red box under a green
     pyramid, the gift shop, Temple Gate and an outbuilding stucco cottages with orange roofs, the garden's wooden
     bridge a white plank 5.5 m in the air. New landmarks/sf/tea-garden.ts (the old `spec()` placeholders removed from
     districts/parks.ts): the FIVE-storey square pagoda (vermilion posts, plaster panels, five flared dark roofs with
     kicked-up corners, a stone podium, a gilt nine-ring finial); the South Gate in natural timber under two stacked
     roofs; the red Temple Gate; the open tea house on its deck; the gift shop and outbuilding in timber and shoji;
     and `teaGardenGrounds` (standalone, draped): the steep half-moon drum bridge over a pool, the plank bridge, ten
     stone lanterns, the seated bronze Buddha on its plinth, the bamboo fence on OSM's garden ring.
     And a city bug under it: a model's `coversRaised` claim was IGNORED wherever declared bridge decks exist
     (`outsideCovers` asked only `modelDeck`), so the garden's two bridges were drawn twice. A cover area whose middle
     is on no declared deck now counts by its own outline (render/city.ts). Looked at from three sides.
239. **Muni trains stuck 100% of the time turning at 9th Ave & Irving** (owner's screenshot): two causes, both fixed in
     render/life.ts. (1) OSM maps the N line as many ways; each became its own rail with its own train shuttling between
     the rail's ends — and a one-way street track's END in the rider's sight holds its train there ("stands at the end"),
     so every train reaching the piece's end in the junction stood there for good. Rail pieces that meet end to end
     (exactly one other rail's end, same stock, at grade) are now JOINED as they arrive (`joinRail`), the trains already
     on the line keeping their place; the line runs through the turn. (2) A street tram looked for obstacles along a
     straight line from its head car — in a 90° turn that line crosses the cross street, so the cars queued there (and
     the other train round the corner) held it. It now samples its own rails ahead every metre (on its lane line).
     Watched for 90 s: a train runs down 9th Ave, through the turn at 8 m/s and east on Irving; it queues behind cars
     at the lights and moves on.
240. **The Seward Street Slides, second pass** (owner, with a photograph: "one of SF's weirdest landmarks. note they are
     curved and have colorful metal. and the park is heavily wooded"): the chutes now SNAKE down the hill in an S-curve
     (±1.6 m), built in half-metre lengths and draped; grey concrete walls with rounded copings; hoops of painted steel
     tube — red, yellow, and one half-and-half — over the top and the foot; red and yellow bollards along the concrete
     path beside them; a sand pit with green stepping-stump seats at the foot, cardboard lying about. The woods: a
     planting zone on the park's OSM ring (`seward-mini-park`, dense evergreens, laurels, bottlebrush, pohutukawa), the
     slides and path a clearing — lands with the next replant.
241. **A "supertall" in Dogpatch: UCSF Psychiatry at 210 m** (owner, 37.762945, −122.388721: "there are 4 very unique
     apartment buildings on this corner, but they're just 5-6 stories"): the h1 batch's recipe for the UCSF Nancy Friend
     Pritzker Psychiatry Building carried `height: 210` (the only three-digit placeholder left in the batches). Now its
     own model (landmarks/sf/mission-bay.ts `ucsfPritzker`, from a Mapillary frame on Third): five storeys, 23 m, a pale
     stone base, pale grey standing-seam panels, a band of dark vertical fins, the white-framed corner box with its
     glazed bay, terracotta side panel and sunshades, a recessed glass top storey, rooftop plant frames. The other three
     buildings on the corner and the rest of Mission Bay are the next pass.
242. **The Third Street & 20th corner, two more models, and their boxes off the wall** (owner: "4 very unique apartment
     buildings on this corner"; then "the new mission bay building … has cubes floating in the air … it's a cool design,
     it just shouldn't be floating"): from Mapillary frames, `dogpatchSawtooth` (way/1256126073: taupe stucco, lime-green
     boxed bays with brown cheeks, red awnings over a white shopfront floor, a white SAWTOOTH of gabled roof monitors) and
     `dogpatchBronzeBoxes` (way/1256126075: bronze-framed glass grid, staggered bronze box balconies, green tile at the
     foot), new public/landmarks/san-francisco/mission-bay.json. The boxes first hung in the air: they were placed at the
     footprint's BOUNDING box front, and this footprint is an L, its wall well behind the box where it steps back. Every
     Mission Bay builder now places street-face features on the real street wall (mission-bay.ts `frontWall`: the edge
     facing the street, `boxAlong` on it), and nothing above the roof. Looked at from above and from the courtyard side.
243. **A roof floating in the air by Chase Center** (owner; audits to include aerial views): found by circling the arena
     from 120 m and then from 40 m — a thin dark slab in the sky west of it, picked as `uberHqWing` (Uber HQ, 700 3rd St,
     sf/scratch-3.ts). Its walls stop at the orange cornice (0.85 H) and its roof cap was drawn at H, 6.6 m above them.
     The corner wing (`uberHqCorner`) has a setback top storey above its cornice; the wing now has the same (1.5 m in,
     curtain glass to H, a cap over the cornice ledge). Looked at again from the same 40 m view: both wings alike.
244. **Mission Bay, the UCSF campus core** (owner: Mission Bay "full of unique prominent buildings"; docs/MISSION-BAY.md):
     four batch recipes replaced with models from Mapillary frames (landmarks/sf/mission-bay.ts; the recipe keys deleted
     from batches/b9.ts and b3.ts, their b9/b3.json entries pointed at the new builders and named):
     `genentechHall` (600 16th: pinkish stone panel grid, deep-set teal windows, a stone attic, a dark plinth; 30 m, not
     OSM's 47 for five lab storeys), `byersHall` (1700 4th: blue-green glass ribbons between white stone spandrels under
     a deep overhanging flat lid; 30 m), `gladstoneInstitutes` (1650 Owens: bronze-tinted glass front, projecting cornice
     ledge, set-back plant floor), `dropboxOwens` (1800 Owens: teal glass grid with white floor bands and a darker bronze
     set-back top). Also: the nonbuildings audit's lists follow the Tea Garden's new builders, and its expected defects
     lose the Ghirardelli sign × mermaid fountain duplicate (#235 moved the sign) and gain the Tea Garden grounds round
     its own buildings (by design). `npx vitest run`: all pass.
245. **SPARK Social SF and Stagecoach Greens** (owner, with an aerial: two Mission Bay park blocks "have many permanent
     buildings that are not in OSM … SPARK Social and there's a mini golf course"). OSM does map them — as two dozen
     unnamed 4–8 m `building`s on the lots, drawn as brick huts and octagonal brick towers. Each is now what it is
     (mission-bay.ts, mission-bay.json): the two 12-sided ones are `sparkTent` (round tan tent pavilions on low walls),
     the octagons `sparkUmbrella` (a big tan umbrella over picnic tables), the little rectangles `sparkTruck` (food trucks
     and trailers in five liveries, serving hatch and awning). `sparkSocial` (standalone, draped) adds the picnic tables
     on the lawns and the orange shade sails, inside the two lots — 4th Street between them left clear (a first try put
     trucks and sails in it). `stagecoachGreens` on the course: eleven turf holes with kerbs, flags and gold-rush
     props, a blue water feature, small pink, teal and tan sails. Looked at from 4th Street and from the south.
246. **A map jump forfeits the delivery in hand** (owner: "otherwise you could just jump to complete the delivery"):
     `Delivery.forfeit()` (game/delivery.ts) ends a job on offer or under way, unpaid, with its own event; `__jump` (the
     map's go-to) calls it, clears the route and the lit building, and the banner says "Jumped — delivery forfeit".
     Test: forfeiting mid-job, then arriving at the old drop-off, earns nothing.
247. **Backward churches, city-wide** (owner: "mt holy redeemer … rendered backward (with the front facing the center of
     the block away from the street)"; "we should check for other backward churches"). Most Holy Redeemer (way/223211125,
     churches-4) is a `church()` kit model; its tower and front stood at the inland end, Diamond Street behind it. An
     audit in the page — every one of the 29 churches in churches-1/3/4/5: jump there, take the tallest vertex of its
     mesh (the tower), and see which side of the footprint it is on against the church's front — showed the kit
     turned EVERY church given a bearing 180° off it: the `"front": "street"` ones faced into their blocks, and Saints
     Peter and Paul (front 180, onto Washington Square) faced Union Street. The 09-22 "correction" that added the half
     turn (render/landmarks/church.ts `church()`) was judged against Old Saint Mary's, since a custom builder; the half
     turn is gone. The 15 kit churches with no bearing at all (no front, no named frontage — the kit's long-axis guess)
     now carry a numeric `front` toward their nearest street. After: 26 of 29 face their street, four with central
     towers read "middle"; three (Sinai Memorial Chapel, the Seventh-day Adventist Japanese Church, Soto Zen Mission
     Sokoji) still put their tower away from the nearest street — the nearest street may not be the entrance; they
     need a photograph each, not a guess.
248. **24th St Mission BART: open plazas, not downtown kiosks** (owner, with an aerial: "huge holes with stairs going
     down, exposed to the air"): new landmarks/sf/bart-plazas.ts — `bartPlazaRound` (the south-west corner: a round
     red-brick plaza, the circular well inside a low parapet with a rail, planting round it, the stair and escalators
     going down across it, the station-sign pylon) and `bartPlazaLong` (the north-east corner's oblong sunken stair).
     Draped (a flat model sunk half a metre was buried the first time), placed from OSM's subway_entrance nodes and the
     aerial. They declare `coversRaised`, and the city now skips OSM subway-entrance points inside a model's cover
     footprint and rebuilds a built tile holding one (render/city.ts `inCover`), so the kiosks are gone there. Looked at
     from above against the aerial. Still to do: the same two plazas at 16th St Mission.
249. **16th St Mission BART's open plazas too** (the rest of #248): `bartPlazaLong` on both corners, from OSM's three
     entrance nodes; the south-west one moved onto the corner where its stair runs. And OSM's stair ways down into the
     station (`highway=steps`, no tunnel or layer tag) were drawn as white flights RISING out of the plazas: the stairs
     layer now leaves out a flight a hand-built model's cover footprint contains (render/stairs.ts `drawnBy`, the city's
     `inCover`), and the cover marks tiles holding such a flight for a rebuild. Looked at from above: clean.
250. **The Women's Building** (owner: "could use a redo. Fascinating landmark with a huge colorful mural covering it"): it
     was the b5 batch's blind guess — a plain three-storey brick shopfront block. Now landmarks/sf/womens-building.ts
     `womensBuilding` (the b5 recipe key deleted, the entry pointed at it): the 1910 hall's three tall storeys of arched
     windows under a cornice, and MaestraPeace over every long wall in layers 4 cm apart — sky bands in blue and violet,
     rolling hills of orange, red, yellow and turquoise, tall robed figures with round heads and dark hair, and a giant
     face high on the long front — deterministic, the windows breaking through it. Mapillary's nearest frames show the
     neighbours, so it is drawn from what the building is known for; a photograph of the real mural would sharpen it.
     Looked at from 18th Street.
251. **Embarcadero Center: walls under the walkways, and the decks navigable end to end** (the goal: "the embarcadero
     decks are not navigable, and walls block the way under them"). Walked at street level under the whole promenade
     and up every cross street (Battery, Front, Davis, Drumm, Clay): two walls across the walk beneath its east end —
     (a) the promenade's deck path ran on to x 1420, so the way down to Drumm (78662) stayed at deck height until its
     last 7 m and fell 8 m there; the deck now ends at 1397, where the promenade does, and that way grades down its
     26 m as a ramp (looked at from Drumm); (b) way 78924, a 4 m `pedestrian` bridge climbing 8 m (a lift or stair
     core), was drawn as a slab across the ground route and ROUTED — the green line went straight up. A raised way
     steeper than a wall (stairs excepted: the plaza's spiral stairs are the way up) is now neither drawn as a deck
     (render/city.ts `steeperThanWall`) nor ridden (city/ridegraph.ts `classify`); 36 such ways city-wide, 24 of them
     stairs. Reachability measured on the ride graphs from across the city: every Embarcadero podium deck edge (36 by
     bike, 42 on foot) is connected. Golden Gateway Center's 12–13 m podium was reachable on foot only: the podium's
     bike-trails zone now covers it (`embarcadero-podium`), and `--rides` re-applies zone trails, so a zone edit no
     longer needs a full bake (70 more paths opened). `air-paths` within 500 m: bike none in the air; run one, a
     switchback stair from Front Street up to the skybridge, drawn (looked at) 3 m off OSM's line.
252. **The Marriott Marquis, second pass** (the goal: "rendering for the hotel around YB Center is still pretty bad"):
     #195's model was right in mass — the stepped cross-plan ziggurat — and wrong in skin: a grey PUNCHED grid read as an
     office block, and blue glass funnels hung on every step's arm ends looked like cones stuck to the walls. Now the
     faces are what the hotel is known for, continuous ribbons of mirrored blue glass between bands of pale stone, floor
     after floor; the funnels are gone; the jukebox crown keeps its glass half-round fans and gains their radiating ribs.
     Looked at from Mission Street and from across Yerba Buena Gardens.
253. **Union Square's z-fighting, found by measurement and fixed at the source** (the goal: "around Union Square, several
     of our custom buildings … have severe Z fighting"). A new probe (scratch `zfight.mjs x z r`) takes every triangle of
     every landmark within a radius in the live page, buckets them by plane, clips each overlapping pair exactly, and
     reports the area of faces within 1.2 cm of each other. Within 250 m of the square it found ~7 000 m², almost all
     inside single models. Causes and fixes:
     - landmarks are drawn double-sided, and `box()` draws a floor: every pavilion, step, plinth and seat standing on a
       deck had its floor in the deck's plane; abutting boxes' touching faces coincide; a facade band drawn twice. A pass
       on every built landmark (render/landmarks/coplanar.ts `dropCoincidentFaces`, tests/coplanar.test.ts) drops a
       triangle lying wholly within larger coplanar ones (within 1.5 cm), and any floor lying on a top; it never punches
       a hole (a face only partly covered stays). ~1.5–1.8 s of work spread over a few hundred models as downtown streams
       in, the worst a single 52 ms (Salesforce Transit Center);
     - two models fitted to buildings that share a party wall drew it twice in one plane (Saks × Tiffany, 900 m²): every
       footprint-fitted model now stands 5 cm inside its outline, mitred along each corner's bisector (an offset folded
       short edges back on themselves); `"inset": 0` opts out (the ICA, whose builder depends on the exact ring);
     - a DRAPED slab is a plane only at its corners, so layers draped at their own vertices cut through it (Union
       Square's deck, 1 100 m²): a draped model is subdivided to 4 m triangles first (`subdivideLong`);
     - Macy's 1928 store mass overlapped by the taller I. Magnin corner and the Geary front, and the Westin's rear block
       overlapped by its wings: the lower masses drawn 25 cm in (union-square.ts `shrinkRing`).
     After: within 250 m of the square, the largest is 315 m² (an internal wall inside Macy's, never seen), then ≤ 106;
     across the Financial District ≤ 128. The square looked at from above Geary & Powell: no shimmer in the paving.
254. **Embarcadero condos on the podium** (the goal: "buildings that are supposed to be ON TOP of buildings are appearing
     INSIDE them"): looked at again from the air after #199's podium caps and #225's parts: Golden Gateway Center's
     townhouses stand on top of their 12–13 m garage podium, the Punch Line on One Maritime Plaza's (#187), the
     Commons on their brick base (#229). None inside.
255. **Trainer hands-off, re-checked after the ride-graph changes** (#251): an 8-minute soak from the Ferry Building with
     no input at all — 1 729 m ridden, the longest stand 2.8 s (the welcome card), the longest traffic stand 2.3 s.
256. **CPMC Van Ness Campus** (owner: "rendering as a two-story partially underground building. reality is it's a huge
     metal structure with a tower"): OSM's block outline carries 12 m. From SmithGroup's and the opening coverage (12
     storeys, 2019; granite and rain-screen metal podium; the tall tower moved to the Geary side after community review)
     and a Mapillary frame on Van Ness: new landmarks/sf/hospitals.ts `cpmcVanNess` — a dark granite base, a 22 m podium
     of pale metal panels and window ribbons over the whole block with dark louvre strips, the 64 m tower over the Geary
     third with glass corner bays and a finned mechanical crown (new hospitals.json, included from the root manifest).
     Placed facing Geary (front 171); the tower first stood on the Post side — this frame's −z is the bearing's end.
257. **Footbridges that stop in the air at both ends** (owner: "footbridge at 37.753044, -122.455970 does not connect to
     the ground on either end"): the bridge over Clarendon Avenue is ONE OSM way with its spiral ramps at both ends, and
     `bridgeHeights` gives a bridge one level — the ramps included stood at 79.1 m, 3–5 m over the paths they meet. New
     bake pass (tools/bridges.ts `rampFootBridgeEnds`, in the full bake and in `--decks`): a foot bridge's end more than a
     metre over the highest non-bridge way meeting it is graded down to it along the bridge's own line, smoothly, over
     drop/0.1 m (at most 45 % of its length), so the middle keeps its clearance over the road; a foot bridge alongside a
     road bridge is its sidewalk and stays level with the road (the first run dipped the Fillmore-over-Geary sidewalks 4 m
     and put them back). Looked at: the Clarendon spiral now winds down to the path on each side.
     FOR THE OWNER — needs a reference: at 37.752148, −122.442793 OSM maps a helix of foot bridges tagged layer 1 to 5
     (ways 4571, 4600, 4900–4905, 4909); the bake stacks them to 31 m (64 → 95.5 m) — what is really there?
258. **Mission Bay: three more UCSF buildings**, from Mapillary frames (mission-bay.ts): `smithCardiovascular` (the Smith
     Cardiovascular Research Building, Viñoly 2014: tan terracotta-plank bands and dark louvre ribbons under a deep flat
     overhanging roof with louvres beneath), `helenDiller` (the Helen Diller Family Cancer Research Building: pale metal
     and glass ribbons over a recessed glass ground floor behind round concrete columns, a louvre band over them),
     `missionHall` (Mission Hall: a pale grid of narrow storey-high windows, a dark set-back top floor, the red-framed
     glazed recess at the corner). Shared helper `lid` (a thin overhanging roof slab with optional louvres).
259. **Mission Bay: the Rutter Center and the "zany" garage**, from descriptions (no usable Mapillary frame; SF Chronicle's
     2019 "10 buildings" piece and UCSF's 2005 coverage): `rutterCenter` (Legorreta's red-orange stucco cube, few deep-set
     windows, the purple-walled courtyard on the front, the steep orange tower at a corner) and `garage450South` (the 450
     South Street garage by Chase Center — the owner's "zany parking structure": off-white chiselled stucco with deep
     parallelogram voids leaning by turns, a grey concrete stair tower). FOR THE OWNER — references would sharpen both:
     which face the Rutter courtyard opens to, and the garage's real void pattern.
260. **Red bus lanes** (owner: "ideally we would also render bus lanes as red"): San Francisco's OSM carries them as
     `lanes:bus`, `lanes:psv` (with :forward/:backward), `bus:lanes`/`psv:lanes` (`designated` in the list) and `busway:*`.
     The bake now records which curbs have one (tools/bake.ts `busSide` → PackedEdge `busSide`: 1 right of the way's
     direction, 2 left, 3 both; test tests/bus-lanes.test.ts), carried through v3 as two new street flag bits (busRight
     2048, busLeft 4096; docs/MAP-FORMAT.md §5.5), and the renderer paints a curb lane up to 3.3 m wide in SF's brick
     red under the lane lines (render/city.ts). After a full bake: Geary Boulevard 377 ways, Mission 138, Sacramento,
     16th, Clay, Post, Sutter, Geary St, O'Farrell, 3rd, Potrero, Pine, 4th, Stockton, Otis. Looked at on Geary. (The
     bake also graded 130 foot-bridge ends, #257.)
261. **Observe from the top** (owner: "a toast that allows you to press o to Observe from the top of major high up
     landmarks, like Sutro, Salesforce Tower, Coit Tower, de Young Tower"): within 150 m of Sutro Tower, Salesforce
     Tower, Coit Tower, the de Young's Hamon Tower or the Transamerica Pyramid a pill says "🔭 Press O to observe from the
     top of …" (on a phone it is the button); O lifts the paused look camera to 2 m over the placed MODEL's own top
     (heights guessed from the buildings put the eye inside Coit Tower's shaft) to look round — drag, WASD — and O or P
     brings it back down (game.ts `OBSERVE`, `goUp`, `comeDown`; hud.ts `observe`). The photo mode's fog toggle moved
     from O to K. Looked at: from Coit Tower over North Beach to the Transamerica Pyramid.
262. **People playing on the courts** (owner's idea: "avatars in our athletic set that are going around on fixed paths, I
     don't think we need a ball"): new sim/players.ts `CourtPlayers` — for each of the 808 baked courts within 140 m of
     the rider (render/city.ts `courtsNear`), a deterministic set of players in athletic kit as crowd walkers (kind 'run',
     the same bodies and gaits): tennis and pickleball singles or doubles shuffling along their baselines, three to six
     round a basketball key, volleyball sides, four square children bouncing in their squares, children running loops
     in a playground; a quarter of courts empty. They wander Lissajous paths about their spots, faced the way they move,
     their gait paced by their speed. Added to the walkers the renderer and the avatar bodies get each frame (game.ts).
     Looked at: a half-court by Dolores Street with five players round the key.
263. **Waterfront audit, 1: Pier 7** (the owner's "audit the entire waterfront"; aerial views every few hundred metres from
     Aquatic Park to Mission Bay, each looking back at the shore from over the bay). Pier 7, the public fishing pier north
     of the Ferry Building, was a batch model's axis-aligned 220 × 167 m slab with its OSM walkways floating beside it a
     metre UNDER the water (baked to the sea bed). Now `pier7Promenade` (batches/s1.ts; the old recipe deleted, DISABLED
     MODELS): the real 260 m diagonal wooden deck on OSM's outline (copied: the pier is no building in the bake), piles,
