     a black iron railing, lamp standards; and a declared deck on the same ring at the model's deck top (city/sf/bridges.ts
     `PIER7_DECKS`), so `--decks` lifted its five walkways onto it and graded the walk from the Embarcadero up; the entry
     `coversRaised`, so the city does not draw its own raised walk over the boards. Air-paths within 300 m: none.
264. **Waterfront audit, 2: walks under the water, city-wide; Pier 54's shed.** The same fault as Pier 7, everywhere: a pier
     is mapped as an area with its walks drawn on it as footways, and the footways took the ground — over the bay, the
     flattened sea bed — 273 ways baked under the water (Pier 14's whole promenade, the Hyde Street and Wharf piers,
     Sausalito's docks). New bake pass (tools/bridges.ts `liftWalksOffTheSea`, after the bridge heights): a foot way's
     points inside a fixed `man_made=pier` outline go to the deck, 3.5 m over the sea; inside a floating one, to a float
     0.5 m over it; any other foot-way point below the sea, to a float. Streets are never moved; declared decks (Pier 7)
     still win. Lands with the full bake running now. And Pier 54's building (way/288651155), a brick block, is a cream
     corrugated-metal shed with roll-up doors and a red man door (mission-bay.ts `pierShed`, from the Commons photograph
     "Building on Pier 54 (2), October 2020"). Seen fine this pass: Aquatic Park, the Wharf, Pier 39, the Ferry Building,
     Rincon Park, Pier 30-32, Oracle Park.
265. **Market Street audit, 1: the Castro Theatre.** At the top of Market the theatre was a batch "cinema" recipe, a plain
     box. Now `castroTheatre` (sf/castro-theatre.ts): a cream Spanish-Baroque frontispiece stood forward of the wall on
     Castro Street, its stepped and scrolled gable over the roofline, the tall arched window, ornament bands, the red
     marquee over the doors, and the vertical CASTRO blade sign lit in neon. The old recipe is deleted (DISABLED MODELS).
     Gotcha: with a NUMERIC front here `edgeFacing(ring, 0)` (as mission-bay.ts's `frontWall` uses, with `front: "street"`)
     landed the features on the back (east) wall; `edgeFacing(ring)` puts them on Castro Street — check by eye each time. Looked at from over Castro Street. Air-paths: n/a (no deck).
266. **Golden Gate Park audit, 1: Kezar Stadium.** (Aerials over the park's landmarks: de Young, Cal Academy, the
     Conservatory, the Tea Garden, Stow Lake, the windmills, the Beach Chalet, Kezar.) Kezar's two stands are
     `building=grandstand` in OSM and were drawn as two 139 m barracks with windows and red tile roofs facing a lawn,
     with the park's trees and undergrowth across the running track (OSM's `leisure=track` is fetched, never baked). Now
     sf/kezar.ts, manifest public/landmarks/san-francisco/golden-gate-park.json (new include): `kezarNorthStand` and
     `kezarSouthStand` — open concrete stands of 18 rows rising from a railed front wall, sectioned orange seats (the old
     Candlestick seats fitted 2014–16, Wikipedia), a back parapet and rail — and `kezarField`: the red 8-lane 400 m
     track with its lane lines, the grass infield, the soccer pitch's lines and goals; its oval goes to the city as
     paved GROUND, so the trees are cleared from it. And the undergrowth now respects those clearings too: `Plants`
     (render/plants.ts) gains `clearings`/`clear(ring)`, fed by game.ts from the same landmark grounds and footprints
     the trees use (a raised deck's own planting — Salesforce Park — is exempt and checked), and `pitch` joins the
     areas nothing is planted on. Looked at from the air and from the infield.
267. **Golden Gate Park audit, 2: Strawberry Hill — and every summit in the city.** The island in Stow Lake was a
     flat-topped mesa 14 m over the water, its trails ringed by cream stone retaining walls. Two causes:
     - *The ground.* The bake's 60 m bare-earth OPENING shaves any summit narrower than ~120 m, and it does so
       everywhere: Twin Peaks, Mount Davidson, Corona Heights, Telegraph Hill and the rest are ~30 m too low, while the
       zoom-15 data here is already bare earth (measured over 409 buildings of 40 m+). Written up for the owner as
       BUGS.md #260 with the full table: the city-wide fix moves streets and descents and is theirs to call. Strawberry
       Hill itself is fixed with a `relief: raw` zone on the island's shore (data/zones, `strawberry-hill`; nothing
       ridden crosses it): its top is 129.5 m against the data's 130.4 (was 101.7). The raw path now takes the raw
       ground blurred as the opened ground is (a zero-radius `bareEarthF`), not the tile's posts — 8 398 samples raw in
       the two zones. Full bake 4; air-paths within 400 m: none.
     - *The walls.* render/city.ts's retaining walls (BUGS.md #38) took every way, so a trail benched across a slope
       stood in dressed stone with a parapet. A ground FOOT way is drawn draped on the terrain (the corridor layer), so a
       wall from its levelled height was a ridge beside a path lying on the ground; an earth bank, tried first, read as
       dark slabs on the Lands End lawn and green stripes on Strawberry Hill's duff. Foot ways now get no wall at all;
       stairs (drawn at their own heights) and streets keep theirs.
268. **Golden Gate Park audit, 3: the California Academy of Sciences**, rebuilt (sf/cal-academy.ts; the parks.ts entry
     now points at it, `front: 318` so the entrance faces the de Young): the glass box under its 35 ft living roof of
     seven hills — the rainforest's mound to the south-west, the planetarium's to the north-east, studded with porthole
     skylights; five low hills round them — the piazza's glass roof between the domes, and the photovoltaic canopy
     round the edge on slender columns (was a flat lime slab with two little domes). Sources: Wikipedia; Dwell.
269. **Universities audit, 1: Lone Mountain (USF).** The old San Francisco College for Women (Henry A. Minton, 1932) on
     its hilltop was a generic 15 m block. Now `loneMountainMain` (sf/lone-mountain.ts, new manifest
     san-francisco/campuses.json): cream stucco wings under red tile hips, and the Spanish Gothic tower on the south
     front — buttressed corners rising into pinnacle clusters, the double lancet, a pinnacled crown, the low tiled
     pyramid with copper ridges and the cross, ~44 m (Commons photographs "…Lone Mountain Campus-Main-10" and the 1932
     bookplate). And at the head of the stairway from Turk Boulevard, its Baroque GATEWAY (`loneMountainGate`, a
     lat/lon entry on the path OSM runs through it): paired pilasters, the round arch, a stepped crest with its
     cartouche and finial, balustrades with urn posts either side (Commons, "Lone Mountain entrance.jpg"). The
     stairway's own terraces are the city's stairs; their balustrades and urns would be the next step.
270. **Hospitals audit, 1: San Francisco General.** The campus was beige generic blocks round a beige drum. Now
     (sf/hospitals.ts, hospitals.json): **Zuckerberg SF General, Building 25** (2016) — `zuckerbergSFGH` on the union of
     its two OSM parts (385073691, 1212166666): the round glass drum stepped back off a glass podium, white fins every
     couple of metres, a deep white sunshade at every floor, the open louvred crown, the brick-red band along the
     podium's parapet (Commons, "San Francisco General Hospital 2016"). And **the 1915 brick wards** — Buildings
     10/20/30/40, the southern block, Building 1 and Buildings 80/90 (`sfghWard10…50`, `sfghBuilding1`,
     `sfghBuilding80`, one builder `sfghBrick`): red brick on a stone base, a stone string course, the top storey an
     arcade of round-headed openings under a deep brick cornice, low red tile hips, four to five storeys where OSM
     has 12 m parts. Looked at from the air and from Potrero Avenue (whose red transit lane is right).
     And, seen on the way, a storefront on Potrero read "PIIARMACY": the facade atlas's shop words were set in Didot,
     whose hairline crossbars vanish at the atlas's resolution. tools/facadegen/draw.py now tries Georgia Bold first;
     atlas repainted (`python3 tools/facadegen/paint.py`); PHARMACY reads in the game. (Gotcha on the way: Pillow's
     `getbbox()` on an RGBA difference looks at ALPHA only, and the words are painted at alpha 0 — compare as RGB.)
271. **Presidio audit, 1: the Letterman Digital Arts Center** (aerials over the Main Post, Tunnel Tops, Letterman, Crissy
     Field, Fort Point, the chapel). Lucasfilm's campus stood as eight- and nine-storey slabs under orange tile: OSM's
     16–25 m parts drawn in the presidio style's 3 m storeys. Now restyled (new manifest san-francisco/presidio.json,
     seven parts): cream stucco, `eaves` from OSM's height less its own roof:height, four storeys (five on the 25 m
     wing), the roof toned to the muted terracotta of the photographs and OSM's roof:colour (Mapillary on Letterman
     Drive). And the general fault under it, in render/prepare.ts: an entry's hand-counted `levels` were dropped by the
     facade grammar whenever the storeys had to stretch past 1.35× the style's — it re-derived the count from the
     height. An entry that names its storeys now marks the building `stretch`, as churches are, so the count holds —
     when it makes sense for the height (2.6–5.5 m a storey; twelve mechanical entries carry counts that do not, e.g.
     1200 Van Ness 68 m in 3, Arden 30 m in 16, and are left to the grammar as before).
     The Main Post's brick barracks, the Tunnel Tops lawns and the Palace of Fine Arts beyond read right.
272. **★ FOR THE OWNER FIRST — the Golden Gate Bridge moved 49 m north, and its south end re-ordered** (Presidio audit,
     2: Fort Point). Found at Fort Point: the bridge's anchorage block and pylon stood INSIDE the fort and the Fort
     Point arch landed on the bluff south of it; Commons photographs from the courtyard show the arch spanning the
     courtyard and the concrete pylon beyond its far wall. Two faults, both measured against OSM:
     - *Along the axis.* `tools/bridge-fit.ts` fits the model ACROSS the axis (0.8 m) and cannot see an error ALONG it.
       The model's towers stood 47 m (south) and 51 m (north) short of OSM's tower stacks (building:part 13298…,
       13308…), whose spacing is 1 283 m against GG_MAIN's 1 280. The NEAR entry (bridges.json) is now centred on the
       stacks' midpoint (37.819756, −122.478558; was 37.81932, −122.47851), bearing unchanged (OSM's axis −5.27° v −5.259).
     - *The south end's order.* Was side span → anchorage (30 m) → arch (90 m). OSM, south to north: South Anchorage
       (1560010167) z 1848 → Pylon S2 (1560010164/5) 1886 → Fort Point Arch (1560010166) 1939 → Pylon S1 (1560010162/3)
       1998 → side span → south tower 2340 (S1 to tower 342 m, GG_SIDE 343). Now pylon S1 at the side span's end, the arch
       112 m over the fort to pylon S2, the anchorage 38 m beyond (src/city/sf/bridges.ts, golden-gate.ts).
     | world z | before | after | OSM |
     |---|---|---|---|
     | south tower | 2293 | 2340 | 2340 |
     | north tower | 3567 | 3616 | 3618 |
     | side span end / pylon S1 | 1951 | 1999 | 1998 |
     | arch end / pylon S2 | 1832 | 1887 | 1886 |
     | steel's south end (GG_SOUTH_END) | 1830 | 1830 (local −1105 → −1154) | ways leave the straight |
     | steel's north end (GG_NORTH_END) | 4199 | 4199 (local 1275 → 1226) | ways leave the straight |
     Both ends of the steel were fitted by eye in the world (#218b) and keep their places. Checked: `bake --decks`; the
     sidewalks now ride −1149…1225 of the −1154…1226 deck at 0.0 m; air-paths bike and run within 500 m of both ends:
     none; cars reach both carriageways (81353, 81374) both ways; vitest 1625. The FAR skyline entry (tools/cities.ts,
     baked into the index) was already at the true midpoint along the axis (z 2983) and is left for the next full bake.
     Also Fort Point itself: its barbette roof was capped right across, so the fort was a solid block; now a ring
     round the open parade ground (`capPolygonWithHoles`), the parade floor lifted clear of the terrain.
273. **Hospitals audit, 2 / Mission Bay: the UCSF Medical Center at Mission Bay** (way/24451951, 1825–1975 4th St; the
     children's, women's and cancer hospitals), a 250 m generic beige block. Now `ucsfMissionBayHospital`
     (mission-bay.ts): a glazed ground storey, seven storeys of white metal panel in deep horizontal bands with ribbon
     windows, a recessed glass top storey (OSM's 24 m raised to 31); and `ucsfMbCanopy`, the entrance canopy on its
     bright green columns, a lat/lon entry over the drop-off loop on 4th Street (Commons, "UCSF Mission Bay Entrance").
     Not yet: the warm cream panels some of its blocks wear.
274. **Libraries audit, 1: three Carnegie branches.** Mission (1916, 24th St), Noe Valley (1916, Jersey St) and Sunset
     (1918, Irving at 18th Ave) were a brick block and two apartment-styled ones. Now one shared builder
     (sf/libraries.ts `carnegieBranch`, new manifest san-francisco/libraries.json): a raised basement of small
     windows, the tall reading-room windows over it (arched at Mission and Sunset, square at Noe Valley), a moulded
     cornice, a red tile hip, the entrance in the middle of the street front up a flight of steps in a stone surround
     with a little pediment — in each one's own wall: pink-beige terracotta, buff brick, red brick (a Mapillary frame on
     each street). Remaining branches without models: Park, Potrero, Ortega, Anza, West Portal, Eureka Valley,
     Parkside, Mission Bay (Park's Mapillary frame showed the wrong building — a reference would help).
275. **Museums audit, 1** (aerials over the Legion of Honor, the Asian Art Museum, the Exploratorium, the Contemporary
     Jewish Museum, the Maritime Museum, the Palace of Fine Arts):
     - **The Legion of Honor's Court of Honor** held a little Victorian cottage: OSM's glass pyramid skylight
       (way/393275341, `tourism=artwork`) drawn by the house style. Now `legionPyramid` (legion.ts): a low glass
       pyramid on a stone curb. And the court itself was lawn — the park's slope rose through the model's paving slab;
       the court now also goes to the city as paved GROUND (`out.grounds`), draped.
     - **Aquatic Park's two Speaker Towers** (1930s WPA, ways 288388248 and 564206112) were narrow Victorian houses on
       the lawn, one red, one pink. Now `speakerTowerEast`/`West` (sf/speaker-towers.ts): the white Streamline Moderne
       column flaring into a round louvred drum (Commons, "StreamlineModerneTower.jpg").
     Seen fine: the Asian Art Museum, the Exploratorium's piers, St Patrick's beside the CJM, the Maritime Museum's
     streamlined bathhouse, the Palace of Fine Arts.
276. **The ferry gates by the Ferry Building** (owner: "the pier … where the ferries actually dock needs tons of work"
     — bare grey gangways out to T-shaped walks over the water). Now `ferryGatesEFG` (sf/ferry-gates.ts, one entry in
     embarcadero.json, world-aligned about one point, y 0 the sea): for each of Gates E, F and G, the grey granite GATE
     kiosk with its pedimented cap, the gate's letter cut over a stainless marquee; the gangway's white steel trusses,
     handrails and arched roof along the package's own foot bridge (80426, 80425, 80424) and its heights; the float — a
     dark steel barge on OSM's outline (23623549, 661723971, 661723973), four tall white guide piles, a white arched
     canopy over its boarding end (Commons, 2019–2020 photographs of the expansion). The entry is `coversRaised`, so
     the gangways' floors are the model's (steel plate); on the floats the city draws the walks, and no longer the
     retaining walls it stood along them over the sea bed: render/city.ts leaves out a FOOT way's wall piece inside a
     landmark's declared footprint (`inCover`; streets keep theirs — the bridges' footprints take in Doyle Drive and
     the Embarcadero).
     And a regression of my own, found from the plaza at rider height: #264's `liftWalksOffTheSea` lifted EVERY foot
     way point inside a fixed pier outline to the deck height — but the Ferry Building's pier outline takes in its
     whole plaza on dry land, 2 m above the sea, so the walks there rose 2 m and stood in walls. Now only a point down
     at the water (at most 0.3 m over the sea) is eligible (tests/lift-walks.test.ts). Full bake 5.
     Verified after bake 5: the walks to the gates now run level into the kiosks (298 walks lifted, was 702 — the other
     404 were on dry land inside pier outlines); air-paths run 25 stretches / 0.35 km, unchanged; bike 16 / 0.78 km,
     none near the gates or the Golden Gate's ends.
277. **Civic Center, 1: the mechanical "southern SF" brick restyles.** A rule from an earlier batch (lowrise.json, "rule
     6: southern SF (lat < 37.7850)") made fourteen buildings brick by latitude. Looked at: the **Earl Warren Building**
     (1922, the Supreme Court of California, on Civic Center Plaza) now `earlWarrenBuilding` (sf/civic-center.ts):
     granite Renaissance Revival, a rusticated base with the three great arched entrances and their lanterns, the
     piano nobile's tall arched windows over a balustrade, two plain storeys under a deep cornice, a low slate roof
     (Commons). The **State of California Building** (modern: pale panels and blue-green ribbon glass, Mapillary)
     restyled pale `bauhaus`. And from what they are: the **Hall of Justice** (1958 concrete) `brutalist`; the **Public
     Safety Building** (2015) `skyscraper`; six 2010s residential towers on Van Ness and in Mid-Market (Konrad on the
     Park, Chorus, Arden and three unnamed) `bauhaus`. Left as brick: 1750 McAllister, the Academy of Art's School of Fashion,
     DeMarillac Academy, Bayanihan Community Center and one unnamed block (unchecked).
278. **Labyrinths and libraries, 2.** Grace Cathedral's outdoor terrazzo labyrinth (OSM n9010894444) was not drawn: now
     `graceLabyrinth` (park-sights.ts), the eleven circuits and the central rose, draped on the cathedral's plaza. And
     the **Park Branch Library** (1909, Page St) joins the Carnegie branches (#274) from its Commons photograph: buff
     brick, tall square windows, the pedimented door up a long stair, a dark cornice over a flat roof. (The Bernal
     Heights labyrinth is not in OSM — FOR THE OWNER: a location would let it be drawn like Lands End's.)
279. **The "square of water inside the pier" by the Ferry Building** (the owner's screenshot, 37.794588 −122.392631).
     Between the Embarcadero promenade and the South Basin plaza, and round the gates' kiosks, the bay showed through:
     OSM's coastline there is the OLD bulkhead (1717,142 → 1673,187), the 2020 deck over the basin is the pier outline
     661723975 with no surface, and only the plaza's own rectangle had a deck (marina.ts `plazaDeck`). Now
     `southBasinDeck` (ferry-gates.ts): concrete on the pier outline clipped to the seaward side of the coastline and
     south of the Ferry Building's south corner, edge beam and piles, `coversRaised`. And its LEVEL, which took two
     bakes and is the general fix (tools/bridges.ts `liftWalksOffTheSea`, tests/lift-walks.test.ts):
     - which walk points are ON a deck: inside a fixed pier's outline AND over the water by the bake's own sea and
       lakes (the coastline is the shore) — not "down at the water" by the elevation tiles, which read the basin as
       low land at −83 (bake 6 left the kiosk walks there, 1.5 m under the plaza);
     - at what level: a fixed pier whose outline also takes in dry walks (the promenade, the Ferry Building's arcade)
       is level with the highest of them, held to 1.5–4.5 m over the sea; a pier wholly over the water keeps the
       generic 3.5 m; a deck is flat, so its walks take its level exactly — except a foot BRIDGE on it, only lifted;
     - floats and walks loose on the water as before. Full bake 7.
280. **The three churches from #247 whose fronts were in doubt**, settled by OSM's own address where it has one:
     **Soto Zen Mission Sokoji** is 1691 *Laguna* Street — the street lies at bearing 81 from its middle — so its front
     is 81 (was 170, toward Sutter). **Sinai Memorial Chapel** is 1501 *Divisadero*, at bearing 81: its front was
     already 81, the entrance's street, so the kit's tower being away from the NEAREST street (Geary) is right. The
     **Seventh-day Adventist Japanese Church** (way/262751771) has no address, and the nearest Mapillary frame shows a
     grey Italianate house that may be its neighbour — FOR THE OWNER: a photograph or the address would settle it.
     After bake 7 the basin's walks are on its deck (sea + 3.5, the pier's own dry level), the walk out to the gates
     ramping 0.7 m up off the promenade over 8 m; 410 walks decked city-wide. Left: from the promenade the deck's edge
     stands up to a metre over the paving, with a strip of water at the old bulkhead line — FOR THE OWNER to judge.
281. **The ferry gangways, declared.** Their OSM ways (80424–6) came out of the generic bridge rule humped mid-span and
     dipping 2 m at the kiosk (its node lies exactly on the pier outline's edge, so the deck rule missed it). Now
     declared decks (src/city/sf/bridges.ts `FERRY_GANGWAYS`, `gangwayY`: a straight fall from the deck at sea + 3.5 to
     the float at sea + 0.5, the line run 1.5 m past each end so a rounding cannot drop an end node off it), and the
     model draws its trusses and floor on the same line. `bake --decks`; air-paths within 200 m: none.
282. **The Wiggle** (queue: "the flat bike route"): `wiggleSharrows` (sf/wiggle.ts, sights.json) — green-backed
     sharrows every 35 m both ways along Duboce, Steiner, Waller, Pierce, Haight and Scott to Fell, from OSM's own
     intersections, draped; one entry whose footprint is the Steiner & Waller crossing alone, so it claims nothing.
283. **Golden Gate Park and Parnassus, more.** `mclarenLodge` (1896, the park's headquarters: rough sandstone, tile
     hips with dormers, the loggia-and-balcony block) and `stowLakeBoathouse` (the log-sided boathouse, its deck on
     the lake, front 187 toward the shore 10 m south) in sf/park-buildings.ts (Commons photographs). UCSF Parnassus
     (campuses.json): Moffitt-Long Hospital (relation/3830592 — a relation is packed as id × 2 + 1, which is why the
     package's "7661185" is in no cache), Medical Sciences and Clinical Science cream with ribbon windows; the Health
     Sciences twin towers concrete (Commons, "UCSF panorama.jpg").
284. **Market Street audit, 2: the whole street, end to end** — eleven views from the Ferry Building to Castro, each
     30 m back and 20 m up over the street's own line (from the package's Market Street ways, not a straight line:
     the first try put half the cameras inside blocks). Read right: the Path of Gold lamps, the red transit lanes and
     boarding islands, Lotta's Fountain at Kearny, the Hibernia and the Mid-Market blocks, the Civic Center towers,
     the Castro and Sutro Tower at the top. Nothing new to fix on this pass.
285. **Checks on this stretch's city-wide rules** (advisor review):
     - `liftWalksOffTheSea` after bake 7 (410 walks decked, was 298): city-wide air-paths unchanged — run 25 stretches
       / 0.35 km, bike 16 / 0.78 km, the same "under something" counts. Every placed `deckAbove` model checked against
       the walks inside its box: the Ferry Plaza deck and the South Basin plaza at 0.00; Pier 7 at +0.35 (its declared
       deck); the marinas' and the Sutro Baths' boxes take in land walks (their models draw boats and floats at the
       sea, not a deck), and Hyde Street Pier's 154 × 150 m box takes in Beach Street's walks — its own way
       (25319880) is `access=no` and not in the ride graph, the only walk over its water a foot bridge at the deck.
     - `prepare.ts` `stretch` (#271) gated on a sane storey (2.6–5.5 m), after listing all 168 entries with both a
       height and a count: twelve fall outside.
     - The foot-way-only `inCover` wall skip (#276).
286. **Presidio Tunnel Tops: the blue slab.** Over the Parkway tunnels, a bright blue 135 × 47 m rectangle lay on the
     lawn: the Outpost nature-play hillside is mapped as one `leisure=playground`, and the playground kit
     (render/playground.ts) surfaced the WHOLE fitted box. The surfacing is now a pad no bigger than a play area is
     (32 × 18 m at most) round the kit, along the box's middle; the kit is chosen and spread for the pad, and a large
     mapped playground keeps its grass. Small playgrounds are unchanged. The Tunnel Tops' gardens themselves (the
     paths, the overlooks, the planting) are otherwise the park the city draws — FOR THE OWNER: a photograph of what
     they should look like from the lawn would let them be modelled.
287. **Parc 55 (owner's report E, 37.784857 −122.409074: "another intersecting case … might actually be real").** It
     is real, half of it: OSM's Parc 55 outline is the whole lot with a NOTCH for the Found Hotel (140 Mason, 33 m)
     and its neighbour — the footprints do not overlap, and the photographs (Commons, "Parc 55, San Francisco 2019")
     show the tower rising straight up beside them. What was wrong was the roof: ring-tower.ts capped every preset
     with a fan from the centroid ("tower footprints are near-convex"), which on a notched ring threw a lid over the
     notch at 125 m and past the outline. Now triangulated (earcut, `capPolygonWithHoles`) — every ring-tower preset.
288. **Owner's report A (37.787745 −122.403771, "another custom building intersecting a generic building")** looked at
     again: the Mutual Savings Bank (way/149335987, custom) between 716 Market and the tower on Kearny — no footprints
     overlap (sampled at 0.5 m), and from Market and from above the three stand side by side without fighting; resolved
     by #191–193's insets and coplanar removal. Seen on the way: the diagonal ridge on 2-8 Kearny's roof is real — its
     28 m arms (part 940203045) close in a 37° wedge over a 20 m part (940203046).
289. **Union Square, the careful pass** (owner's queue), from the square at rider height looking north, south, east and
     west: Macy's (the stone block and the glass building with its star), Neiman Marcus and the City of Paris glass
     rotunda, Saks, the St. Francis and its tower, the Grand Hyatt, the Dewey Monument, 555 California's red granite in
     the distance — each where it is and dressed. Nothing new to fix from the square itself.
290. **Golden Gateway Center's townhouses** (queue: "still a brick restyle"): Mapillary on Jackson and Davis shows them
     WHITE — stucco walls, steep dark gabled roofs, chimneys, angled bay dormers — on a podium of dark brown
     exposed-aggregate panels with white-trimmed round arches along the street. Restyled (prominent.json, both
     podiums' entries): `victorian` in white with dark gables; the south garage podium (way/588365127) `brutalist` in
     the aggregate's brown. Not yet: the podium's street arcade, and OSM's 12–13 m make them four storeys where the
     photographs show about three.
291. **The Seward Mini Park, "heavily wooded"** (#240's zone): the park asked for 0.03 trees a square metre and got 11
     trees on 3 000 m² — every park spot was capped at `MAX_DENSITY` (0.0078, set for forests). A park under a hectare
     may now be as thick as its zone asks, up to 0.02 (src/city/park-planting.ts `SMALL_PARK_MAX_DENSITY`); `TILE_TREES`
     still bounds the tile. Only two zones ask past the old cap: Seward and the Tree Fern Dell.
     **And a mistake of mine, caught and repaired:** I first re-planted with `bake --replant`, which turned out to be
     STALE — it re-tiles from the package JSON, which covers only the detail box, and left 7 746 tiles of 9 206 (the
     wider ring's deleted, v3 too) and 46 864 street trees of 72 204. A full bake (8) restored them; `--replant` now
     refuses to run (tools/bake.ts) unless forced. The game on 5200 served the cut-down city for about ten minutes.
292. **The Presidio coastal batteries, placed** (queue: "the batteries"). Their builders (districts/batteries.ts, BUGS.md
     #213c) were parked with batteries.json EMPTY until a model shot showed a fortification — so every battery stood in
     the game as a 12 m presidio-style barracks with windows and a red tile roof. The shots now do: low grey concrete
     parapets broken by gun pits, magazine doors, an apron, the earth behind (Chamberlin from the beach and from the
     bluff). Placed: Chamberlin, Crosby, Godfrey, and Boutelle and Cranston on the builders of Crosby and Godfrey
     (batteries.json; the audit's dead list updated). And their SHADOW: a landmark's shadow is its whole footprint
     projected at its tallest point, which over a 63 × 114 m outline holding 4 m parapets laid a dark field across the
     grass; a model under 6 m now casts none (render/landmarks.ts).
293. **The cliff paths at the Legion of Honor** (queue): the Coastal Trail and Lands End Trail below the Legion — air-paths
     within 600 m: none in the air; from the trail at rider height, the path on its slope under the cypress and the
     view out. What was wrong there was my own #267 bank (dark slabs beside the trail), gone with the foot-way rule.
294. **Mission Bay, the creek-side and Third Street apartments**, restyled from Mapillary frames on Berry and Third
     (mission-bay.json): **Edgewater** (355 Berry) in its bold red horizontal siding; **Park Terrace** (325 Berry) white
     with dark blue window bays and orange vertical accents; **Mission Walk** (330 and 335 Berry) white with dark grey
     frames; the **Edwin M. Lee Apartments** (1150 3rd) blue-grey metal over a board-formed concrete band and a glass
     base. Strata, Five 88, MB360 and UCSF Housing still want usable frames (Mapillary's are all of parked cars).
295. **The tallest generic buildings, a census** (scratch generic.ts: every baked building over 35 m that no manifest
     claims, inside 4.5 km). Fixed from it: the **Potrero Power Station stack** (way/678950945, `man_made=smoke_stack`,
     91 m) was an 11 × 11 m glass skyscraper — the bake's infrastructure filter knows `chimney` but not `smoke_stack`,
     and the stack is a landmark worth keeping; now `potreroStack`, the tapering concrete column with its dark lip and
     platform ring. The **Hamm's Building** (1550 Bryant) restyled to its brick base and pale stucco floors (OSM's 72 m
     unverified — FOR THE OWNER). Left as they are: the Hilton Union Square's tower 3 (106 m, a plain tower), Bay View
     Tower (2601 Mission), and parts of claimed buildings.
296. **Scenic viewpoints: the trees off the summits** (queue: "clear the trees at the key views"). From rider height at
     Twin Peaks, Alamo Square, Dolores Park, Bernal Heights, Grand View, Corona Heights and Ina Coolbrith: the
     `plant-the-hills` zone gave Twin Peaks' and Corona Heights' summits its park planting — eucalyptus, oak, pink
     blossom on a mown lawn, in the view. New treeless zones over Twin Peaks' summits and Christmas Tree Point, Corona
     Heights' rocky top and Tank Hill (data/zones, priority 150, density 0). Full bake 9; Twin Peaks now open grass.
     (The summits are still ~30 m low and flat-topped: BUGS.md #260, the owner's call.)
297. **Historic buildings, a census** (scratch hist.py: named OSM buildings with wikidata/historic/heritage tags that no
     manifest claims, inside 4.5 km — 53 of them). First from it: the **Mission Dolores Basilica** (1918, way/256442760),
     deliberately "left untouched" when the old Mission was modelled, so a generic block stood beside the city's oldest
     building. Now `missionDoloresBasilica` (civic-center.ts, churches-5.json): white stucco under red tile, the tiled
     dome on its windowed drum at the crossing, the two Churrigueresque bell towers on Dolores Street with their open
     belfries, finials and green-tiled caps (Commons). Still on the census list for later: the Swedenborgian Church, St
     Nicholas Russian Orthodox Cathedral, St John the Evangelist, Kezar Pavilion, the Randall Museum, the Vogue Theatre,
     Hotel Majestic, the Jones Street and Ashbury water tanks, Treasure Island's naval buildings.
298. **Historic census, 2: Saint Nicholas Russian Orthodox Cathedral** (way/260494870, 15th Street), a generic block.
     Now `saintNicholasCathedral` (civic-center.ts, churches-5.json) from a Mapillary frame on 15th: yellow clapboard
     Gothic with white trim, the front tower through the gable with its pointed window and red doors, the dark steep
     spire, the teal onion bulb and the Orthodox cross. Front 356 (the bearing to 15th Street) — a "street" front put
     the tower on the back twice.
299. **Historic census, 3: the fire department's high-pressure water tanks** — the Ashbury tank and the Jones Street tank
     (1909–1913, the Auxiliary Water Supply System) stood as 12 m apartment blocks with windows. Now `ashburyTank` /
     `jonesStreetTank` (speaker-towers.ts, campuses.json): the round riveted steel tank with its bands, the low conical
     roof on a deep eave, the little cupola (Commons, "Ashbury Heights tank, circa 1915"). Jones Street's cream gatehouse
     with its red pediment (Commons, 2020) is a separate small building, left to the city.

### Night of 2026-10-03/04 — summary for the owner (#265–#299)

**Look at first:** #272 (the Golden Gate Bridge moved 49 m north onto OSM's towers, its south end re-ordered over Fort
Point) and BUGS.md #260 (every hilltop ~30 m low — your call on the city-wide fix) and #261 (the Bay Bridge's towers
~115 m south-west of the real ones — your call on how to move it).

**Second: the ride graph (#303, #304).** The rider floated up to 5.5 m over 18th Street (a split's heights drawn in a
straight line), and on split-level streets rode the midline between the two roadways. Both fixed in
src/city/ridegraph.ts; air-paths bike 16 stretches / 0.78 km → 4 / 0.09 km, run 25 / 0.35 km → 16 / 0.22 km. #304 is
a routing change: carriageways more than 1.5 m apart in height are no longer merged, so ~36 split boulevards are now
two one-way roads (bikes may ride either way), each with its own junction prompts — your "one path per street" rule
gives way where the two roadways are on different levels.

**Fixed / built:** Castro Theatre; Kezar Stadium (stands, track); Strawberry Hill's summit (raw relief zone); trail
walls gone (foot ways draped, no retaining walls); California Academy of Sciences (living roof); Lone Mountain (USF)
and its stairway gate; SF General (Zuckerberg drum, the 1915 brick wards); Letterman Digital Arts Center; Fort Point's
open courtyard; UCSF Mission Bay hospital; four Carnegie branch libraries; the Legion's glass pyramid and paved court;
Aquatic Park's speaker towers; the ferry gates E/F/G (kiosks, gangways, floats) and the South Basin deck; walks on
piers (level with their land, decked only over the water); Earl Warren Building and the "brick-by-latitude" restyles;
Grace Cathedral's labyrinth; the Wiggle's sharrows; McLaren Lodge and the Stow Lake boathouse; Parnassus colours; Parc
55's roof (owner report E); Union Square pass; Golden Gateway townhouses (white); Seward Mini Park woods; Presidio
batteries placed; treeless summits at Twin Peaks, Corona Heights and Tank Hill; Mission Bay apartments on Berry;
the Potrero stack; Mission Dolores Basilica; St Nicholas Cathedral; the Ashbury and Jones Street water tanks;
PHARMACY spelled right; playground slabs sized to a play area.

**A mistake of mine, repaired:** `bake --replant` deleted 1 460 tiles; a full bake restored them and the flag now
refuses to run (#291).

**Your call:** #305, the Fillmore overpass's sidewalks meeting the trench walks 4 m lower (two fixes tried and
reverted; the designed fix is for walks inside a ramp street's corridor to take its heights).

Later the same morning: the Vogue Theatre, Kezar Pavilion, Hotel Majestic (#300–302).

**References wanted:** the footbridge helix (37.752148 −122.442793); the Rutter courtyard; the 450 South garage; the
Seventh-day Adventist Japanese Church; the Bernal Heights labyrinth's place; the Tunnel Tops gardens; Strata, Five 88,
MB360, UCSF Housing; the Swedenborgian Church; Hamm's Building's true height.
300. **Historic census, 4: the Vogue Theatre** (1912, Sacramento Street, way/263646726), a generic Victorian. Now
     `vogueTheatre` (castro-theatre.ts) from a Mapillary frame: the cream Art Deco front stepped up in piers, the brown
     VOGUE blade lit down both faces, the marquee board and the streamlined canopy over the doors; front 171, the
     bearing to Sacramento Street.
301. **Historic census, 5: Kezar Pavilion** (1924, way/120481450), a 96 m presidio barracks beside the stadium. Now
     `kezarPavilion` (kezar.ts): pale olive stucco, the tall arched windows over a darker base, the deep dark corbelled
     eave, the low tiled roof (Mapillary on Stanyan).
302. **Historic census, 6: Hotel Majestic** (1902, Sutter at Gough), restyled `sfapartment` (stacked bays, cornice) in
     its own beige with white trim and the sage-green ground storey (Mapillary at Gough).
303. **The rider floating 5 m over 18th Street** (air-paths' worst bike stretch, 37.762413 −122.399279). The ride graph's
     `dropSidePaths` (src/city/ridegraph.ts) splits a road where a side path joins it, and gave the two halves heights in
     a STRAIGHT LINE between the cut's ends — throwing away the street's profile between them. Over Potrero Hill's dip
     on 18th Street the rider rode a chord up to 5.5 m above the road. The halves now take the road's own heights at
     every point (bisected stage by stage: classify → … → dropSidePaths was where −74.1 became −69.2). `bake --rides`;
     air-paths bike 16 stretches / 0.78 km → 11 / 0.42 km; run unchanged; ride-graph tests pass.
304. **Split-level streets ridden down the middle of the wall.** The ride graph merges a street's two parallel
     carriageways into one edge down the middle (`mergeParallel`), which allowed up to 4 m between their heights — so on
     a split-level street (Carolina and De Haro on Potrero Hill, Bridgeway in Sausalito, Van Ness by Aquatic Park) the
     averaged midline ran between the two roadways, over the retaining wall, with nothing drawn under the wheel. Now
     only carriageways within 1.5 m of each other merge (`isParallelPair`); the others stay two roads. Measured:
     air-paths bike 11 stretches / 0.42 km → 4 / 0.09 km, run 25 / 0.35 km → 16 / 0.22 km; the ride graph 12 107 → 12 180
     edges, dead ends unchanged (716); every scenic route still routes (tools/check-scenic.ts); 1656 tests pass.
     (With #303: bike 16 / 0.78 km at the start of the night → 4 / 0.09 km.)
305. **OPEN — the Fillmore overpass's sidewalk steps.** At Fillmore over the Geary underpass (37.784347 −122.432880) the
     bridge's sidewalks (59050/59051, kept level with the road deck by #262's rule) meet the walks beside the trench 4 m
     lower: air-paths' biggest remaining run cluster. Tried: grading the joining ground foot ways up to the sidewalk
     ends (10%, smoothstep along the connected walks) — it made run 16 stretches → 43, because a lifted GROUND foot way
     has no structure drawn under it. Reverted (full bake 10). A second try flagging the lifted walks `ramp` was worse:
     several sidewalk ends lifted the same walk again (8–13 m), run 43 stretches. Reverted (full bake 11). Needs a
     designed fix — the trench's side walks should follow Fillmore's own ramp, not the ground — FOR THE OWNER to
     prioritise; it is one junction.
306. **The last five "brick-by-latitude" restyles** (#277's list), each checked against a Mapillary frame: **1750
     McAllister** a grey stucco tower with orange balcony bays (`towerblock`); the **Academy of Art's School of
     Fashion** buff stone Beaux-Arts (`classical`); **way/35176281** by Civic Center grey rusticated granite
     (`classical`); **DeMarillac Academy** cream Italianate stucco with arched windows (`stucco`); the **Bayanihan
     Community Center** really is red brick with white trim — kept. The rule's fourteen entries are all looked at now.
307. **The rule-2 churches, 1: four built with the church kit** (sf/kit-churches.ts, churches-5.json; the lowrise.json
     "place of worship → classical" restyles for them removed). From Mapillary frames: the **National Shrine of Saint
     Francis of Assisi** — white Gothic, twin square towers with pinnacle clusters, lancets (front 172, Vallejo);
     **Nuestra Señora de Guadalupe** — white Spanish baroque, the curved gable and its medallion, twin domed belfries,
     raised on its stair (171, Broadway); **Saint Dominic's** — grey granite Gothic, flying buttresses, the pinnacled
     corner tower, a rose window (81, Steiner: the kit puts the front on a SHORT end, and the nave runs along Bush);
     **Temple Sherith Israel** — sandstone Romanesque, the great arched window, the dome (171, California). Saint Agnes'
     frame was a crane; seven rule-2 churches left (Konko, St Brigid, St Vincent de Paul, St Agnes, St Mark's, Central
     Seventh-day Adventist, First Baptist).
308. **The rule-2 churches, 2:** **Saint Brigid** (grey granite Romanesque, the square tower; front 261, Van Ness),
     **Saint Vincent de Paul** (brick, the corner belfry tower; 171, Green), **Saint Mark's Lutheran** (red brick
     Romanesque, the pinnacled corner tower, rose window and stair; 171, O'Farrell) — kit-churches.ts from Mapillary
     frames. **First Baptist** really is a cream classical block — its restyle kept. Left: Konko Church, Central
     Seventh-day Adventist, Saint Agnes (no usable frames yet).
309. **"Rule 1: chinatown zone" outside Chinatown**: the Stanford Court Hotel and the Brocklebank Apartments on Nob Hill
     and Central Police Station on Vallejo wore the Chinatown style (red balconies, signs). Restyled from Mapillary:
     buff and cream Nob Hill masonry (`sfapartment`), and the station's concrete fins over a dark base (`brutalist`).
     The Chinese Independent Baptist Church really is in Chinatown — kept.
310. **Rule 8 "tenement" restyles, 1: the Curran Theatre** (way/71504452, Geary) wore a tenement restyle. Now
     `curranTheatre` (sf/castro-theatre.ts, churches-5.json, front 351): buff brick over a stone base, tall arched
     windows, the dark marquee over the doors and the pink vertical CURRAN blade, from a Mapillary frame on Geary.
311. **Rule 8 "tenement" restyles, 2 — the last four**, from Mapillary frames: **Saybrook University** a converted
     warehouse in charcoal-painted concrete with wide steel windows (`bauhaus`, wall #4a4b4d); the **Consulate General
     of Mexico** (532 Folsom) red brick (`brick`); **Rhoda Goldman Plaza** (2180 Post) pale sage stucco with trellises
     (`stucco`, #c9ccb6); **Next Door Shelter** (1001 Polk) a cream terracotta classical block (`classical`, #ddd3bd).
     No "rule 8" tenement restyles are left. tools/photo.ts gained `--pick=N` to download a candidate other than the first.
312. **The Masonic** (way/32947040, 1111 California) was a rule-7 `chicago` office block. Now `masonicTemple`
     (sf/masonic.ts, churches-5.json, front 0): a windowless white Vermont-marble box, the glazed lobby at the west of the
     California front behind twin columns crowned with globes, Emile Norman's sculpture (four tall figures, the frieze,
     the inscription) at the east end (Mapillary: white veined marble; Wikipedia / artandarchitecture-sf for the rest).
     **Engine: `Fit.grade`.** A landmark's base is the LOWEST ground under it; the Masonic's block falls 19 m from
     California to Pine, so its entrance at y = 0 was buried under the street and the front read as a blank wall.
     The loader now measures the ground at the front's middle over the base (`fit.grade`, src/render/landmarks.ts);
     a builder on a hillside puts its street-level features there. Only masonic.ts uses it so far — other hillside
     landmarks with buried entrances can adopt it.
313. **Rule-7 `chicago` restyles**, from Mapillary: **2055 Sacramento** a buff 1960s concrete slab (`towerblock`
     #c8b79c); **way/693630795** on Rincon Hill rust-red panelled flats with bay windows (`towerblock` #8c4632);
     **way/944981401** pale limestone panels (`bauhaus` #e0dbcf). Kept: the **Army and Navy YMCA** (red brick, a
     terracotta-arcaded top, a stone base: Chicago-school tripartite is right), the **Academy of Art game
     development** building (plain cream concrete). **way/1258748457** (Pine at Battery): frames only of a granite pier
     and a brass doorway — left as is, NEEDS A REFERENCE.
314. **Hillside churches stand on their basements.** Old Saint Mary's portal was half under California Street: the
     loader seats a landmark at the lowest ground under it and California climbs 5 m across the church. The church
     kit (`church()`, src/render/landmarks/church.ts) now lifts the whole church by `fit.grade` − 0.5 (#312) onto a
     plain grey ashlar plinth — the exposed basement on the downhill side — when that is over 0.8 m. Every kit church
     on a slope gets it (Saints Peter and Paul ~1.5 m). Audit of all procedural landmarks on footprints spanning > 5 m
     (scratch slopeland.ts; 57, many of them piers over water): the Cable Car Barn and CPMC Van Ness lose only a
     little of their lowest storey to the hill — left.
315. **Notre Dame des Victoires** (566 Bush) was a 10 m ochre ring with one corner campanile. Rebuilt with the church kit
     (civic-1.ts, front 180 — Bush is SOUTH of it, the downhill side; height 22): buff sandstone, the stained-glass
     wheel between two pinnacled towers, the porch and its steps (Wikipedia, noehill SF Landmark #173: after
     Fourvière in Lyon, "a stained-glass central bay flanked by cross-topped masonry towers").
316. **Rule 4 ("Russian Hill/Nob Hill apartment") restyles**, from Mapillary: **1960 Vallejo** pink Mediterranean

### Sunday 2026-10-04 — summary for the owner (#310–#341)

**Your requests today, all done and checked in renders:** the red cubes at Powell & Post (the Westin St. Francis's
awnings — the hotel was a quarter turn round; rebuilt to your photo, colonnade and both awning rows, #327–#328);
Market Street's pavement (red centre lanes Gough→3rd only, green bike lanes Gough→8th, brick squares off Market,
#329, #338); the public clocks telling the real time in every city (#331); the mobile UI (#332).
**A mistake of mine, repaired:** a python edit emptied src/render/city.ts; rebuilt from this session's transcript
and checked line for line (#330). PLEASE COMMIT when you can — a day of work is only in the working tree.
**Also:** Curran Theatre, the Masonic (and `Fit.grade` for hillside landmarks), Notre Dame des Victoires, eleven more
churches, hillside churches on plinths, no shopfronts or fire escapes on institutions (the bake now marks
schools, hospitals, churches, civic buildings and the works), USF's campus, Saint Francis Memorial, the Presidio
sheds and golf course planting, Pier 70, Strawberry Hill's trail steps, the Exploratorium, the Bernal labyrinth,
the Market helix (region layer cap), meadow shrubs. Bakes 12–16.
**References wanted:** Treasure Island Building 1's curved front; the Potrero branch library; Madrone Mission
Bay; Strata / Five 88 / MB360; way/1258748457 (Pine at Battery); the Presidio chapel's entrance side; the Tunnel
Tops gardens; Building 113's window rhythm. **Your call:** the Market helix's spiral-to-deck join; #305, #260, #261.

353. **The Mission's mural alleys** (icons audit): **Balmy Alley** (24th–25th, murals since 1972) and **Clarion Alley**
     (17th–18th, the Clarion Alley Mural Project) were plain. New sf/mural-alleys.ts `muralAlley`, a standalone
     model laid along each alley (sights.json, sized from the OSM ways): garage-door and fence sized panels down both
     sides, each a field of colour with bold shapes on it. Also checked present: the Painted Ladies, the sea lions,
     the Peace Pagoda, City Lights, Vesuvio, the Fillmore, the Warfield, Lombard, the windmills, the bison.
354. **Pedestrian bridges** (visitor-joys queue): the named foot bridges listed (scratch query) and the notable ones
     rendered. The Japanese Tea Garden's Drum Bridge and the Exploratorium's Fog Bridge read right. **Point Bonita**
     did not: the 1877 lighthouse was a 4 m stucco box beside a 12 m apartment block, and its suspension footbridge a
     bare plank. New sf/point-bonita.ts — `pointBonitaLighthouse` (white tower, black lantern, red roof, the fog-signal
     house) and `pointBonitaBridge` (white towers, sagging main cables, hangers, deck and rails, laid along the trail's
     bridge ways); the block restyled a low white outbuilding (sights.json). FOR THE OWNER: the whole point stands
     near sea level — the real knob is ~30 m up (the coarse terrain at the map's edge, as #260). The queue item is
     ticked; tunnels were audited in the bug-group views (#341).
355. **BUGS.md open items reviewed:** #248 closed (the de Young is built; the "unplaced" builders are superseded by
     market-east.json's entries or are unphotographed placeholder guesses beaten by the photo-checked restyles);
     #255 marked fixed (non-Paris styles paint the English sign list); #254 updated (air-paths near Yerba Buena 0
     stretches, the islands traffic zone baked, Macalla Road clear of the building — only the tunnel portals' look
     left). #244 (junction gridlock) is untouched: a traffic-sim item, priority, owner's to schedule. Air-paths after
     bakes 12–20: run 15 stretches / 0.21 km, bike 4 / 0.09 km.
356. **#244 junction gridlock — the head-on cycles.** The 900 s soak (`npx vite-node tools/traffic-soak.ts --
     san-francisco 900`) left 38 of ~200 vehicles stuck > 60 s; its cycles were two cars facing each other across a
     short street between two junctions, each refused "no room beyond" because `roomThrough` (src/sim/traffic.ts)
     counted ANY vehicle standing in the far junction's box as filling it — including the oncoming car waiting to come
     onto that street the other way, or already on it heading the other way. Those are in the other lane and are now
     ignored. Soak: stuck 38 → 6, moving ~50 → ~75; overlaps are same-direction queueing (head-on 2 pair-seconds).
     Traffic tests 16/16. The last 6 are left for the owner's #244 work.

### Sunday 2026-10-04 afternoon — summary for the owner (#342–#356)

Brick squares now actually reach the tiles (Embarcadero Plaza is brick); Madrone shows its real massing; the views
audit ran over every group (298 views) — fixes from it: Twin Peaks' trail stripes, the meadow shrub clumps, the
Temple of Music rebuilt, Market's red lanes continuous, pier and fountain cameras re-aimed. New: Five 88 and MB360
(Mission Bay), Rock Hall, the Marin Headlands' and Alcatraz's planting and cliffs, Grant Avenue's lanterns, the
Castro's rainbow crossings, Balmy and Clarion Alley murals, Point Bonita's lighthouse and footbridge. **Traffic
(#244):** a head-on rule in the junction look-ahead fixed — the soak's stuck vehicles 38 → 6. Bakes 17–20.
Still FOR THE OWNER: Strata's colours; the Potrero branch library; the Wharf's generic shop signs; the far
headlands' low terrain (Point Bonita, #260); the six remaining #244 vehicles.
357. **Monuments** (the queue's "fountains" audit): the city's fountains are covered (Vaillancourt, Lotta's,
     Mechanics, the Huntington Park turtles, the Asawa mermaid); three figure monuments were not drawn at all — the
     **Pioneer Monument** (Civic Center), the **McKinley Memorial** (the Panhandle) and **Goethe & Schiller** (Golden
     Gate Park). New sf/monuments.ts: stepped granite platforms, pedestals, bronze figures suggested by stacked
     tapering prisms (the Pioneer Monument with its four corner groups); placed by OSM's points (sights.json),
     classified `monument` in tools/nonbuildings.ts. The sculpture itself would be the owner's GLB work.
358. **More monuments** (OSM memorials with no entry): two generic builders in sf/monuments.ts — `figureOnPedestal`
     (scaled to the entry's height) and `emptyPedestal` — placed at OSM's points (sights.json): the **Admission Day**
     (Native Sons) Monument on Market, the **California Volunteers** Memorial (Market at Dolores), **Benjamin
     Franklin** in Washington Square, **Garfield** and **Halleck** in Golden Gate Park, **Sun Yat-sen** in St Mary's
     Square, **Lincoln** at City Hall, and Mount Olympus's **Triumph of Light** (its statue lost in the 1950s — the
     bare pedestal at the city's geographic centre). Heights approximate. `duplicate | figureOnPedestal` is a known
     audit finding (standalone entries cannot shadow one another).
359. **Dolores Park's lawns** were strewn with shrub clumps (an offline run of `plantTile` on the tile: 72 of its 93
     shrubs stood on mapped lawns). Two causes, both in render/plants.ts: the woodland undergrowth rule (two trunks
     within 14 m) fired under a mown park's scattered trees — it now never plants on a mapped `grass` lawn; and the
     edge rule lined every lawn's border, which in Dolores is a path on all sides — a lawn's own border now gets an
     occasional shrub (12 %) and a park's outer edge keeps its planting (70 %). Lawn shrubs on that tile 72 → 18. The
     mid-lawn beds and lone shrubs also now need a path within 12 m. Applies city-wide at once (render-time).

     with blue panels — restyled (`bauhaus` white, blue accent; mission-bay.json; MISSION-BAY.md row done). Parks
     checked from the air: Alamo Square, Washington Square, Lafayette, Alta Plaza read right.

     Mission Dolores Basilica — whose builder faces Dolores whatever its 180 says). Fixed: **St Brigid** faced away
     from Van Ness (261 → 81); **450 O'Farrell** was a second "First Church of Christ, Scientist" wearing the Franklin
     Street church's brick Romanesque model — it is the FIFTH Church, a cream neoclassical temple front: its own
     kit church facing O'Farrell. St Dominic's checked with frames from each street (2026-10-05): the portal and tower front is on Steiner, the
     buttressed flank on Bush — its 81° is right. St Joseph's (portal to the south-east) — FOR THE OWNER if wrong.
     (Strawberry Hill, continued: even at the pass's maximum density the island got 54 trees — the planting thins
     towards every path, and the hill is laced with trails. A zone's wood may now say `trailside: true` (PlantSpot,
     ParkSpot, park-planting.ts): only the path's own 2.5 m kept clear, no thinning. Bake 30: 74 trees within 70 m of
     the summit, and from the air the island reads as a wood.)
394. **Angel Island by ferry** (from the Alcatraz work, #388): Angel Island added to the region's `ferryIslands` (its
     network was dropped like Alcatraz's), and a Ferry Building ↔ Angel Island (Ayala Cove) ferry (OSM maps that
     route). Bake 28 kept 397 edges on the two islands. Ridden headless: arrival at Ayala Cove, the prompt there,
     the roads up from the cove; holding ↑ alone meets a dead end at the cove — the Perimeter Road (8 km, asphalt,
     bicycle=yes) is a turn away at the junction above it.
395. **The Music Concourse** (ggp-lm views, re-aimed): between the de Young and the Academy, the city drew open lawn
     with scattered palms; the real concourse is a sunken oval under a GRID of pollarded London planes. New
     `musicConcourse` (strawberry-hill.ts; golden-gate-park.json, standalone, turned to the axis from the bandshell):
     planes every 8 m with knobbed limbs under low flat canopies, the tan gravel promenade, the three fountains'
     stone rims and jets. The two ggp-lm views that sat inside a hedge and under the de Young's overhang re-aimed.
396. **Attractions census** (OSM buildings with tourism/historic/heritage/wikidata tags that no manifest names — 88;
     scratch attr.ts): restyled — Fog City (one low dark storey, red awning; was a 12 m walk-up), Diocesan House (grey
     stone), Yerba Buena Island's Coast Guard and light-station buildings (ten, white with red roofs, as the owner's
     photograph shows; were walk-ups), Cavallo Point and the Headlands Center for the Arts (Army white with red
     roofs — style colour, NO photograph), El Capitan (ochre ornamented stucco; was brick), the Alexandria and the
     Coliseum theatres (cream). Left: PG&E's Stations E/G/I/J/K (no usable frame), the earthquake cottages (small and
     right enough), Point Blunt Light (Angel Island, unvisited).
397. **Angel Island's buildings** (now reachable by ferry, #394): 94 buildings drawn as Victorians (54) and walk-ups
     (27) on an old Army post. A `buildings: 'presidio'` zone (`angel-island-buildings`) gives them the Army's white
     with red roofs, as the Presidio's (bake 31: 90 of 94). The Immigration Station's Detention Barracks restyled from
     Commons (white over tan, dark grey hip). Views of Ayala Cove and the Immigration Station added.
398. **Fort Baker and Fort Barry** (Marin Headlands Army posts): 74 and 29 buildings drawn as stucco boxes and walk-ups;
     Fort Baker's parade ground is white Colonial Revival under red roofs (Commons "Fort Baker, Cavallo Point.jpg"), so
     `buildings: 'presidio'` zones for both (bake 32). **Tiburon and Belvedere** (383 buildings: 217 SF walk-ups and
     160 Victorians in a Marin suburb) — no Mapillary coverage to go by; FOR THE OWNER: give them Sausalito's
     `mediterranean` zone, or something else?
399. **Japantown's yagura clock tower** (the open question in #380): the Commons caption ("looking across Post Street
     north on Buchanan Street") and four Mapillary frames at Post and Buchanan place it at the Buchanan mall's Post
     Street entrance. New `japantownYagura` (pickwick.ts; prominent.json, standalone): four tall dark timber legs,
     cross-braced, the red-brown box with a working clock on each face under a small roof (render/clocks.ts — it read
     12:35 at 00:35). View added.

400. **Fountain census** (OSM's named fountains against the manifests): the **UN Plaza fountain** on Market Street
     (Halprin, 1975) was missing — plain paving and a lawn patch. New sf/fountains.ts `unPlazaFountain`, standalone on
     OSM way/128534058's box: a granite kerb round water, smooth buff granite blocks stepped and stacked at every
     height to 4 m slabs, water over their lips and jets arching between (Commons). Not yet: the Yoda fountain at
     Letterman, the Dandelion and Aurora fountains, Fillmore Center's — small, and the generic fountain furniture
     stands at their nodes.
401. **San Francisco Designated Landmarks census** (Wikipedia's list, 97 rows with coordinates, matched to the
     building under each point — scratch lmmatch.ts): most are modelled; ten were generic and are restyled from
     Mapillary — the Belli/Genella and Hotaling and Solari buildings in Jackson Square, Donaldina Cameron House, the
     Goodman Building, the Gibb-Sanborn Warehouse, the Market Street Railway substation on Fillmore, the Chambord,
     the Westerfeld House, the Alcazar Theater (prominent.json). Frames that showed only neighbours (Old Holy Virgin
     Cathedral, Stadtmuller House, the Oak Street firehouse, Engine House 31) — left.
402. **The Yoda fountain** (Letterman, the Presidio; fountain census): new `yodaFountain` (fountains.ts; presidio.json,
     standalone at OSM's node) — the round stone basin at bench height, the faceted tan pedestal with water down its
     faces, the bronze Yoda on his cane (Commons). Raised by `offset` 0.5 m: a standalone model is seated half a metre
     below the lowest ground under it, which buried the basin wall. **Natural scenery**: `cliffs` zones on Corona
     Heights' summit, Twin Peaks' two summits and Bernal Hill's crest (bake 33) — which draw NOTHING yet: the 60 m opening
     (#260, the owner's call) flattened those summits into plateaus with no steep faces. Noted under #260.
403. **Civic Center, the close look** (owner queue): every building over 600 m² within 450 m of City Hall that no manifest
     names, listed and the big ones photographed (scratch ccbld.ts): City Hall's model covers its stray 30 m
     `chicago` part (picked: the model is what draws); the two big courtyard blocks are right as generic — rusticated
     granite classical (the courthouse on McAllister) and a modern white block on Golden Gate. Four civic views
     added. Dolores Street's palm median and the Embarcadero's palms checked (trees audit).

404. **The Westin St. Francis tower** (owner queue #125: "a unique old building with a unique modernist tower behind
     it"): the 1972 tower was a punched grid — the photographs from Union Square (Commons "WestinStFrancis.jpg") show
     horizontal bands of dark glass between darker taupe spandrels, rounded corner piers, the glass lift slot up the
     middle. `westinTower` (districts/union-square.ts): banded faces, darker taupe, rounded corner piers. View added.
405. **The Church of 8 Wheels** (the old Sacred Heart, Fillmore at Fell; sports-centre census): a 21 m sfapartment block
     — now `oldSacredHeart` on the church kit: tan Romanesque, round arches, the corner campanile, the gable front to
     Fillmore (front 270; checked from both ends). The census of malls, sports centres and supermarkets otherwise
     found them reasonable as generic (Crocker Galleria, Koret, the Safeways are plain modern boxes).
406. **Generic-church census** (every place of worship of 12 m or more still the bake's `classical` box — 24 — each
     photographed from Mapillary): six on the church kit with their fronts to their streets (kit-churches.ts: Saint
     James on Guerrero, the red brick church on South Van Ness, Saint Monica's on Geary, Park Presidio Methodist,
     Sha'ar Zahav, the 19th Avenue Baptist), thirteen restyled to what the frames show (a sawtooth-roofed hall,
     blue-painted and grey Edwardian houses that are churches, white stucco missions, tan concrete), churches-5.json.
     Three views (group `churches`) checked: fronts face their streets.
407. **School census** (named schools of 10 m+ the campus rule did not reach, photographed): AcroSports in the old
     Polytechnic gym (tan Art Deco), Star of the Sea School (cream render), Wonder Years in an old synagogue (cream
     Moorish front with a wheel window), San Francisco Pacific Academy (red stucco) restyled; the rest are schools in
     ordinary houses and shops, right as they are.
408. **Campus audit** (UCSF Parnassus, USF and Lone Mountain, Zuckerberg SF General, UC Law SF, Kaiser Geary; views
     `campus 0`–`13`): SF State and Laguna Honda are south of the box (south edge 37.7526), so they are not drawn.
     **Saint Ignatius Church** was the church() kit's one-spire guess, made with no photograph. It is now its own model
     (sf/st-ignatius.ts), built from two Commons photos: twin towers at the front corners on Fulton, each a square
     shaft, then a tall octagonal stage and an open belfry under a grey-blue domed cap and spire; a giant colonnade
     and an upper columned stage with a pediment between them; the clerestory nave; and the big ribbed dome at the
     north end. The other campuses read right.
409. **McAllister Tower (UC Law SF, 100 McAllister)**: the crown's fin pinnacles floated a whole crown height (10 m)
     over the tower. The builder started them at H, but tower() ends the tiers at H − crownH and the stepped crown
     rises from there. They are now square corner piers standing a metre over each setback's parapet, as the
     Mapillary refs show, with the flagpole on the top step. The shaft now has the `fins` face (deep piers between
     window strips) in a greyer buff. The manifest fits the model to OSM's 114 m tower part (way/1258861094) and
     restyles the 56 m and 24 m wing parts buff. The wings were not visible in the aerial render, so check them on
     the next bake.
410. **Market Street's sidewalks had a lawn down the middle** (bike audit at rider height, Embarcadero to Castro). The
     bake gives a North American street at most 3.8 m of sidewalk (Market's ways got 3.2 m). Downtown the buildings
     stand 17–18 m from the centreline, so between the kerb strip and the 3 m footway OSM maps along the shopfronts
     the city's base ground showed through. That base ground is GRASS (`road:grass` is the base plane, not a way).
     public/patches/san-francisco.json now overrides `walk` on 267 two-way Market ways, out to the nearer building
     line less 0.3 m and capped at 10 m. Measured from v3 footprints (scratch mktwalk.py); the split one-way
     carriageways were left alone so their median stays as it is. It is now brick from kerb to shopfront, as it
     really is. Other wide downtown streets may have the same gap (not yet surveyed).
411. **The Embarcadero's pier bulkheads faced the bay.** With `front: "street"`, a long pier picked whichever street
     was nearest, often the promenade along its flank, and the bulkhead (pilasters, sea-green doors, the number)
     ended up on the side or the far end. Every pier in piers.json now has a numeric front: the bearing from its
     shore end out along the pier (scratch pierfront.ts). Checked from the air: Piers 1, 5, 9, 15, 17, 23, 29–35,
     24–28, 38, 48 and the Fort Mason piers. **The convention, measured:** a numeric `front` puts the builder's
     local −z toward the bearing (the fit's own comment: "the model's front (local (0, −hz))"), so a builder that
     dresses +z needs bearing + 180.
412. **Grass between the Embarcadero sidewalk and the seawall/bulkheads.** OSM maps the wharf apron as nothing, so
     the base ground (grass) showed through. New region field `wharfAprons` (bake): paving on the bay side of the
     named street, segment by segment wherever water lies 40–55 m out, from the kerb to `reach` m. Drawn under the
     parks and the water. SF: The Embarcadero, reach 60. Takes effect on the next bake.
413. **Yerba Buena tunnel mouths** (BUGS.md #254 item 2, owner: "poorly rendered"): rebuilt from Commons "San
     Francisco-Bay Bridge02.jpg" (the west mouth). Weathered grey concrete instead of cream; three concentric rings,
     each 0.6 m forward of the one inside it, frame the upper roadway's arch; the headwall stands forward of them,
     cut into panels by vertical joints, with a coping; wide plain piers either side; a ledge across the face at
     the upper deck. The bore's own arch stays in the face plane, so the tube still meets it. Rendered after bake
     27: reads as the photo does. The same bake confirmed #412 (the Embarcadero apron is paved, no grass) and #410
     (Market's sidewalks are unchanged through the bake).
414. **Bank of California (400 California)** was a generic `chicago` box (historic-landmark census). It now has its own
     model (sf/bank-of-california.ts) from Commons "Bank of California - 400 California St (with annex).jpg": a
     grey granite temple with 6 giant fluted Corinthian columns on California and 8 on Sansome between massive
     corner piers, a recessed wall of tall windows, a step, and a deep entablature lettered THE BANK OF CALIFORNIA
     with an attic. Rendered from Sansome and California: reads as the photo. The census's other GENERIC rows are
     modelled under other ids (Fairmont, Palace, Columbus Tower, Cable Car Barn, Ghirardelli, Mills, Macang),
     except the New Mission Theater and St John's Presbyterian (next).
415. **New Mission Theater (2550 Mission)** was a generic brick box. It now has its own model (sf/new-mission.ts) from
     Mapillary on Mission Street: the plain auditorium, a red-brick front, and the tall brick PYLON square to the
     street with NEW / MISSION in lit cream letters down both faces, over the red marquee. Rendered from Mission
     looking south: matches the Mapillary frame.
416. **St John's Presbyterian (25 Lake St / Arguello)** was a generic Edwardian box. It is now built with the church kit
     (`stJohnsPresbyterian`) from two Commons photos: brown Gothic, a corner tower with a slate spire, a steep front
     gable with the portal and pointed windows, a flush transept and buttresses. Front 0 (Sacramento Street), checked
     from the corner. The kit's tower is slenderer than the real squat one under its wide spire, but it reads as
     this church. This finishes the historic-landmark census's GENERIC list.
417. **Performance audit, first pass** (owner: "any improvement makes the whole game better"). Measured with
     tools/perf.ts (headless, 1600×900 at 2×, unthrottled):
     - Downtown (Montgomery/Bush): 33 ms median, 7.4 M triangles.
     - Ferry Building: 26 ms.
     - Golden Gate Park: 24–26 ms, 9 M triangles.

     What the park's frame time is made of:
     - The frame is FILL-BOUND at 2×. With the crowd and trees off it is still 22 ms, against 10 ms at 1×.
     - At 1× the crowd and trees together cost about 10 ms.
     - Triangles there: trees (LOD1/LOD2 primitives) 4.7 M, the crowd 2.1 M (179 walkers, about 12 k each: body
       2.5 k, the plainest shoes 2 k, hair 2–5 k, the hooded jacket 7.7 k).

     Done: shoes are drawn only within 40 m (crowd-baked.ts `SHOES`, beside the eyes' `NEAR`), 270 k fewer
     triangles. Walkers at 45 m checked at 4× zoom: no difference. The frame-time gain is inside the ±3 ms noise.

     Levers left, larger:
     - Desktop's unlimited `maxDpr`: 1.5 saves 2–6 ms. That is a visible sharpness trade, the owner's call.
     - A cheaper far tree.
     - A far crowd body.
418. **Retaining-wall "boards" on junction islands** (Marin Headlands audit, rider-height samples along Bridgeway,
     Bunker, Conzelman and Alexander): at the Conzelman/McCullough junction a 2 m wall piece stood on the triangle
     island between two roads like a sign board. The wall test samples the ground 0.6 m out from the kerb, and that
     lands on the island's coarse ground, up to 3 m under the levelled roads either side (probe: island 91.3 m, the
     roads 91.3 and 94.7). A wall piece is now skipped when another street's corridor lies within 4 m further out
     (render/city.ts). Rendered: the island board is gone. The walls round the roundabout's centre (more than 4 m
     across, genuinely below the levelled ring) stay. New debug hook `window.__groundProbe(x, z)` → {grade, raw,
     drape}. The rest of the Headlands and Sausalito samples read fine.
419. **30 Otis Street** (26 storeys, 2023; the tall-building census's last generic tower over 70 m apart from the Sutro
     parts, which its own model draws) was a dark grey `bauhaus` box. It now has its own builder (sf/thirty-otis.ts)
     from Mapillary on South Van Ness: a close white grid, with a full-height blue glass slot down the middle of
     the east face. Rendered from South Van Ness: reads as the photo. Note: with `front: 90`, `edgeFacing(ring)`
     (the default, 180) picked the SOUTH face here, so the east face was found with `edgeFacing(ring, 90)` and
     confirmed by render. The local frame after a numeric front is not something to reason out; render it.
420. **Bay View Tower (2601 Mission at 22nd, 63 m)**: generic skyscraper box → `bayViewTower` (sf/thirty-otis.ts), from
     Mapillary on 22nd: dark grey finned office slab over a four-storey parking podium screened in black mesh.
     Aerial checked. Rest of the 45 m+ census: Cathedral Hill's apartment towers (fine as they are), the Azure,
     the Hotel Kabuki's tower, and parts of claimed outlines; none clearly wrong from the air.
421. **Library census**: every SFPL branch and campus library has an entry except the **Ortega Branch** (2011), which
     was a 5 m house. No photograph on Mapillary or Commons, so it is restyled from SF Public Works' description:
     one storey, pale and glazed, a green living roof. Checked from the air. **OWNER: a reference photo would make
     it a proper model.**
422. **Sausalito's Marinship as pink palazzi.** The town's `mediterranean` zone (owner: low stucco Spanish colonial)
     also covers the wartime shipyard on the north waterfront, so its sheds and warehouses came out as huge pink
     arcaded palaces under acres of red tile. The largest is the Bay Model Visitor Center, 26 000 m². Two changes:
     - btype.ts `sausalito-marinship`: a footprint over 2000 m² in that zone is a flat-roofed shed (retailBox).
       Takes effect at the next bake.
     - The Bay Model now has a grey restyle of its own (prominent.json). Checked from the air.
     Other museums, theatres and civic buildings without entries: the Railroad Ferry Museum and two small marine
     sanctuary offices (presidio style, which suits them), the Lost Church and the On Broadway Theater (small;
     fine).
423. **Treasure Island's Exposition palaces (Buildings 2 and 3, `tiHangar`)**, from HABS CA-2785-2 (the two palaces
     from across Clipper Cove): each long front is now a row of tall window bays in plain white stucco, with a stepped,
     fluted Moderne pylon at each end rising above the barrel roof. Before, it was a banded wall under the vault.
     Rendered from the cove: reads as the photograph.
424. **Bake 28** applied #422 (Marinship: every shed in Sausalito's industrial waterfront is now flat-roofed and
     plain, not a palazzo) and the Crissy Field planting zone (open meadow and dunes along the Golden Gate
     Promenade, no forest). Both checked from the air.
425. **Pixelated dark patches on Twin Peaks** (seen in an aerial after bake 27): last night's summit `cliffs` zones
     (Corona Heights, Twin Peaks north and south, Bernal crest). On these smooth inland slopes the cliffs renderer's
     steep-cell rule picks scattered 2 m cells, which read as a dark chequer on the grass, not as an outcrop.
     Removed the four zones; BUGS #260 notes why. Takes effect at the next bake.
426. **Notre Dame School** (Dolores at 16th, across from Mission Dolores; 1907) was a shopfronted `sfapartment` block.
     It now has its own builder (schools.ts `notreDameSchool`) from Mapillary on Dolores: a cream Second Empire block
     on a raised basement, a slate mansard with dormers, and the columned belvedere with its small grey dome.
     Rendered over the Dolores roofs beside the Basilica: reads.
427. **The Presidio Landmark (the 1932 Public Health Service Hospital)** was a roofless red-brick `facade()` box,
     seen floating as a pink block in the haze from the Richmond. Rebuilt in b4.ts from Commons "Presidio Landmark,
     San Francisco (former Public Health Service Hospital), 2017.jpg": seven storeys of buff brick, a stone ground
     floor, a cornice, a hipped red tile roof. Checked from Wedemeyer Street. (The "floating" was haze over its
     base at 1.5 km, not a height error: it stands on its ground.)
428. **b4 batch audit** (its facade() placeholders from the air): Mosser Towers (both) were red-brick and generic boxes.
     Mapillary on Jones/Eddy shows 1970s white-and-grey concrete towers with curved balconies, so both are now
     pale `towerblock` restyles. Checked from the air. The rest of b4 fits what it stands for: the Redstone and
     Bethlehem Steel buildings are red brick, the Storek a brick loft, the French American School, Gramercy and the
     Ballet pale modern blocks.
429. **Golden Gate Park Carousel** (Koret Children's Quarter, the 1941 WPA rotunda over the 1914 Herschell-Spillman)
     was a `midcentury-slab` recipe drawing a dark banded drum (found by the h2 batch aerial sheet). It now has its
     own builder (strawberry-hill.ts `goldenGateCarousel`) from Commons "Golden Gate Park carousel building 01.jpg": a
     cream colonnade of twelve fluted columns, dark glazed bays, a deep entablature, and a low grey ribbed dome on a
     stepped drum with a lantern. Rendered: matches the photograph. Batch sheets b1, h1 and h2 were checked; the
     rest read as the buildings they stand for.
430. **The Presidio Tide Gauge House stood alone on the water** (found by the h4 batch aerial sheet). It is at the end of
     the old Coast Guard pier off Crissy Field, which OSM does not map, so it floated in the bay off the beach. It now
     has its own builder (strawberry-hill.ts `tideGaugeHouse`, front 224, `deckAbove` 2.5), from NOAA Greater
     Farallones' "Pier & Tide Gauge House": a small white clapboard house with a red hipped roof on the pier head, and
     a 110 m timber deck on piles back to the beach by the sanctuary building. The Buoy Shack beside it (NRHP) stays
     as the bake draws it. Rendered from the beach: the pier lands on the sand. Batch sheets h3 and h4 checked;
     nothing else wrong.
431. **PG&E's Embarcadero Substation** (51 m, Fremont at Folsom) was a ten-storey office tower of small windows (a b3
     facade() placeholder). Mapillary on Fremont shows a windowless beige exposed-aggregate block fluted its whole
     height. It is now a blank finned concrete block on a recessed plinth (b3.ts). Rendered from Fremont: reads as the
     photos. Batch sheets b2 and b3 checked; the rest read as what they are.
432. **PG&E substations as flats with shopfronts.** Stations E, G, I, J and K and the Caltrans Sterling substation were
     `sfapartment` blocks with shops. A new classifier rule (btype.ts `power-substation`) makes anything tagged
     `power=substation|plant` a plain loft-style masonry block, and the bake's no-shops rule now covers the same
     tags. Takes effect at the next bake. (Larkin and Mission substations have their own entries; the Embarcadero
     one is #431.)
433. **The San Francisco Mint (155 Hermann, 1937)** stood at street level on a lawn as a generic bank temple with small
     windows. It now has its own builder (fountains.ts `sanFranciscoMint`) from Commons "NRIS 88000026 Mint Building -
     Duboce St Elevation.jpg": a 12 m rubble-stone wall and rock plinth, because the terrain's summit here is
     flattened (BUGS #260), and on it the white granite fortress with one tall storey of narrow window bays between
     piers, a high attic lettered UNITED STATES MINT, and a flagpole. Rendered from Duboce and from the air: reads as
     the photograph. The b5 and bank-temples placeholders were removed. Batch sheets b5 and b6 checked.
434. **Batch-sheet audit finished**: every procedural or restyled entry in b1–b12, h1–h4 and s1 shot from the air
     (scratch mansheet.sh; the camera sits off at the building's size and height). Fixed from it: #427 (Presidio
     Landmark), #428 (Mosser Towers), #429 (Golden Gate Park Carousel), #430 (Tide Gauge House), #431 (Embarcadero
     Substation), #433 (the Mint). The rest read as what they stand for. Mile Rocks Light (b11) is the right plain
     white caisson with its dark helipad deck.
435. **Bake 29** applied #425 (Twin Peaks' slopes are clean grass again, no dark chequer) and #432 (all eleven
     substations are brick with no shops; Station J checked from the air).
436. **More manifest aerial sheets**: churches-1/4/5 (no church faces away from its street; Saint Patrick's on
     Mission checked up close), hard-1, manual-1, oddities, scratch-1, civic-1, civic-6, model-1/2/3/opus and
     fidi-1–4. Nothing wrong. Downtown's density hides about half the fidi entries from any aerial.
437. **Sheets for waterfront-1/2, sights and parks.**
     - Point Bonita's ridge out to the lighthouse lay under the sea on the coarse ground (−86.7 m against the sea at
       −84.5). The Marin Headlands' fine-terrain zone stops at 37.82, 400 m north of it. New `point-bonita` zone
       (fine, raw relief, cliffs, bike trails), as at Yerba Buena's light station; takes effect at the next bake.
     - The Temple of Music is now warm Colusa sandstone instead of the park's pale grey stone.
     The rest were right: the Painted Ladies, the windmills, the Presidio rows, the Tea Garden.
