438. **Sheets for tierA-1–6 and tierB-1–3** (tierA-7–10 are empty): nothing wrong. Two that looked odd were the camera
     inside a neighbouring tower (Evangelical Free Church) and Oracle Park's brick back wall behind the 3rd Street
     substation.
439. **Bake 30**: Point Bonita now stands as a rock ridge about 31 m over the sea with the lighthouse on its tip and
     the suspension footbridge across the gap (#437's zone). Before, it was a brown sheet under the water. Still
     there: a thin green shelf at sea level round its foot, where the coarse coastline's land outline runs wider
     than the rock.
440. **Sheets for lowrise, prominent (76), museums and campuses**: nothing wrong.
441. **Sheets for the reface-haiku manifests (65 entries, model-made restyles) and market-east/west**: nothing wrong
     that could be seen. Downtown sheets are mostly cameras inside the next tower; a fixed-offset aerial is no
     tool for the Financial District. Those need street-level views (views.ts `audit` group) instead.
442. **Yerba Buena Lofts (855 Folsom, Saitowitz)** was a `brutalist` box. It now has its own builder
     (thirty-otis.ts `yerbaBuenaLofts`) from Mapillary on Folsom: an exposed concrete grid of slabs and columns, each
     cell either a frosted glass panel, a frosted glass box standing proud, or an open dark balcony, over a glazed
     base. Aerial checked. Next in the unique-modern census: 8 Octavia.
443. **8 Octavia (Saitowitz)** was a `bauhaus` box. It now has its own builder (thirty-otis.ts `eightOctavia`) from
     Mapillary on Octavia: blue-silver aluminium bays, about half of them screens of thin vertical louvres, banded by
     floor, over a glazed ground floor. Aerial checked.
444. **Full `audit` view group re-run (60 views, after bakes 27–30)**: nothing has regressed. Audit 19 ("Bay Bridge
     west span") was aimed at water south of the span and is re-aimed (views json). The bridge was always there.
445. **A 16 m wall across 22nd Street under I-280** (Dogpatch rider-height sweep). 22nd Street crosses the Caltrain
     cut on a short bridge, and the I-280 viaduct passes 16 m above it. The bridge outline's structure
     (render/bridge-structures.ts) takes its deck height from the median of the raised ways' heights in each 1 m
     bin. Where the freeway crosses, that median of two picked the freeway, so the structure rose to it: a dark slab
     filling the road from deck to viaduct. Freeway ways no longer feed a bridge outline's heights (they draw their
     own decks). Rendered from the street and from the air: the road runs clear under the viaduct. This is
     city-wide, so any other overpass under a freeway is fixed the same way.
446. **Freeway bridge heights, two bake bugs found by a rider-height sweep under every freeway (129 crossings).**
     - **Junctions in the air**: a deck chain eased to the GROUND at its ends, including where three freeway decks
       meet in mid-air. The Central Freeway's westbound deck and two ramps came down to the street at their shared
       node, in the middle of Bryant at Division, and the deck's fascia stood across Bryant as a wall.
       tools/bridges.ts now gives a node where only freeway decks meet (three or more) the highest top of those
       chains, and they end at it. Bryant runs clear under the freeway.
     - **Crossing footways**: the #217 rule lifts a raised footway onto a roadway within 25 m along 60% of its
       length. Mariposa's 43 m sidewalk passes under I-280 and was lifted 15 m onto the freeway. The roadway must
       now run within 30° of parallel to the footway. 18 → 16 adoptions. The Golden Gate sidewalks are still on
       the deck (−17.3 and −16.6 against −16.7), and Mariposa's sidewalk is back at −74.5.
     Air-paths after bake 32: bike 4 / 0.09 km (unchanged), run 14 / 0.20 km (was 15). Both bugs have tests.
     Also seen in this sweep: the misplaced Bay Bridge anchorage blocks Main Street (BUGS #261, the owner's call).
447. **Golden Gate Bridge: the lamp standards stood in the middle of the sidewalks** (a rider-height ride along both
     sidewalks, cameras at deck + 2 m via scratch rnddeck.mjs). They were at DECK_HALF − 1.2, which is the sidewalk
     way's own line, so the rider rode through a 9 m post every 61 m. They are now on the roadway kerb (DECK_HALF
     − 3.2), with the walk between them and the rail, as on the real bridge. The rest of the ride reads well: the
     deck, the rail, the suspenders, the towers ahead, the south pylon's legs either side.
448. **Deck-height ride checks**: Bay Bridge Bikeway (helix, rails, deck), Macalla Road and Treasure Island Road read
     well. Noted for the owner, not changed: **Alexander Avenue in Marin climbs 26% over 25 m** at local
     (−6380, 4760) → (−6400, 4745), and the raw terrain agrees (1.3 → 7.8 m). The real road is about 8%, so the
     elevation data likely carries the US-101 structures there. It is terrain work in the same family as #260.
449. **Raised walks at deck height** (48 of the 104 raised footways downtown and SoMa; cameras at deck + 2 m): Salesforce
     Park, the Embarcadero Center podium walks, the Pier 1–5 promenade and the Yerba Buena Gardens bridges read well.
     Three Moscone walks (ways 63688, 63700, 64807) run into the convention halls' walls. They are only in the RUN
     graph (running is off), so they are left alone and noted here. Way 68900 ends at a tower's glass wall like
     the real skybridges.
450. **Ride-graph samples**: 96 random points drawn from the BIKE ride graph itself, each shot at the ridden height + 2 m
     facing along the way (scratch rndride.ts + rnddeck.mjs), so bridges, decks and trenches are seen as ridden.
     Nothing broken. Also sampled from the air: a third of the 74 `brutalist` buildings (Mission Bay and SoMa
     institutional blocks; plausible, if drab).
451. **Pier 35's bulkhead stood across the Embarcadero roadway** (deck-height ride along the Embarcadero). OSM's outline
     includes a shore wing lying over the northbound lane, and the model, fitted to the whole outline, put its
     bulkhead on the road. It is now placed by the shed alone (centre, 55 × 319 m along 356°, front 176), still
     claiming the outline. The Embarcadero is clear, and the SS Jeremiah O'Brien lies alongside (OSM has it at
     Pier 35 now; the ships.json note said Pier 45). The rest of the Embarcadero ride reads well; the Fort Mason
     Shed A flank (panel 11) is a real long white shed.
452. **Streets drawn as grass outside the detail box** (ride-graph samples; 33rd Avenue at Ortega, south of the box).
     A tile draws the edges whose FIRST point lies in it, whole. Outside the detail box the bake does not split
     ways at their junctions: 324 ground ways run over 400 m (240 km in all, the longest 3.5 km), and 33rd Avenue
     is a single 1.3 km edge starting at the box edge. So the street under a rider was drawn only while the tile it
     started in was within view; five tiles on, the rider rode on the grass base. 164 km of the bike ride graph is
     out there. Now render/city.ts `splitLong` cuts every long ground edge at load into pieces of about 120 m that
     share their cut points, each bucketed in its own tile. The joins are two-armed synthetic nodes (negative ids),
     so they get no junction hull and no cutback. Raised edges are left whole. 24 samples inside and outside the
     box: every road drawn. Also a debug hook, `window.__city`.
453. **For the owner**: with #452 fixed, the ride graph outside the detail box is drawn, but much of it (West Oakland's
     7th Street and West Maritime, the Mill Valley–Sausalito path, the Port of Oakland roads) runs through empty
     flat green land. cities.ts says those corners are "in the box only as roads and shoreline". Whether to keep
     them in the ride graph is a design call.
454. **An apartment tower beside the Palace of Fine Arts** (historic/attraction tag census): two 21 m `building=roof`
     ways at the exhibition hall's east end were drawn as a brown tenement tower, and way/1550664401 showed a
     fire-escape apartment through the hall's east wall. Claimed by a placeholder entry (prominent.json), as the
     St Ignatius parts are. Checked from the air. The rest of the census reads as what it is: China Cabin, the
     Rothschild House, Presidio Building 201 and others.
455. **Canopies drawn as houses**: the bake already drops `building=roof` on raised layers, but 42 at ground level
     (petrol-station canopies, shelters, 3–6 m; the two at the Palace of Fine Arts were 21 m) came out as walled
     Victorian houses. tools/bake.ts now gives a ground `building=roof` minHeight = height − 0.5: a roof slab, no
     walls under it. Takes effect at the next bake.
456. **Building-tag census** (every `building=` value in the package): the canopy rule (#455) now covers carport (17),
     shelter and gazebo too. `building=no` (OSM's "this is not a building"; an outdoor stage at 750 Howard) is no
     longer baked as a block. Both take effect at the next bake. Left as they are: ruins (11), grandstand (5),
     tent (4), construction (6) and pavilion (5). Each draws a box that is about right in size.
457. **Bake 33**: the first attempt was stopped by the bake's own manifest audit (5 findings against a baseline of 4).
     The `building=no` exclusion (#456) had removed the Yerba Buena Gardens Esplanade stage, which the
     `esplanadeMainStage` model claims. Ways a landmark entry claims are now exempt, and the re-run went through:
     all 42 ground `building=roof` ways (with carports, shelters and gazebos) now carry a raised half-metre slab.
     Checked on UCSF's Parnassus plaza.
458. **Trail samples** (48 random points on the bike graph's footways and paths, at the ridden height): park trails,
     the Lands End and Sutro Heights paths, the Embarcadero promenades and the downtown plazas all draw. One
     looked missing (the Coastal Trail at Merrie Way); the ride graph had moved its end 7 m to the merged junction
     node, and the camera stood in that gap. 846 of 3787 matched edges have an end moved more than 3 m by the
     ride graph's junction merging, by design.
459. **Ride samples round eight landmarks** (City Hall, the Ferry Building, Coit Tower, the Palace of Fine Arts,
     Mission Dolores, the Castro, Union Square, Alamo Square; 48 frames on the bike graph within 200 m, at the ridden
     height): all read well. The odd-looking frames were the camera facing sideways off a hillside street at its
     retaining wall (States Street; along the street it is right).
460. **"Trees sometimes disappear when the rider gets close" (owner), FOUND.** Not the LOD. A landmark's footprint clears
     the trees inside it, and that runs as each tile arrives, so near the rider. A standalone entry's footprint is its
     `size` rectangle, and the Golden Gate Bridge's is 130 × 2650 m and the Bay Bridge's 60 × 2890 m. The Presidio's
     trees round Fort Point and the toll-plaza approach, and Rincon Hill's under the Bay Bridge, vanished as the rider
     came close. Measured at the Golden Gate's south approach: 0 tree instances within 80 m before, 122 after.
     Bridges, the Richardson Bay moorings field and Sutro Tower no longer clear their rectangles
     (render/landmarks.ts).
461. **Floodlit landmarks at night** (a night pass over six audit views): City Hall, the Ferry Building, the Asian Art
     Museum and both bridges were near-black shapes against the night sky, lit only by the moon; the real ones are
     floodlit. The cartoon shader (render/cartoon-material.ts) now adds, on the landmark, bridge-structure and
     skyline materials (new uniform `flood`), a warm wash on vertical faces after dark, stronger on the faces
     towards the rider. Roofs and ground stay dark. The Ferry Building's tower and arcade at street level read as
     floodlit without being overblown, and the Golden Gate's towers glow red across the water. New `night` view
     group (six views, `query: time=night`).
     (Dusk and dawn checked at the Ferry Building and on Larkin: the flood term at night 0.55 and 0.25 adds a warm
     evening light, not a glare.)
462. **Noted, not changed**: a block of the `brick` style can read PHARMACY three or four times in a row (Kansas
     Street, SoMa). Each style's shopfront has only two painted variants (tools/facadegen/paint.py), and the
     grammar picks one per bay from the bay's seed, so neighbours repeat. Giving the variants distinct words does
     not stop that. The real fix is more ground variants (an atlas layout change, a third or fourth cell per
     style), which is a facade-generator job for later.
463. **The Hearts in San Francisco, first look in the renderer** (#211 had added ten without seeing them): every heart's
     front and back showed as a black hole. The loft ran to a point at both faces, so the last rings were slivers,
     and the outward-normal fix flipped only the normal, not the winding, leaving the side quads culled from outside.
     sf/art.ts `paintedHeart` now flips the winding with the normal, stops the loft at ±0.9 of its depth, and caps
     each face with a domed fan. The two Union Square hearts stood on the street-level ground inside the square's
     raised terrace, showing only their tops over its wall; they now have a `baseY` on the terrace (−57.72 and
     −63.16, picked from the model). Rendered: the bronze Big Heart on the Rock and both painted Union Square
     hearts stand whole on their pedestals.
464. **Butterflight (Union Square's third heart)** stood half-sunk in the terrace's corner wall by the public toilet. It
     is moved 6 m onto the corner paving by the café, at street level. All 18 art.json pieces were shot up close
     (scratch rndart.mjs). The rest stand right: Vaillancourt, Cupid's Span, Language of the Birds, the Francis
     Scott Key canopy, the Stevenson memorial and every other heart.
465. **Every small standalone model shot up close** (71 lat/lon entries outside art.json: lighthouses, public toilets,
     monuments, turntables, labyrinths, BART plazas, canopies, skybridges). All stand where they should. The odd
     frames were the camera inside a neighbour, or on the water by Lime Point's rock spur, which really is there.
466. **Every generic statue read as a dark spike** (`figureOnPedestal`: Lincoln, Franklin, Garfield, Halleck, Sun
     Yat-sen and the others). The figure was a tapering column on a tall pedestal. monuments.ts `figure` is now a
     standing bronze man: legs under a long coat, chest, shoulders, arms at his sides, neck and head, still about
     80 triangles. The pedestal is a little over half the height, with the figure at least 2.6 m (monumental bronzes
     are larger than life). Rendered at Lincoln by City Hall.
467. **Balmy and Clarion mural alleys, first look**: the painted panels stood as free boards 1.6 m from the centre
     line, so one row stood on the alley's carriageway and every panel read as a billboard on legs. The shapes
     painted on them were boxes standing off the back face, invisible from the alley. mural-alleys.ts now lays the
     murals on the alley's wall line, 4.2 m out, as 0.3 m slabs from below the ground to garage height. The shapes
     are larger patches on the face towards the alley. Rendered down both alleys.
468. **Standalone models from the nearest ridden point** (80 entries, each shot at the ride graph's height from the
     nearest bike-graph point 8–60 m away, facing it): nothing further broken. Hyde Street's turnaround, Lotta's
     Fountain, the Yagura, the Dragon Gate, Lombard's gardens and the Sutro Baths read as they should from where a
     rider sees them.
469. **Perf re-check after the night's changes**: frame times read 30–50% higher than at 03:00, but the machine is
     loaded by background services (Spotlight, media analysis and Time Machine, load average about 5.5).
     Alternating A/B runs at the same moment show the night flood term costs nothing (45.7 vs 45.3 ms, then 54.7
     vs 53.4). Triangle counts are as before. Re-measure on an idle machine before reading anything into the
     absolute numbers.
470. **Hospitals and universities census** (every `building=hospital` / `building=university` way the manifests do not
     claim, shot from the air; Mapillary and Commons for the doubtful ones). The hospitals read as what they are:
     plain institutional blocks (SF General's old brick wards are already models). Two USF buildings were wrong:
     **1860 Hayes Street**, tagged `building:material=brick`, was a beige block because the institution rule
     outranks the material (the only such case in the city), and is now red-brown brick with cream trim. **Malloy
     Hall** is now white render, from its Commons photo. Noted for the owner: UCSF Mission Bay's **1651 4th
     Street** (the Weill neurosciences building, 80 × 78 m) is a 12 m beige box; Mapillary's frames there show
     only a garage screen (resolved in #473).
471. **Museums census** (`tourism=museum` / `building=museum`): every one is a model or a checked restyle but the
     **Railroad & Ferry Depot Museum in Tiburon**, the 1884 station house, which was a 12 m SF flat with a row of red
     doors. New model sf/tiburon-depot.ts from the Commons photo: two storeys of pale grey board-and-batten, the
     brown-grey belt course and sash frames, a pale green corrugated gable along its length. Rendered from the water
     side and from the land.
472. **Tiburon and Belvedere were San Francisco** (seen round the depot, #471): 691 of the 1015 buildings on the
     peninsula were SF flats (`sfapartment`), 212 Victorians, the waterfront a row of fire-escaped tenements. New zone
     `tiburon-belvedere` (data/zones, `buildings: 'marin'`; east edge kept west of Angel Island's own zone) and a
     btype rule: pastel stucco houses for most, plain modern ones for a third, a footprint past 2000 m² a flat
     store. Bake 34 (9206 tiles, audit at baseline): 656 stucco houses, 352 modern. Rendered over Belvedere and
     Tiburon's waterfront. Heights are left to the region's level inference, as in Sausalito.
473. **UCSF Weill Neurosciences Building** (#470's open item): found the architect's photograph (cavagnero.com) after
     all. New model sf/weill.ts: a four-storey block wrapped in a silver fin screen, lifted over a set-back glass
     base on slender white columns, 31 m. Was a 12 m beige box. Rendered from the air over Mission Bay.
474. **Golden Gate Park ride samples** (32 points on the bike graph, east and west halves, at the ridden height).
     Most read well: the drives, the meadows, Stow Lake, the Sunset edge. One fix: **JFK Drive by 36th Avenue ran
     between a 0.9 m stone parapet**, as if on a viaduct. The terrain dips 1.5 m in the meadow beside it, and a
     side whose worst drop passes 1.5 m gets the tall parapet along its whole length. A road in a park
     (`e.park`) now gets no parapet unless the drop passes 3 m, and its face is turf, not stone, so the drive
     sits on a grassy bank (render/city.ts). Not a bug: a brown face up Strawberry Hill was the forest floor
     rising to the summit.
475. **Presidio ride samples** (16 points): the barracks rows, Fort Scott, the Main Post and the drives read as
     the Presidio. Pershing Drive's low dark banks are #474's turf faces over real 1–3 m drops between the
     levelled road and the coarse terrain. **Noted, not changed:** roads in the Presidio's housing areas
     (Lendrum Court at Fort Scott, Hitchcock Street) are not inside a park polygon, so they keep the city's stone
     retaining walls and 0.9 m parapets where the terrain falls 1.5–2.5 m below the levelled road. The real
     roads there sit at grade. The cause is the terrain's coarseness, so the proper fix is grading those
     roads into the terrain (the #260 family), not more wall rules.
476. **Mission Bay ride samples** (16 points): the campus blocks, 3rd Street's light rail, the creek parks and
     the Chase Center edge read well. One fix: **the 4th Street bridge over Mission Creek had cream slabs across
     its lanes.** OSM maps it as two raised ways, one each direction, that converge at both ends. Each deck drew
     its own pavements 15 cm proud, and each inner pavement ran on across the other's roadway, then filled the
     narrowing median as a cream triangle. A deck's pavement (render/bridges.ts `deck`) is now drawn only in
     runs where neither its middle nor a point 2.5 m beyond it lies on another raised road's carriageway
     (render/city.ts `carriageOf`). `openRuns` now shares the new `sampledRuns`. Rendered from the air and at
     the rider's height: two carriageways and a median.
477. **Fountain census** (the open "fountains" queue item; all 87 `amenity=fountain` features in the cut). The
     bake's furniture only takes `amenity=drinking_water`, so a decorative fountain is drawn only where a
     landmark models it: Vaillancourt, Lotta's, both Ruth Asawa fountains, Yoda, UN Plaza. The biggest one missing
     was **Levi's Plaza** (Halprin, 1982), whose hard plaza was bare paving. New model fountains.ts
     `leviPlazaFountain` (sights.json, from the Commons panorama): a broad pool in a concrete coping, brown
     granite weirs with sheets of water, a stepped granite pyramid with a stair and a jet, stepping slabs and a
     clipped planter block. It needs a `baseY`, because the plaza's paving (picked at −80.15 to −80.35) stood over
     a pool built from the footprint's lowest ground. **The five Levi's Plaza office blocks** are now red-brown
     brick with ribbon windows (Mapillary on Battery and Filbert); they were beige. Still unmodelled, noted:
     the Rideout Fountain in Golden Gate Park, Dandelion Fountain at Embarcadero Center, Ruth Asawa's
     *Aurora* on the Embarcadero, the Fillmore Center fountain and Levi's Plaza's soft-side stream. (Ned Kahn's
     Bus Fountain is already in the Salesforce Park model, transbay.ts; Rideout and *Aurora* are #478–#479.)
478. **The Rideout Fountain** (Music Concourse, from #477's list): the concourse model's three pools each held a
     grey spike for a jet, which read as a pole. Each now has a stone pedestal and a bronze bowl. The middle
     one, the Rideout (Commons photo), has the pale stone figure wrestling a swan whose neck arches over its head,
     and a ring of 28 jets arching in from a pipe ring onto the bowl, each a five-piece parabola. Straight
     two-strut jets were tried first and read as a spider's legs. The side ones get a short plume. Rendered from
     the concourse walk.
479. **Ruth Asawa's *Aurora*** (Embarcadero at Howard, from #477's list) was not drawn. New model fountains.ts
     `auroraFountain` (sights.json), from the Wikidata image: a ring 4.5 m high on its edge, folded from
     stainless steel. Each station round the ring is a diamond cross-section, alternately deep and shallow, so
     the skin between them folds into sharp facets. It stands in a round basin tiled deep blue, turned
     (`rotateDeg` 60) so a rider on the city side looks through it at the Bay Bridge, as in the photograph.
480. **For the owner: the Dandelion Fountain.** OSM maps a 14 m round `amenity=fountain` "Dandelion Fountain"
     (artist Bob Woodward, way/1177704967, layer 1) on the Embarcadero Center podium at 37.79567 −122.39890.
     Wikipedia puts Woodward's "Peacock" or "dandelion" fountain on Maritime Plaza (Battery and Clay). I found no
     photograph of either, and Mapillary does not reach the podium, so I have not modelled it. A photo would settle
     both where it is and what it looks like (presumably Woodward's sphere of radiating spray tubes on a stem).
481. **Golden Gate Park's trails and car-free drives were not drawn at all** (GGP view G2F, then a census of the
     loaded edges). A foot way draws only in the corridor layer, and the park rules (#201) took the corridor
     layer out of the park, so every foot way in a park drew nothing. Within 600 m of the de Young that is 268
     footways, 59 paths and 48 `highway=pedestrian` ways: the **John F. Kennedy Promenade** (12 m, the car-free
     JFK Drive), Music Concourse Drive, Overlook Drive, Hagiwara Tea Garden Drive and 8th Avenue in the park.
     They were lawn, with their zebra crossings painted on the grass, and the rider rode across it. A park's
     foot way (not its steps, which draw themselves) is now drawn at its own width with no slop, on its own
     surface page (asphalt, dirt, gravel, sand, boards, setts; render/city.ts `TRAIL_PAGE`). Each page is its
     own mesh (`trail:<page>:<tile>`) in the corridor's group, so a drive that crosses a trail paints over it.
     From the air the park now has its network of asphalt paths and brown dirt trails, and the Promenade reads as
     the broad road it is.
482. **#474's park banks now slope.** The vertical turf face stood across the meadow like a clipped hedge (G2F,
     Transverse Drive). A park road's bank now runs from the road's edge out to the ground at about 1 in 2, its
     foot on the raw ground where it lands, in a darker turf so that a face catching the sky does not glow lime.
     **Crossover Drive's approaches to its bridge over Transverse Drive** stood in the meadow as dark stone walls.
     A raised park road on an embankment now gets the same sloped turf bank (render/bridges.ts `bank`); a city
     ramp keeps its retaining walls, and a bridge abutment keeps its stone face. Checked from the air: the
     Presidio golf course's cart paths and Buena Vista's trails now draw too.
483. **Checks after #481–#482**: all 44 bridge views (no change from the deck-pavement rule, #476), Twin Peaks and
     Corona Heights ride samples (16), Lands End and Sutro Heights (16), and the landmark view group (53). One
     view was re-aimed: St Ignatius's close-up had its camera in a courtyard south of Fulton. Glen Canyon and
     Mount Davidson are outside the detail box. **#244 re-measured** on bake 34 (900 s soak): the same 6
     vehicles stuck in one pocket round a 5 m street (edge 75103), as at #356. In the game the release valve
     retires them once out of the rider's view. Left for the owner's #244 work.
484. **The Botanical Garden after #481**: its web of paths drew as a maze of broad dark bands. They are mostly
     tagged asphalt in OSM, as across GGP (1 033 asphalt footways, 556 concrete, 339 untagged, 110 dirt). Park
     footways and paths are now drawn at no more than 2.2 m (the bake's default is 3 m), and their asphalt is
     tinted sun-bleached grey, paler than a drive's. Car-free roads (`pedestrian`) keep their width and the
     road's depth. Also mapped: woodchips and unpaved to dirt, paved to asphalt. The Conservatory of Flowers and
     the Rose Garden read well from the air.
485. **Alcatraz: a lawn floating in a cove under the cliff rock** (random ride sample, the Agave Trail). The base
     ground is the only ground drawn where no area covers it, and it was 4 × 4 quads a tile (50 m cells) draped
     only at their corners. Over the cove's concave ground the chord stood 5 m up (−68.5 against −73.5 raw), so
     the rock hung over a green sheet. A base cell now splits into 4 × 4 or 8 × 8 where the raw ground strays more
     than 0.8 or 2.5 m from the cell's plane (render/city.ts). Cost on Twin Peaks, the hilliest view: 27 600
     ground triangles against the scene's 21 million. The cliff layer now samples the raw ground, as the grass
     under it is draped (it took the road grade near the island's switchbacks). Left: the cove lies inside OSM's
     sea polygon but 11 m above sea level in the elevation model, so it is lawn rather than rock or water (#260
     family).
486. **Views after #485**: the hills (39) and audit (60) groups. No regressions from the base-ground or trail
     changes. Noted, not changed: from high over Twin Peaks (audit 46, 47) the back yards of Midtown Terrace's
     far blocks show as white, sky-coloured gaps. The same render with the base subdivision turned off is
     identical, so it predates today. Near tiles are green. Investigated, not solved. It shows from the Twin Peaks summit at rider
     height too. Facts: up close the same ground is green, so the white only appears with distance. Hiding the
     sea plane changes nothing. A coarse ground over the whole terrain grid outside the fine distant mesh (tried
     and reverted: 256 000 triangles, no visible change) did not fill it either. So it is not a missing
     mesh beyond the bounds. The likeliest remaining cause is that the ground fogs to white much sooner than the
     fringe buildings beside it read, or that the far ground is depth-hidden by an occluder chunk. For the owner:
     a look with fog off (photo.ts `fogIsOff`) would settle which. Hill c (Sausalito, Marion Avenue) is a hillside street on a real
     retaining wall; the camera stood below it.
487. **FOUND: the sky painted over all ground further than about 540 m** (the white back yards of #486, which
     was wrong about the cause). A ray through a white patch hits the `sky` box 540 m out. The dome
     (render/sky.ts) is a box of radius ~520 round the camera in rendering group 0, writing no depth but still
     testing it. The far ground (`distantGround`, the base) and the sea are in group 0 too, and nothing ordered
     the group. Wherever the dome drew after them, its faces, nearer than any ground beyond them, painted the
     sky over it. Buildings are in a later group, so they stood clear, and that is why the gaps showed between
     the rows. The dome is now drawn FIRST in group 0 (`setRenderingOrder`). From Twin Peaks the far
     neighbourhoods now sit on green hills with San Bruno Mountain behind them. From Crissy Field (audit 11) the
     bay shows in front of the bridge where there was a pale band. Ocean Beach, the Bay Bridge and the night
     views are unchanged. Tried first and reverted: a coarse ground over the rest of the terrain grid (no change,
     256 000 triangles).
488. **Blown-out white paving at rider height** (nonbuilding view group: Union Square's hearts, the Ferry Plaza
     behind the Ferry Building). The landmark material lights an up face with sky and full sun together (about
     1.8×), so a paving grey past about 0.55 clips to pure white, and Union Square's lawn to lime. Not new today
     (the same with #487 reverted). Union Square's granite (union-square.ts: PALE 0.82 → 0.6, MID 0.63 → 0.48,
     DARK 0.44 → 0.36, LAWN darker) and the Ferry Plaza deck's bands (marina.ts) are set darker, and now read as
     light granite and pale concrete with their banding showing. The other 46 nonbuilding views read right.
     Noted: any other model with pale up faces will do the same. A shader-wide fix (soft-clipping up faces in
     cartoon-material.ts) would touch every landmark, so it is the owner's call.

### Open questions — parked for later (owner, 2026-10-05: "record those open questions for later, they are not too important")
- Pale up faces clipping to white in the landmark shader (#488): shader-wide fix or per model?
- The Dandelion Fountain: where it is, and a photo (#480).
- Presidio housing streets on stone retaining walls: grade the roads into the terrain (#475, #260 family).
- #244: the 6 vehicles still stuck in one pocket round edge 75103.
- The blocky white streaks on the far sea horizon at Ocean Beach (seen while checking #487; predates it).

489. **The San Francisco Federal Building (Nancy Pelosi Federal Building, 90 7th Street), a custom model** (owner,
     2026-10-05, with six photographs: "one of the strangest buildings in the city … on the entry path"). It was
     a tower-grammar slab with a scooped crest. New sf/federal-building.ts. It is built in the slab's own frame,
     measured from OSM: the slab backs onto Stevenson, its long face looks across the plaza to Mission, and its
     north-east end is on 7th. The entry claims the slab, the annex and the café (98 7th Street, which the bake
     had drawn as a 12 m flat standing on the plaza) and is placed by lat/lon, turned −45°. What it has:
     - the mesh skin (warm silver panels) with irregular dark window slots;
     - the roofline rising towards 7th, carried up as an open crest on a truss, then bent over the 7th Street end
       into a cantilever with a glazed box under it;
     - the "pizza slice": a dark fold from the roof down to the three-storey sky-garden opening (cut 5 m into the
       body, with a balcony slab), the panel beside it standing proud;
     - the pale panel with four boxed square windows;
     - the back: a glass wall behind a grid of catwalks and fins, a darker bay, and an open steel truss along
       the roof (the owner's photo from Market);
     - teal glass on the south-west end, a sloped skirt along the base;
     - on 7th, the glass wall against the sidewalk with the angular concrete column jutting out over it;
     - the zigzag canopy, folded planes on a truss over the café, with a concrete plinth wall round the corner;
     - the glass-fronted annex on the plaza.
     Rendered from the 7th and Mission corner, from Market, from Mission and from the air.
490. **Muni buses** (owner: "SF city buses are always silver-gray with a red stripe at the top of the chassis and
     another at the bottom"). Buses were solid red or the traffic sim's random car colour. carkit's `transit`
     livery is now silver-grey (spec.ts), and its bus body (geometry/body.ts) carries two red bands: one along
     the top of the sides under the roof, one along the bottom over the sill. Both run round the back, and the
     bottom one also crosses the front. On the kerb side they break for the doors, and the bottom band breaks for
     the wheel arches. render/street-fleet.ts no longer overwrites the bus's paint with the sim's colour.
     Owner, on the first versions: the bands read as panels and stuck out past the body at the back. They were boxes
     on the body. Rebuilt from Commons photos of New Flyer XDE40s ("XDE40 at Woods Division, July 2017.JPG"):
     the livery is PAINTED in carkit's shader (render/material.ts) by height on the body's side and back faces.
     A broad red band runs from the window heads to under the roof (so the windows are shortened as on the real
     bus), silver above it to the roof, and a red skirt from the sill to under the windows. The car carries
     the body's height in `paint2.a` (render/fleet.ts `bands`). No geometry, so nothing stands proud. **The carkit edits are not committed** (by
     the standing rule).
491. **A map jump with a delivery offer open left the offer stuck on screen** (owner). The jump forfeits the job in
     hand or on offer (delivery.ts `forfeit`), but the forfeit handler in game.ts never took the offer card down.
     Its job was gone, so Take and Skip did nothing and it never timed out. The forfeit now hides the offer card
     and ends the phone's offer too. Checked headless: offer forced (`nextOfferAt = 0`), the card up, `__jump`, the
     card gone and the delivery idle.
492. **"Z-fighting on DO NOT ENTER signs"** (owner). The game draws no DO NOT ENTER sign. These were the STOP signs
     (us-furniture.ts `stopSign`): a red octagon with STOP drawn as one solid white bar is, from a few metres
     off, a DO NOT ENTER sign. Its border, face and bar stood 5 mm apart, so they z-fought from across a
     junction. The face now stands 2 cm off the border and the back plate faces backwards. STOP is spelt in
     block letters built from strokes, mirrored so they read from the traffic side. Rendered face-on and edge-on.
493. **Two crosswalks on top of each other** (owner). A zebra is painted for each OSM crossing way, squared to the road
     it crosses. A census of the 1 096 zebras within about 1 km of downtown found 82 overlapping pairs. Two kinds:
     - a crossing split at the centre node with a piece in each of two tiles, so each tile painted the whole zebra;
       it is now painted only by the tile its middle is in;
     - two different crossing ways, mapped side by side or at a slight skew, painting a doubled or crossed-over
       zebra (Market at 10th, 1117,−210). Within a tile, a zebra whose middle is within 3.5 m of one already
       painted, or which lies within 2 m of one and within 45° of parallel, is now left out.
     Rendered before and after at both sites.
494. **Street-name blades out in the road** (owner). The pole stood along the bisector of the two streets, at a reach
     set by the wider street's width. That is the corner only where two equal streets cross square. The junctions
     are also the ride graph's, which merges divided roads and clusters of nodes, so their point and widths are
     not the drawn streets'. render/signs.ts `blades` now puts the pole where the two kerb lines meet (each
     street's half-width plus 0.6 m of pavement), then steps it out along the corner until it is clear of every
     drawn carriageway in the tile (city/carriageways.ts). A corner that never clears within 12 m gets no sign.
     At Pine and Powell the pole stood 3 m into the side street; it is now on the corner by the signal.
     Spot-checked with a 12-point rider-height sample.
495. **Huntington Falls rebuilt** (owner: "it looks really bad … serious rendering glitches where the renderer can't
     decide where the ground is … the real site is quite beautiful"). The ground glitches were the landmark's
     footprint SHADOW: an 80 × 20 m box shadow draped at its corners across Strawberry Hill's slope, lying as dark
     planes through the hillside. A manifest entry can now say `"shadow": false` (render/landmarks.ts), and the
     falls do. The terrain itself is right. It falls a steady 45% here (30 m to 3 m over 60 m, measured), and the
     zoom-15 data does not have the built cliff, so the model builds its own (strawberry-hill.ts
     `huntingtonFalls`):
     - three terraces of piled angular rock in weathered tan, brown and mossy grey (no surface tag: the granite
       library drew a masonry grid, and the old tall tagged prisms read as stone towers), each standing out of
       the slope to a sheer, craggy face;
     - a clear slot down each face with rock sides, a dark cleft and white ribbons of water into a churned pool;
     - pools along the ledges, ferns and shrubs in the cracks;
     - the two wooden footbridges with X-braced rails across the upper falls;
     - a stone-rimmed pool at the lake's edge.
     Rendered from the lake path and the air. Still blockier than the real rockwork: real boulders would want a
     rock generator (the trailtrainer `rockgen` library).
496. **Golden Gate Park's famous buildings** (owner's list). Each shot from the air and up close, then rebuilt from a
     photograph where it was wrong, in a new file sf/ggp-buildings.ts:
     - **Conservatory of Flowers**: it was an opaque white mass (the owner: "does not appear"). Now a white-framed
       glasshouse: the tall central octagon of glass under a ribbed glass dome, a cupola and finial, the glass
       entrance porch up broad steps, two long wings with arched glass roofs on white ribs, and a small domed
       pavilion at each end. `front: 180`, so the porch faces the path from JFK Drive.
     - **Pioneer Log Cabin**: it was a flat-roofed box. Now round logs crossing at the corners, a steep brown
       shingle gable, log gable ends, a porch on log posts, shuttered windows.
     - **Anglers Lodge**: it was a pale modern block. Now dark red-brown boards, a shingle hip roof on the long
       wing, the gabled wing with chevron boarding and two plank doors, and the rough stone entrance with its
       arched door and a stone chimney.
     - **County Fair Building** (Hall of Flowers): it was the park zone's colonial style with red tile hips. Now
       long low brick walls with a dark clerestory under a thin white roof slab oversailing them all round
       (Mapillary on 9th Avenue).
     - **McLaren Lodge** and the **Beach Chalet** already read as themselves (sandstone and red tile; cream
       stucco under a red hip).
     - **California Kahve** (300 Bowling Green Drive, the kiosk by the carousel, way/287951728): found in the end
       on lotsafunmaps' page for the Koret playground. New `kahveKiosk`: white textured stucco, round-arched
       windows in dark green frames with fanlights, the service window over its counter with the menu boards
       and a round wooden sign, a red tile hip roof, planters. It was a generic stucco house.
     The old builders (parks.ts `conservatoryOfFlowers`, h2 `anglersLodge`, s1's cabin) are removed. The
     nonbuildings audit is clean for all four.
497. **Landmark name plates unreadable** (owner, "ONEER LOG CABIN"). The coloured kind marker stood on the plate over
     its left end, and the name, centred across the whole plate, ran under it, so every plate lost its first
     letters. The marker now stands just outside the plate's left end (signs.ts `landmarkPlates`). The smaller
     plates grow from 1.4 × 0.35 m to 1.8 × 0.45 m. Names are drawn with narrower margins, and two-line names
     at up to 40 px in the 96 px cell instead of 34. Checked on the Pioneer Log Cabin's and the SF
     Conservatory of Music's plates.
498. **"Observe from the top" of the de Young put the eye over the museum, not the tower** (owner). The observe
     point (game.ts `OBSERVE`) was the museum's centre, 65 m south-west of the Hamon Tower at the north-east corner.
     It now stands on the tower, taken from the model's own highest vertices (local −4954, −2336), with the eye
     just over the copper roof (top 47 m over the ground). At 42 m it stood inside the opaque glass top storey.
     Checked headless: jump, press O, the eye at (−4957, 33, −2336) looking out over the park.
499. **The Music Concourse's tree grid as real trees** (owner: "could just be real tree models instead of green
     boxes"). The concourse model (strawberry-hill.ts `musicConcourse`) drew each pollarded plane tree as a trunk,
     three limbs and a green hexagonal prism. Each grid point now hands the tree system a 1 m deck with trees on,
     shrubs off and a pitch wider than itself, so it plants exactly one tree from the city's library
     (vegetation.ts `addDeck`). Rendered along the promenade and from the air: rows of real trees either side
     of the fountains. Noted: the species come from the deck planting mix, so a few are palms. The real grid is
     all London planes; a species-locked planting would need a rule option.
500. **Ocean Beach, slice 1: the surf, the beach face and the sand** (owner: "simple waves crashing effect that is only
     ok … a more realistic waves crashing animation … better sand").
     - **A new surf for sandy beaches**, render/beach-surf.ts, on zones with `water: 'beach'` (Ocean Beach, Baker and
       China Beach; the rocks keep surf.ts). A strip along the coastline, 14 m up the sand to 160 m out, and one
       shader. Swells march in and stand up, a darker green face under each crest. Each wave breaks at its own
       distance (45–75 m out, varying along the shore and wave to wave), and its crest pitches up as a lip of up to
       ~2 m. A bore of white water runs in with a broad band behind its front and a lacy tail. Drifting streaks of
       foam lie over the surf zone, the shallows are paler and greener, and a churned fringe runs along the edge.
       At the shore the swash runs up the sand as a glassy film with a bright lacy edge (4–11 m, wave by wave),
       drains back, and leaves the sand dark for a few seconds. Heights are displaced in the vertex shader.
       `window.__surfTime` freezes the clock for contact sheets.
     - **The beach face.** The elevation data has the sand ~3 m over the sea at the waterline: the sand ended in a step
       with the sea painted below it. `Terrain.shore` (city/terrain.ts) now lowers the ground near a beach's
       coastline onto a 1-in-20 beach that runs into the water (`shoreRamp`; it never raises, and fades at the
       runs' ends). Set in game.ts before tiles drape.
     - **Sand** in its own mesh on the road shader's grainy sand page, with the wet/dry/dune bands still tinted
       per vertex; it was the ground material's long pale stripes, like decking. Tinted to Ocean Beach's pale grey-tan.
     The zone change needs the next bake; the v3 index (generated, ignored) was patched in place for now.
     Checked from the air, from 15 m and at rider height, with frames 2–4 s apart. **The motion itself is the
     owner's to judge.**

501. **Ocean Beach, slice 2: people lounging, towels, umbrellas** (owner: "people lounging on the beach; beach umbrellas
     and towels … we'll need this for other beach scenes"; swimwear: "all swimwear back" for beaches).
     - **Avatarkit** (dandyhorse 68bd3e8): four lounging clips derived from the clip library when it loads, `Lie_Back`,
       `Lie_Front` (arms folded under the head), `Sit_Ground` (legs out) and `Sit_Knees`. The lying poses set the legs
       straight, because the idle's stance otherwise lifts a foot off the ground. Also `beachSpec`/`BEACH_WARDROBE`:
       one-pieces, bikinis, trunks and board shorts, used for beaches only. And plain-geometry props: `towel`,
       `beachUmbrella`, `beachChair`. Their quads were wound the wrong way for Babylon, so they were culled from
       above, and towels didn't show until that was fixed.
     - **render/beach-crowd.ts**: 8 bodies in beachwear, each with one lounging clip baked into a vertex-animation
       texture (the street crowd's machinery, `instancedParts` now shared from crowd-baked.ts), then thin-instanced
       with per-instance skin, suit and hair colours. On the dry sand of `water: 'beach'` zones, 16–80 m up from the
       waterline, a 9 m grid with a group on 1 in 5 cells: 1–3 people on towels facing the sea, an umbrella planted
       behind the heads on 40% of groups, an empty sling chair on 18%. Props merge per tile into one mesh.
     - **Lift per body, measured**: the lowest skinned point over the loop sets each variant's root height. A fixed
       lift sank the seated bodies and floated the lying ones.
     - `window.__beachLineup(x, z)` sets every variant out in a row for judging poses. `?without=beachcrowd` turns the
       crowd off.
     Checked at rider height and close up (lineup, groups, umbrellas). The idle's breathing runs through the poses.
     **The motion is the owner's to judge.**

502. **The surf on the rocks** (owner: "add the same surf rendering to the cliffs/sutro baths area … and other areas with
     surf and waves (much of the shoreline)").
     - render/beach-surf.ts takes the `water: 'waves'` zones too (`beachRuns(…, 'waves')`; runs carry `rock`). The same
       rollers, breaking much closer in (14–30 m: the bottom drops away off the rocks). No rows up the land, no swash,
       no wet sand, no beach face. Instead the water boils white along the foot of the rocks as each bore slams in, then
       sucks back. The strip is shorter (62 m) and everything fades by ~45 m, so the strips from a cove's sides don't
       draw lines across each other. Wave faces are kept faint. Seal Rocks and the other islands get rings of foam (the
       sea's holes are runs too).
     - **Spray** as an upright ribbon along each rocky run, on the strip's own clock. Plumes burst up as the white
       water arrives (most a couple of metres, the odd point up to 7 m), hang, and fall away. The old render/surf.ts
       (flat foam quads and spray sheets on their own 7 s pulse) is deleted.
     - New zones: `presidio-cliffs-surf` (Baker Beach to Fort Point) and `marin-strait-surf` (Kirby Cove to Lime Point);
       Lands End/Sutro and Fort Point were already `waves`. The v3 index was patched in place; it needs the next bake.
     Checked from the cliff above Sutro Baths and from the Cliff House terrace across a wave cycle. **The motion is
     the owner's to judge.**

503. **Street benches** (owner: "benches on the street are almost always parallel to the street … all of ours are
     [perpendicular] … the geometry could be improved").
     - **Turned along the kerb.** The pavement loop's `heading` points a model's +z along the street; the bench is long
       in x, so every bench stood across the pavement. It now takes the normal towards the road: parallel to the
       street, facing the traffic. It stays in the lamp post's slot, because moving it to the building side put it
       against the racks and bins and onto stepped terraces. It is also kept off stair mouths, as the lamps are.
     - **A real bench** (models.ts `bench`, with a `slab` helper for tilted boxes): four hardwood seat slats raked back
       a touch and three reclined back slats, on two cast-iron end frames (front leg, rear leg rising into the back,
       seat rail, armrest on a post, feet), plus a middle support and a low stretcher. 1.8 m long, seat 0.45 m.
     Checked close up on six benches downtown and in the Mission.

504. **The Camera Obscura, and looking in it** (owner: "a really detailed model of the camera obscura / holograph
     gallery. we could even have a special feature where you push O to observe in the camera obscura").
     - **The model** (oddities.ts, way/168942987), from the owner's photographs: a pale yellow body lofted through
       chamfered rectangles (the ends cut steeply top and bottom, so the long side reads as a stretched octagon). A
       black/light-blue/black band all round it, black discs on the ends by the front. On the land side a blue portal
       with an arched opening, the ticket window and NOW OPEN, its cream doors folded back, and the black board CAMERA
       OBSCURA / AND / HOLOGRAPH GALLERY over it. GIANT CAMERA on the south end beside its door. On the roof a planked
       drum with a bulging blue ring between black rims, a grey-green shingled cone, the lens hood looking out to sea.
       It stands on the HIGHEST ground under it, on a plinth (from the lowest it sank a metre into the terrace).
     - **O inside it**: the observe list gains an `obscura` entry, offered within 45 m. The eye goes to the lens over
       the model's own roof, pitched down at the sea, and turns once in three minutes as the mirror does (drag still
       looks round). A post-process (render/obscura.ts) throws the frame onto a round white dish table in a dark room:
       an oval 2.4:1, the picture slightly swelled and bowed up at its sides by the dish, desaturated and cold, falling
       off to grey at the rim, with a little grain. O comes out and disposes it.
     Checked by jumping there, pressing O, waiting 20 s (it had turned) and pressing O again.
     **Follow-up**: only O tore the table down. Leaving by ⏸/P (the only way out on a phone) or a jump left the dish
     post-process on the camera and the yaw drifting. A shared `leaveTable` now runs from the pause toggle and from
     the tick's "no longer frozen" branch as well. Checked: O in, P out, `obscura` gone from the camera's
     post-processes.

505. **The sidewalk bench, redone** (owner: "the legs and arms are not aligned with the wood parts … we only need one
     bench, but it should look really good", with photographs of the permanent cast-iron-and-slat kind).
     models.ts `bench` is now built off ONE side profile: the curve the slats lie on, running forward along the seat and
     rounding up into a reclined back. Eleven weathered hardwood slats are laid on it at 8.5 cm pitch, each turned to
     the curve. Three black cast-iron frames (the ends and the middle) are the same curve lowered by a slat's
     thickness and stopped at the top slat, so the wood rests on the iron everywhere and nothing stands past it. Legs
     come down from points on that frame: the front splayed forward, the rear raked back, each on a foot. The end
     frames carry an arm, level from the back, scrolled over at the front and down onto the seat's edge. A low
     stretcher runs between the frames. `?model=bench` in the model showcase shows it alone.

506. **The windmills face the sea, and turn** (owner: "they should face the ocean, and they should really turn").
     - **Turning**: a new `rotors` list on MeshBuilder and render/rotors.ts, the clock hands' mechanism with the
       frame's clock. A builder draws the turning part about its hub at the origin into a builder of its own. When
       the model is placed it becomes a child mesh, spun about its axis at its `rpm`. The Dutch Windmill turns at
       9 rpm, Murphy at 7, each starting at its own angle.
     - **Lattice sails**: each sail is now the whip (main spar), a narrow leading rail, a broad trailing rail and a sail
       bar about every metre, from a fifth of the way out to the tip, plus the hub's stock. Before, each was a spar
       with one batten.
     - **Facing**: both entries were `front: "street"`, which pointed the sails at JFK Drive. The builder hangs the
       sails at local +z, and a numeric front puts local +z at the bearing + 180, so `front: 90` sets them on the
       west side. Measured from the placed rotors: Dutch 8.9 m due west of its tower, Murphy 8.8 m west and 4.4 m
       north (its footprint snugs the bearing a little).
     Checked from the Great Highway side, frames 1.2 s apart.

507. **The Legion of Honor, turned and rebuilt** (owner: "turned the wrong direction … it is supposed to face the
     fountain … the whole thing is a square of colonnades … the double colonnade in the foreground and then separate one
     in the background which is visible through the portico", with the aerial and three photographs).
     - **Turned**: parks.json had it at `rotateDeg` 139, so the arch faced south-east with the fountain at its side. It
       is now 49, so the arch faces north-east down the walk to the round fountain. Its `size` was [94, 60], wide
       across the entrance front; OSM's outline is 60 m along the front and 94 m deep, so it is now [60, 94].
     - **Rebuilt** (sf/legion.ts) as a square of colonnades round the Court of Honor:
       - **The front**: a double row of Ionic columns under a deck and a balustrade; the triumphal arch in the middle
         (piers through both rows, a round head stepped in courses, the attic); and the pavilions at each end, with a
         blind round-arched niche, a tall narrow one, a relief panel and a string course.
       - **The court**: single Ionic colonnades down both sides. Across the back, the hexastyle Corinthian portico,
         its entablature lettered HONNEUR ET PATRIE, a plain attic over it, the door and a relief panel behind. The
         colonnade carries on either side of it.
       - **Behind**: the gallery block with skylights and a copper-green dome.
       - **Outside**: the paved walk out of the arch between white railings.
       - The Thinker keeps only his plinth (statues are for the GLB pipeline). The glass pyramid, placed from OSM,
         lands inside the court. The stone is smooth now; the ashlar page read as grey blocks.
     Checked from the walk (the portico seen through the arch), from the air and inside the court.

508. **The Lands End Lookout** (owner: "the Lands End Lookout is pretty neat nearby too", two photographs). OSM
     way/250216260, a new entry in oddities.json and `landsEndLookout_250216260`:
     - **The front**: a long board-formed concrete wall (panel joints) along the front. A band of sea-green glazed
       tile lettered LANDS END LOOKOUT in pale metal capitals after the Park Service arrowhead, and 680 high at the end.
     - **Over the wall**: a row of clerestory windows in white frames, and a thin flat roof standing a metre out on a
       dark fascia. The building behind is weathered dark timber.
     - **The east end**: the wall turns into a concrete frame round a display window, and blue steel beams carry a
       canopy over the lime-green door. Red posts and a rope edge the planting.
     It is drawn with that end at +x, then mirrored, because seen from the front +x is on the viewer's left and the
     photographs have the name on the left. The stone lion is left for the GLB pipeline.

509. **The cliffs: rock, crags, and the clifftop un-smoothed** (owner: "anything we can do to improve the realism of the
     cliffs … I think perhaps our terrain smoothing is problematic on the falls and cliffs specifically").
     - **Measured first.** The transect above Lands End held dead flat at −34.6 m for 70 m, then fell in an even 37°
       ramp: the bake's 60 m opening had taken the clifftop off. Lands End's zone is now `relief: 'raw'`, as
       Strawberry Hill and Alcatraz are, and the city was rebaked (bake + map:v3, 2026-10-06; the previous tiles were
       backed up first). The clifftop now undulates as the data says (−31 to −35 m). The steepest fall is still 37°
       at Lands End and 30° at Sutro: the zoom-15 source itself has no steeper cliff, and a vertical one needs the
       lidar (queued).
     - **A rock surface** (render/rock-material.ts): Poly Haven rock photographs, copied from Trail Trainer's rockgen
       into public/rocks/textures (CC0, CREDITS.md). They are projected from the three world axes, blended by the
       normal (triplanar), so any face at any angle takes them at a constant 3.5 m scale. Two scales mix so it doesn't
       tile, and the colour is taken down from sunlit beige to greywacke's cool grey, with browner cracked patches
       tens of metres across. Lit by the city's own sun, sky and fog. This replaces the flat-shaded 2 m grey cells
       (the owner's "patchwork").
     - **Crags**: jointed blocks stand out of the steep ground every ~2.4 m (80%), each turned down the slope and
       sunk into it, with upright faces and level tops and a smaller one sometimes stacked behind. Each is broken,
       not sawn: corners at their own heights, the top drawn in, the front leaning back. On a 37° slope they make
       ledges and near-vertical faces. The old prism boulders are small blocks now.
     Checked from the north end of Ocean Beach (the owner's view: a dark jointed crag), at Sutro Baths and at the
     Cliff House. Still to do from the owner's photographs: wind-sheared cypresses, scrub on the faces, the seawall,
     Trail Trainer's boulder GLBs, the rock material on Seal Rocks.

510. **The path under the Cliff House went underground** (owner, 37.778580, -122.514224, reported while #509 was live).
     It was the new rock, not the ground: the footways there run 0.5–1 m over the terrain (measured edge by edge). The
     rock cells were drawn on every steep 2 m cell, paths included, and a crag could lap over a path whenever its
     centre was 4 m off the centreline. cliffs.ts's `onWay` now counts each way's half-width and pavement. Rock cells
     keep 1.5 m off a path's edge, crags keep their whole reach (corner distance + 0.8 m) clear, and boulders keep
     2.2 m. Checked from the path itself: paved and open, with the crags beside it.

511. **Seal Rocks, and a rock surface for every landmark** (the cliffs item: "the small offshore islands").
     - The landmark material gains a 12th surface, `rock` (cartoon-material.ts): the photographed rock from
       public/rocks/textures, sampled triplanar in world metres (every 4 m, and again at 13 m to break the tiling),
       cooled towards grey and tinted by the builder's colour. Any landmark builder can use it now (Huntington Falls
       next).
     - Seal Rocks (sf/rocks.ts) are crags of broken blocks heaped steepest at the heart: one big stack and five
       smaller around it, upright jointed faces and ledges, the high tops paler with the birds. They were smooth
       faceted cones.
     - The rocky surf's spray plumes were seen close up as glassy wedges with ruled edges. They are now softer at
       their sides, ragged along their tops, fainter, and faded out within 30 m of the eye.

512. **Masts standing on the Marina Green** (owner: "many vertical poles … not lined up with the boats and emerge from
     land"). The Marina East Harbor is placed `waterOnly`: every triangle over dry land is dropped. `City.dryAt` said
     "not dry" for any tile not yet loaded, and the marina is often built before the tiles of the land beside it.
     Its berths stayed on the Green under the lawn and the roads, at the water's level, with only the masts standing
     out of the ground. With no tile, `dryAt` now asks the package's whole water (`inWholeWater`: the water rings'
     edges bucketed in 8 m bands of z). Checked from above: the berths run to the harbour wall, every mast stands
     on its boat, and the Green and Marina Blvd are clear.

     **Not reproduced, with evidence (owner, please send a screenshot with the map pin):**
     - *Phantom Muni entrances.* All 59 `railway=subway_entrance` nodes in San Francisco (Overpass cache, the v2
       package and the v3 tiles, before and after today's rebake) lie within 250 m of a real Muni Metro / BART /
       Central Subway station. There are none near the Marina Green (none within 1 km), and nothing else draws the
       BART/Muni stair (render/city.ts point furniture is the only caller). The pair in the owner's screenshot
       stands with the misplaced masts behind it, so it may be another misplaced model of the Marina's; to look
       at again from the owner's exact view.
     - *Rows of stop signs* at 37.804839, -122.433732 and 37.786117, -122.445141. The data has 1 and 0 stop signs
       within 60 m of those points, and no group anywhere in the city of more than 6 within 25 m (all 2 866 checked).
       Rider-height views at both points show none. Stop signs are drawn only from OSM `highway=stop`.
     - *Field of red flags at USF.* The poles are the Muni bus-stop flag (steel pole, yellow band, orange-red flag)
       standing on the lawn where OSM maps a grove of trees. A fresh load there draws the trees: one bus stop within
       60 m, and no cluster of bus stops anywhere but the Transbay Terminal. **All four of these reports came in after
       today's rebake (14:22).** A session that was already running then can draw newly fetched tiles against its
       older index, so a reload is the first thing to try; if they're still there after one, I'll trace further.

513. **Caltrain: one train to a bundle of track** (owner: "WAYYYY too many trains at the caltrain station … around 20
     at once"). Every main-line rail took a train of its own, and the yard at 4th & King is a dozen parallel tracks
     in short pieces. render/life.ts now gives a main-line rail a train only if no rail that already has one runs
     alongside it (its middle within 45 m, its line within 25°). Measured: 5 heavy-rail trains within 500 m of the
     station (17 in the city); the rendered yard has a few standing trains.

514. **Church & 20th** (owner).
     - *The Golden Fire Hydrant* "does not look right": the barrel's top radius was 0.015 m, so it tapered to a spike
       and the bonnet sat on a point. It is now a proper stout barrel, with round outlets and caps instead of boxes.
       Checked in `?model=goldenHydrant`.
     - *The Randall Museum* (way/705309578) is its own model now (oddities.ts) instead of a bauhaus restyle: a long low
       cream block with a clerestory and a parapet, the entrance between two brick piers with red-framed glazed doors,
       a green canopy on posts, the dark name board and a row of flags on the roof. On its hillside, the uphill side
       is partly in the slope.
     - *Streetcar tracks as heavy rail.* Every rail within 900 m is `tram` + `light` in the data, both in the package
       and in the loaded tiles. The J's private right of way south of 20th draws as flush street track on the lawn.
       Not changed yet; the owner should say what reads as heavy rail.
     - *The "underground Muni".* No subway entrance within 1 km (see #512's audit).

515. **The 16th Avenue Tiled Steps** (owner: "another stray Muni stop on top of it"). A fresh load shows no entrance
     there either: the data has none within 1.5 km (#512). What a fresh load DID show was the generic concrete stairway
     (stairs.ts) drawn through the middle of the mosaic: OSM maps the flight as a dozen two-point `steps` ways and
     each drew its own treads, cheek walls and rails inside the model. The Tiled Steps and the Hidden Garden Steps
     now declare `coversRaised`, which takes their flights off the generic layer, as the BART plazas' are. Checked
     from the foot of the stairs: one mosaic flight between its own rails.

516. **Fountains: a water system, and the Legion of Honor's fountain** (owner: "a REALLY solid system for rendering
     fountains … a simple particle system … didn't work that well … DO NOT do statues").
     - **render/water.ts**: not particles. The water in a fountain is a few shapes that stand still while the water
       runs through them, so each is a fixed mesh with the running in its shader. A landmark builder lists its water
       on its MeshBuilder (`out.water`, like clock hands and rotors) and `Water.raise` parents the meshes to the
       placed model. One shader each for flow, pools and splashes; only the clock changes per frame.
       - **`jet`**: a nozzle and a velocity, with gravity giving the parabola; two crossed ribbons widening as the
         water spreads. White streaks travel along it at the water's own speed (uv.y is seconds of flight), from a
         glassy core at the nozzle through white water to drops at the end. A vertical jet stalls and falls back as
         a column.
       - **`sheet`**: a curtain over a lip at a given outflow speed, falling a given drop. For basins overflowing,
         cascades and falls.
       - **`pool`**: a surface over a ring, softly rippled, with glints and some sky.
       - **`splash`**: a disc where water lands, white churn at the middle and rings running out.
     - **The Legion of Honor's fountain** (node/4023038695, new `legionFountain` in parks.json). A round pool in a stone
       rim, a drum and a shallow bowl in the middle overflowing all round in sheets, a tall central jet falling back,
       a ring of 12 arching jets from the rim, splashes where they land. It is stood on the highest ground round its
       rim (the circle slopes). No statue. From the fountain the arch stands square behind it on the axis of the walk,
       which confirms #507's rotation on the ground.
     Checked from the drive and from above, frames 0.7 s apart. **The motion is the owner's to judge.**
     - **Huntington Falls** (owner: "reuse the water spray system for Huntington Falls") now falls in `sheet`s,
       each leaving its lip and landing in a `splash` on the ledge below, with a `pool` at the foot by the lake.
       The static white ribbons and the glass-tag disc are gone. The cliff's blocks take the photographed rock
       surface (#511), tinted by their own tan and brown.

     **Not reproduced: cars on "stairs" at 37.777348, -122.449592** (Golden Gate Ave by Roselyn Terrace, USF). I sampled
     every car within 150 m 60 times over 9 s. All 20 cars' heights move smoothly (no step over 0.35 m between frames
     on any edge), the street's baked level and the drawn road agree within 1 cm, and no `steps` way runs there. The
     motion is the owner's to judge: a short clip or the car's colour and direction would let me trace the one they saw.
     A second report (37.839184, -122.484926, Bunker Road by the Barry–Baker tunnel, Marin) measures the same way:
     every car's sim height moves smoothly, and road level and drawn road agree. What does show there is a cut road
     edged by retaining walls in straight panels, each a little higher or lower than the last; that may read as steps
     from the saddle. Still to identify with the owner. (A further suspect, not yet measured: carkit's per-wheel
     ground reading, ±ROLL on steep side-slopes.)

     **Lying beach loungers hold still** (owner: "npcs lying on the beach should be still, not a sideways standing
     animation"): the lying clips came from the standing idle, whose weight shift showed as a body sliding sideways.
     They now hold its first frame throughout (avatarkit e17a095).

517. **A road through the Harvey Milk Recreational Arts Center** (owner, with a screenshot). OSM's service way 857028540
     runs along the building's north wall at z −2589, where the outline's edge is −2588.9, so its 5 m carriageway ran
     half under the building and buses drove through it. It is suppressed in public/patches (both of its pieces,
     refs `s7990490773-8374070675` and `s7990490771-8374070675`). Checked from above: no road along the wall.

518. **Muni Metro tunnel portals** (owner: "At the east portal site we draw a bunch of trains just appearing and
     disappearing"). The bake drops track in a tunnel, so the surface track ended in the open and its trains popped in
     and out of nothing. New `tunnelPortal` (sf/portals.ts): a concrete headwall and lintel over a dark mouth for two
     tracks, wing walls stepping down, the dark tunnel running 30 m back, and the hill over it. Placed at all five
     places in the box where a light-rail tunnel meets surface track (found from the Overpass cache: tunnel ways
     whose end is shared with a surface way). Each is snapped to the midpoint of the two dead-end tram tracks there
     and turned to them: the East Portal (Duboce/Church), both ends of the Sunset Tunnel, the Central Subway's 4th St
     portal and the Embarcadero portal. West Portal is outside the box. Numeric `front` is the bearing INTO the
     tunnel: the same +180 convention the windmills taught (#506), checked from both sides here. Checked at the
     Sunset Tunnel's east portal: a train runs out of the dark mouth.

519. **Five small buildings from the owner's photographs** (sf/batch-1006.ts, manifest san-francisco/batch-1006.json):
     - the **Presidio Religious Activities Center** (way/288325535): a white clapboard chapel, red shingle roof, tall
       side windows, a brick chimney, a square belfry and a tall red shingled spire over the front gable, a little
       gabled porch on posts;
     - the **Fort Mason Chapel** (way/288387369, unnamed in OSM, 29 m from the post's flagpole node): white stucco
       Mission style, a coped front gable with a quatrefoil, a tiled hood over the arched door up its steps, a square
       bell tower with arched openings and a red tile cap, and the ~15 m flagpole in front;
     - the **General's Residence** (way/288387355, was a presidio restyle): cream clapboard, two storeys, bracketed
       eaves, dark red window frames, the X-braced porte-cochère, the low wing;
     - **Haskell House** (way/288387358): white clapboard, angled two-storey bays on brackets, the lattice-skirted
       porch and its stair;
     - the **former park restaurant, 902 Point Lobos** (way/168942990, 1937, disused): red brick, a hipped roof,
       boarded windows in pale surrounds; it was a house.
     The chapels are drawn along x and turned (door to the manifest's front). The houses and the restaurant stand
     on the HIGHEST ground under them on a plinth: the restaurant's walls were sunk into Point Lobos to 1 m.
     Rendered from the front and above.

520. **Fort Mason Center** (owner: "we're not really doing the Fort Mason Center, which is old military buildings
     like the Presidio", photograph of the gate). Landmark Buildings A–E were `presidio` restyles. They are now
     `fortMasonBlock` (batch-1006.ts): the long cream stucco army blocks with red clay tile hip roofs oversailing,
     small-paned windows in dark red frames, little gabled vents along the ridge, and a red steel fire stair zig-
     zagging down an end wall. New `fortMasonGate` on Marina Blvd: square cream stucco piers with hipped tile caps,
     the arched FORT MASON CENTER plaques and arrowheads, tiled walls stepping out. It stands between the two old
     gatehouses (Equator Coffees and its twin), which were Victorians and are now `presidio`-restyled.

521. **The Wind Arrows** (owner's photographs): `windArrows` (batch-1006.ts). A 12 m white pole with 22 slim rods up one
     side, each tipped with a red or dark arrowhead vane. Every vane is a rotor on its own vertical pivot, turning
     slowly at its own pace and direction (1–2.4 rpm), so the column flickers and is never in step. **Placement is a
     guess**: OSM doesn't map it, so it stands by the rowing clubs' boat ramp at the foot of Hyde Street (the
     photograph looks across Aquatic Park's beach to the Maritime Museum, the Golden Gate beyond). The owner should
     confirm the spot.

522. **Alcatraz and the island ferries** (owner).
     - *Default SF houses with garages on Alcatraz*: the island's restyles were `stucco`, whose ground storey is
       GARAGES. They are `presidio` now (stoop and sash windows, no garages), flat-roofed, every unmodelled building
       on the island included (the broken cottage among them). Colours from the owner's photographs: prison buildings
       grey (Administration Block, Dining Hall; the cellhouse model's concrete is now a pale pinkish cream), the rest
       white. A 30 m concrete smokestack stands by the Powerhouse (position approximate: OSM has no node for it).
       Roofs flat and grey (the presidio palette's red tile showed as flat red roofs). Checked from above: no cottages,
       no garages, no red roofs. Still to do: the second water tower the owner mentions (where?).
     - *The ferry home from an island is free* (stations.ts `fareFrom`, `island: true` on Treasure Island, Alcatraz
       and Angel Island): "there's no way to make money on any island".

523. **Boulders from Trail Trainer** (owner: "a better system for rocks and boulders … for the cliffs, the small
     offshore islands, and the waterfall"). render/boulders.ts loads a coastal subset of Trail Trainer's rockgen
     library (14 GLBs, dark/grey/cracked singles and groups; copied into public/rocks, gitignored, index.json
     committed; their Poly Haven CC0 textures beside them). Each GLB's LOD0 is loaded once, with the __root__ flip
     baked into its vertices. In the cliffs zones, per tile, boulders are scattered by the water's edge (55% of 5 m
     candidates) and on steep ground (22%), turned and scaled each its own way, bedded into the slope, kept clear of
     every path's width. Each tile carries a geometry-sharing clone of each model with that tile's thin instances.
     The layer is added once the models have loaded.

524. **Models from the owner's photographs** (batch-1006.ts):
     - **Spreckels Mansion** (way/257004417, was a terracotta recipe): white limestone Beaux-Arts on a rusticated
       base, fluted Ionic pilasters through two storeys, arched French windows with cartouches and bowed iron
       balconies, a modillioned cornice and a balustrade, a bowed bay on the garden side.
       Its **greenhouse** (way/257004424, drawn as a half-sunk Victorian): a long glass conservatory on a stone sill,
       white bars, a barrel glass roof, a rounded glass end.
     - **Beach Chalet** (`beachChalet1006`): a long two-storey block under a red tile hip, the upstairs band of
       small-paned windows in dark red frames all round, the colonnade of paired round columns before arched
       doorways, the name.
     - **Painted Ladies**: fish-scale courses and a sunburst in each gable with a finial, the spindlework frieze and
       paired brackets at the eaves, colonnettes on the bays, an arched porch hood on colonnettes, a garage under the
       bay.
     - **The Great Highway's beachfront**: 26 townhouses at 700–798 Great Highway (`beachTownhouse_<id>`, each its own
       pastel, a gabled or flat roof, a bowed or square many-paned bay over a garage, a chimney) and the stepped cream
       condominiums at 1224–1361 (`steppedCondo`: four storeys terraced back, balconies on each step, glazed
       sun-rooms with curved glass roofs, chimneys with red caps). They were office blocks.
     - **Bay Model Visitor Center** (way/30680616, was a bauhaus restyle): a row of barrel vaults ending in
       semicircular arches to the water over shadowed porches.
     - **Taj Mahal houseboat** (OSM has none; placed in Sausalito Yacht Harbor, position approximate): a white Mughal
       pavilion on a float, a cusped centre arch under floral relief, two chhatris with onion domes, pinnacles, jali
       rails.

525. **USF** (owner's photographs): **Harney Science Center** (fins, scalloped parapet, stair towers with grilles);
     **Kalmanovitz Hall**, 2081 Fulton (white 1920s block, grouped windows, end pavilions, the centre bay with
     pedimented door and cartouche, the retaining wall); **Kendrick Hall** (red granite piers, the lettered canopy, the
     curved glass lobby, brick steps); the **War Memorial Gym at the Sobrato Center** (folded panel wall, zig-zag
     canopy, the lettered end block, the planted terrace); **St Ignatius** improved (five arched doors with medallions,
     ST. IGNATIUS CHURCH on the frieze, the balustrade, steps the full width). Lone Mountain North, Loyola House and
     Loyola Village are `presidio` (cream, red tile). Not found in OSM: the monastery at 721 Parker (owner, a link or
     the building on the map?).

528. **MIRA's roof, and the Embarcadero Plaza block** (owner). MIRA's builder drew its spiralling bays and the jagged
     parapet, but nothing over the shaft: from above it was hollow. It now has a roof plate and a plant screen.
     The block between the Hyatt and Embarcadero Center 4 at Drumm & Clay (ways 7656237 and 7656235, no name in OSM)
     was a grey Chicago-school building with shop signs and a penthouse box. It is now pale and modernist, without
     shopfronts. First pass: in the owner's view it stands in shade, and I couldn't identify the building to model
     it, so a name or photograph would help.

553. **The Embarcadero, ride-height survey** (owner: "keep working on embarcadero area until it is great"). 22 stops
     150 m apart from China Basin to Pier 39, looking inland and to the bay (scratch embride.mjs). Found:
     - **A 30 m black wall with a lawn on top in the median** by Howard: the Muni portal I placed in #530-ish as a hill
       portal. In a median there is no hill: new `rampPortal` (portals.ts), the track going down between low parapets
       to a low headwall over a dark mouth. Also used for the Central Subway's portal on 4th Street.
     - **The pier bulkheads read crude** (piers.ts, every Embarcadero pier): cold grey stucco, black square holes,
       a portal like a box. Now warm cream stucco, glazing behind the Port's sea-green sash (mullions and transoms),
       round-headed upper windows on the historic fronts, the portal under a solid round head with voussoirs and a
       keystone, a heavier cornice.
     - **Broken twigs lying on the lawns** (Pier 27's park, and every park): the shrub layer drew the library's
       sparse LOD1 cards, which at rider height read as debris. Now LOD0, a looser leaf cutoff, and a leafy core in
       each shrub — a smooth lumpy mound dressed in the shrubs' own leaf texture — so they read as bushes. Cost:
       Stow Lake's active indices 31.5 M → 38.7 M; frame time inside the run-to-run noise (32-55 ms).

552. **Billboards z-fighting** (owner). Two causes: the ad's layers (background, photo, words, product) were drawn 1-6 mm
     apart, which flickers at the distance a billboard is read from — now 1-6 cm, as thin slabs, clear of the frame;
     and OSM maps two of the city's 19 billboards twice, 2-8 m apart, so two 14.6 m boards stood through each other
     (Mission Bay) — a billboard within 16 m of one already drawn in its tile is now skipped. Rendered: one clean board.
