551. **Rincon Center is the whole block** (owner, with an aerial and a Street View: "rincon center is huge"). #544's
     model was a generic square podium. Now built on OSM's own parts (embarcadero-south.ts `towersAtRincon`): the
     pale panelled podium wings on tall square piers with ribbon windows, the one-storey glazed round court between
     the wings, and the two 93 m towers — banded, a balcony slab every floor round each, stepping back twice to a
     glass lantern and a mast. The entry now claims all nine parts (the podium part 944891685 had still been drawn
     as a brick loft inside the model). The Rincon Annex (the old post office on the north half) keeps its Deco
     model and gains the 1989 atrium's long glass roof down its middle (rebuild-e15.ts).

549. **Golden Gate Heights and the Sunset were Victorians** (noticed while looking at the stairways; the real
     neighbourhood is 1920s-50s stucco). Two causes in the building classifier (src/city/sf/btype.ts):
     - a house built after 1945 had no era rule and fell through to the 25-foot-lot default, `rowhouse` (Victorian).
       Now `postwar-house`: stucco (a few post-1980 ones contemporary). 9 184 buildings citywide.
     - the city had no district west of the Western Addition, so a house with no Assessor record there was a
       Victorian too. New `sunset` district (Golden Gate Heights, the Sunset, Parkside; districts.json): its houses
       are stucco, and the interwar wood-frame rowhouse roll no longer applies there.
     Over Golden Gate Heights: 3 343 stucco, 462 Edwardian (dated 1906-24 in the record), 43 Victorian (was 1 616
     narrow-lot Victorians). Rendered: stucco rows with garages and tile roofs.
550. **Performance audit, second pass.** tools/perf.ts at four spots (headless, 1600×900 at 2×): downtown 34 ms,
     Embarcadero 27 ms (the new models cost nothing visible), Lands End ~75 ms and Stow Lake ~55 ms. Frame timings
     swung by ±10 ms run to run (the owner's Chrome and a Time Machine backup were on the GPU), so the comparisons
     were alternated, three rounds each:
     - **Trees** are the big cost in the woods: without them Lands End drops ~77 → 48 ms and 37.6 M → 9.6 M indices;
       Stow Lake ~56 → 46 ms. Per tile the trees are 3 M triangles at the middle detail level (90–225 m), 1 M near,
       0.7 M far. Pulling the far switch in to 160 m changed nothing measurable; the cost is the leaf cards' FILL
       (alpha-cutout overdraw), as #417 found for the park.
     - **The fine ground occluder** costs ~8-10 ms at Lands End (drawn once per rendering group, three copies of a
       70-84 k triangle mesh per tile there).
     Levers, each a visible trade and so the owner's call: desktop maxDpr 1.5 (2-6 ms everywhere); fewer, larger
     leaf cards on the zone-planted woods (Lands End, Golden Gate Park); a coarser occluder in the fine-ground zones.

548. **Lidar cliffs at Lands End and Sutro** (owner: the falls and cliffs "have very steep dropoffs that look wrong when
     smoothed over"). After `relief: 'raw'` the zoom-15 elevation tiles still topped out at ~37° at Lands End and ~30°
     at Sutro: the data itself is blurred. New tools/lidar/dtm.py reads the USGS 3DEP 2023 lidar's ground-class points
     over every zone marked `lidar: true` — only the Entwine octree's levels 0–7 (~3 points/m², a twentieth of the
     download) — and writes the median ground height of each 4 m cell on the bake's own lattice
     (tools/cache/lidar/dtm-<city>.json, gitignored, rebuilt by the tool in ~2 min). The bake lays it over the fine
     ground in those zones, sampled bilinearly, shifted by the median difference between the two over the zone (3 cm
     at Lands End: the datums agree), water and lidar-less posts left alone. Lands End and Sutro Heights is marked;
     116 603 posts replaced. Measured in the running game over the zone: steepest 4 m step 81° (was 37°), 1 876 cells
     over 45° and 351 over 60°; the Lands End transect 50° (was 37°). air-paths over 900 m round it: 0 stretches in
     the air or buried, on foot or bike. Then Alcatraz (4 331 posts), Strawberry Hill under Huntington Falls
     (3 235) and the Yerba Buena light-station cliff (1 165) took the flag too, datum shifts under 15 cm; the falls
     still sit on the hill, and the paths there are clean. Point Bonita is outside the 2023 survey: the tool skips a
     zone it has no data for.

547. **The Embarcadero pass, the rest of the frontage** (owner: "look at EVERY building on mapillary and do a custom
     build"). Every building within 35 m of the Embarcadero from China Basin to Fisherman's Wharf was listed (OSM, over
     150 m²) and checked for a manifest entry; 33 were still generic (brick lofts, Chicago blocks, a few bauhaus
     boxes). Each was looked at in Mapillary (tools/sf-photos.ts, 3-4 frames each, contact sheets) and given its own
     treatment in the new public/landmarks/san-francisco/embarcadero-pass.json — a rules-style restyle where the
     building is ordinary (wall colour, trim, shops or not, roof), a builder where it is not:
     - **188 The Embarcadero** (Bayside Plaza, Google): `baysidePlaza`, the curved front of white bands and blue
       ribbon glass stepping back in terraces.
     - **The Franciscan** (Pier 43½): `franciscan`, a glass pavilion ringed by white fins under a wide roof slab.
     - Restyles, among them: Steamboat Point's red-brick apartments with the round corner tower, 160 Brannan and
       Bayside Village in stucco, 2 Bryant's white office, the Pier 1½-3 and Pier 30-32 bulkheads in pale classical,
       1 Harrison and the ABC Broadcast Center in cream concrete, Rincon Park's dark restaurant pavilions, the Steuart
       Street row, One Lombard's red brick, 1 Beach and 2 North Point in beige concrete, the Pier 39 garage, Boudin
       (grey, gabled), Fisherman's Grotto (white under red tile) and Pier 41 (white, dark hip roof).
     The Ferry Building, the Audiffred, Harbor Court, Golden Gateway Commons and the piers already had models.
     Still to do on this line: the Fisherman's Wharf restaurants' signs, and a closer look at each model once the owner
     has ridden it.

546. **The gravel gardens between One Steuart Lane and 201 Spear** (owner: "there's also a plaza between those
     buildings"). OSM maps two gravel (`surface=compacted`) pedestrian beds there with one-step `steps` ways in and out of
     them; we drew plain paving with six white stair flights and rails standing on the flat. Now
     embarcadero-south.ts `steuartGravelGardens`: grey gravel beds edged with low black stone walls under paler bench
     caps, gaps to walk in, and inverted-cone lamps along the walk; the six flights suppressed in the patches.

545. **Hills Plaza** (owner: "further down … the red-brick-and-white-stone complex with a stepped white tower in the
     middle … rendered as a glass square building"). Two old mistakes:
     - the **Hills Brothers Building** (relation 2280956) had been refaced by a Haiku pass as grey brutalist concrete
       from one photograph — red brick with cream trim now, no shopfronts;
     - its **tower** (way 944986473, OSM 50 m) had been built as a 14 m brick loft: a batch agent believed close-up
       street photos of the arched brick base over the tag. It is the tower — embarcadero-south.ts `hillsPlazaTower`,
       cream stone with punched windows over an arched red-brick base, two setbacks and a stepped crown (the old
       b2 builder removed).

544. **The Embarcadero pass: 201 Spear, One Steuart Lane, Rincon Center** (owner: "the brick building (201 Spear St) is
     very unusual too"; "rincon center towers"). All three were generic glass-tower recipes from web descriptions.
     From Mapillary, in embarcadero-south.ts:
     - **201 Spear** (`twoOOneSpear`): salmon-red precast cut into a deep grid of square windows, stepping back in
       tiers to a stepped crown.
     - **One Steuart Lane** (`oneSteuartLane`): a stack of glass blocks of three to four floors, each slid off the
       one below, framed in pale metal with white slab edges.
     - **Rincon Center / The Towers at Rincon** (`towersAtRincon`, standalone over the bake's outline): a stone podium
       with a glazed barrel vault and a glass pyramid on its roof, and the two pale towers with stacked balconies,
       each ending in a round glazed drum, a stepped crown and a finial. It was one 104 m glass block.

543. **The Embarcadero pass, first buildings: the Infinity twins and 2 Folsom** (owner: the buildings at Howard/Folsom "just
     plain buildings"; "the two tall curved buildings are identical i believe"). Mapillary for each (tools/sf-photos.ts).
     - **The Infinity I and II** (301 Main, 338 Spear) were two different generic glass-tower recipes. One builder now,
       embarcadero-south.ts `infinityTower`, for both: green glass with the floors' pale slab edges across it, two
       setbacks near the top, a parapet, on each tower's own elliptical footprint.
     - **2 Folsom Street** was a plain blue glass box, its note saying no photograph was found; Mapillary has several:
       a deep grid of dark red-brown granite with recessed windows, granite piers at the base (`twoFolsom`). OSM's
       91 m for 15 storeys was 6 m a floor; 62 m.

542. **The Wave Organ** (owner, a screenshot from the jetty's tip: "i'm not sure what it's supposed to be"). Two things
     made the jumble: two short OSM `steps` ways on the flat jetty, drawn as white flights with cheek walls and black
     rails (suppressed in the patches), and the organ itself, a scatter of white boxes and black posts. Re-cut
     (park-sights.ts `waveOrgan`) as a small grey stone amphitheatre: a horseshoe wall of two courses of big granite
     blocks round the back, three tiers of coursed seats inside it, steps going down into the bay at the open side, a few
     marble column drums and a fallen fluted shaft, and the pipes standing up through the seats in grey concrete with
     pale flared mouths.

541. **Marina Branch Library** (owner: "needs a custom model"). It was restyled as a classical Carnegie cottage, which it
     never was: Mapillary on Chestnut shows a 1954 single storey of mid-century modern. Now marina-library.ts: red
     brick with a long ribbon of green glazing on thin mullions to Chestnut (turning the corners for a bay), a brick pier
     by the glass doors, a thin flat roof slab overhanging deep over the front with the name along its edge, brick
     planters in the forecourt. (`front: "street"` faced it to the park behind; numeric front 0 puts it on Chestnut.)

540. **Galileo High School** (owner's Street View on Polk: "we have nothing for Galileo High School"). It was a stucco
     restyle of the bake's courtyard block, drawn pink, three storeys, under a flat orange roof. Now galileo.ts on the
     bake's own outline and courtyard: cream walls over a rusticated pinkish base storey with its joints and string
     course, rows of tall windows, a band under the top floor, the deep cornice and its underside, a low tiled roof
     edge round the outside and the courtyard, and on Polk the arcaded loggia stair tower — two round arches a storey,
     balcony rails, a pergola on its roof. (The ec4-podium.ts outline helpers are shared for this.) Owner, next
     morning: "the pergola is floating above the building" — it stood over the tile slope; it has its own roof deck
     over the loggia now and its posts stand on it.

539. **Golden Gate Park: a path into the air, and walkers above the hill** (owner, at 37.767296, −122.4729, by Stow Lake).
     Three things, all the same mismatch — the park's grass is draped on the RAW ground, everything else here on the
     streets' LEVELLED ground (up to 1.8 m apart on this hill):
     - **The "path into the air"** was a pale concrete corridor, the city's treatment, on the drives round Stow Lake. A
       way is "in the park" by its middle, and each tile asked that of its own copy of the way: a way crossing into
       the next tile had its middle THERE, outside every ring of this tile's mask, so this tile's piece was city. The
       test is now the middle of the stretch inside the tile (park-area.ts `midWithin`). And a tile mostly lake (Stow
       Lake's) counts its water toward the park threshold when it is not built up.
     - **The trails** (park foot ways) are drawn on the raw ground, as the grass is (city.ts trails, drapeRaw).
     - **The walkers** on a park's way stand on the raw ground too (crowd.ts `park`, life.ts, crowd-baked.ts).
     - **The rider** on a park trail stands on the raw ground as well (game.ts).
     Not changed: the cars and a rider on a park DRIVE still follow the edges' baked (levelled) heights, so over a
     hill they can sit up to ~1–2 m off the drawn road. The proper fix is for the bake not to level park ways at all;
     noted for later.

538. **A football field, and Ghirardelli Square** (owner: "the large field at 37.804669, -122.424291 is actually the high
     school football field. we should add that as a type of court (much larger than the others)"; Ghirardelli "needs
     more work … random houses in the middle", "unique colored steps on the water side").
     - **American football** is a court kind (sports.ts `football`; OSM `sport=american_football` anywhere): 120 × 53⅓
       yd of turf in a margin, blue end zones, a line every 5 yards (heavier at the goal lines and midfield), hash marks
       and sideline ticks, yellow goalposts; a practice on it (players.ts). Built in 6 m cells and 5 m pieces of line,
       each draped — one quad 110 m across dipped under the ground's rise and fall and the ground occluder ate it.
       George White Field (way 32839492) is tagged rugby and named, which kept it out of the kit; the region's
       `courtSports` now also overrides the landmark exclusion, since it was looked at. One other field in the city is
       tagged american_football and is drawn too.
     - **Ghirardelli**: only the clock-tower block was styled; the dozen other buildings of the square (Wurster, Plaza,
       Power House, Mustard, Clock Tower, Apartment House, Carousel, Infill, Chocolate, Woolen Mill and two small ones)
       were generic SF houses. All red brick with cream trim now (batch-1006.json). The "little round house" in the
       middle was OSM's gazebo shelter (way 939539790) mapped as a building: suppressed in the patches.
     - **Rainbow steps**: Region `rainbowStairs` (like the Castro's rainbow crossings): a stairway with its middle in
       the circle gets brick-red treads and risers banded through the colours. Ghirardelli's flight up from Beach Street
       (way 939539827). The ground there rises little in our terrain, so only a few risers show.

537. **Embarcadero Center 4's terraces on the plaza** (owner's Street View: "note it is concrete to match the
     surrounding buildings"). The block between EC4 and the Hyatt (the bake's buildings 7656237 and 7656235, which are
     not OSM way ids — #528's restyle of them never took) is now its own model, ec4-podium.ts, on the bake's outline:
     board-formed concrete stepping back in two planted terraces — shops at plaza level behind square piers under a
     deep spandrel, the glazed Harbor View band on the first terrace, Sens set back under a deep overhang lettered
     SENS RESTAURANT — the round banded core rising through them, the curved glass-railed double stair from the plaza
     up to the first terrace, and the arched steel EMBARCADERO CENTER gateway at its foot. (Two traps on the way: an
     entry whose name says "podium" or "plaza" is treated as a deck and does not clear the buildings under it, so the
     old block stood inside the new one; and a flat downward-facing quad drew nothing, so the soffits are thin slabs.)

536. **Embarcadero Plaza: padel courts, and the bowling lawn** (owner: "the pickleball courts in the plaza are wrapped
     in glass", "there are actually only 2"; "the area next to the plaza is odd, criss-crossed osm paths. reality is
     it's a palm-tree-lined plaza with two lawn bowling lanes … and grass", at 37.794421, −122.394226).
     - **Padel** is a court kind of its own now (src/city/sports.ts, render/sports.ts `padel`), for any OSM
       `sport=padel` in any city: 20 × 10 m of green turf, the service lines and net, black steel posts every 2 m,
       glass to 3 m at the ends with mesh above to 4 m, the side walls glass for the first 2–4 m then mesh, a door
       gap each side, and a tall light pole curving in over each corner. The glass and mesh are drawn see-through
       (city.ts `courtGlass`), so the court and its players show through the walls. OSM tags these courts
       `pickleball`; the region's new `courtSports` (regions.json) makes the side-by-side pair (ways 1442051147,
       1550914660) padel. **For the owner:** the aerial and OSM show a THIRD court, turned, to the east (way
       1442051148): it is left as an open pickleball court — say if it is gone or is padel too.
     - **The lawn**: the hand-paved brick square (pavedSquares) was drawn last, over everything inside it, so the
       lawn and its two `sport=boules` pitches were brick. A hand-paved square now goes over the park but under the
       lawns, beds and pitches mapped inside it. The path ring OSM traces along the lawn's own edge (way 196662083)
       and the path down the seam between the lanes (196662081) are suppressed in the patches: their ribbons and
       5-way junction pads covered the lawn in grey.
     - **The lanes** (embarcadero-plaza.ts `bocceLanes`): two gravel lanes in a low concrete kerb, blue steel
       railings across their ends, the big granite balls on plinths; draped on the ground vertex by vertex (the lawn
       is sunken, and a landmark stands at the lowest ground in its box).
     - (I first wrote the lanes into a NEW `sf/embarcadero.ts` — a name already taken by the Embarcadero Center deck
       network. The bake audit stopped on the missing builders; restored from HEAD before anything was baked.)

535. **Market at 4th and at 5th** (owner: signal poles in the middle of the street, "we could just remove some";
     a lamppost in the street; buses constantly stuck).
     - **Signal posts**: on Market, mapped as several parallel ways, an arm's "near-side kerb" is often the next
       carriageway over. A post that would stand in any carriageway (Carriageways.whyNoFurniture) is now left out,
       with its lamps; that arm's traffic keeps its timed light. The Path of Gold lamps test for a median 7 m out on
       Market (was 4).
     - **The jam**: measured over 90 s at Market & 5th, a ring of queues with every car waiting for 'no room beyond'
       on the short connectors between Market's parallel ways. The valve that retires a car stuck for 35 s works only
       out of view. Now a car standing 20 s stops asking for room and goes on its green, rolling only to contact
       (the look-ahead valve). Measured: share of time standing 0.71 → 0.67, cars locked the whole time 9 → 6. Better,
       not cured: the junction's geometry (two parallel Market ways meeting through short connectors) is the root and
       is still to rework.

534. **Market's red lanes torn in patches** (owner: "red pavement looks bad here", Market at 6th). Three causes,
     found by switching the group's meshes off one prefix at a time:
     - mostly the **depth occluder**: the carriageway is drawn in the group below, untouched by it, but the paint is
       in the buildings' group, and wherever the occluder's coarse triangles stood over the street they cut it away.
       Near the eye (250 m) the markings now draw before the occluder, as tunnel mouths do; farther off they stay
       after it, so a street behind a hill keeps its paint hidden;
     - the graded carriageway (`flatroad`, same group, 4 cm up on a straight line) came through where it parted from
       the draped paint: the markings mesh is lifted 6 cm, its own layers in their order;
     - wide bands (6.6 m) are cut across into strips of 1.2 m or less, so they follow the road's crown instead of
       hanging under it as a chord.
     What's left are the 3 m gaps where the paint stops short of each junction, as designed. Checked from above the
     junction.

533. **One Front Street's pavilion** (owner's Street View): way/148710885 was a "Battery Street infill" restyled
     Chicago-school brick. It is now `oneFrontPavilion`: a two-storey wing in the tower's silver aluminium, Philz
     Coffee's glass front with umbrellas and tables on the brick plaza, the dark glass band under the green FIRST
     REPUBLIC BANK board, and a curved aluminium canopy on a round column at the tower's entrance.

532. **Water in the Vaillancourt Fountain** (owner: "has no water"). The basin rim's prism was capped, and its top
     covered the whole basin at 0.4 m. Also, from the model's base (the lowest ground) the basin floor lay under the
     plaza's paving, behind the terrain occluder. The fountain now stands on the highest ground round its basin on a
     concrete plinth, the rim is open, and render/water.ts fills it: a pool, falls pouring from six tube mouths,
     four jets arcing from the high tubes, white water where they land.

531. **Sausalito's pier restaurants** (owner: "small piers on the waterfront, which seem to all be restaurants now").
     A `pierRestaurant` builder (batch-1006.ts): a timber deck on piles, a low clapboard house with a wall of windows
     to the bay, a railing round the deck's end, the name over the door. The Barrel House stands behind 660 Bridgeway
     (position approximate). Scoma's pier already carries OSM's own buildings (now the Cape Cod style), so it keeps
     them; a second Scoma's drawn there was removed.

530. **Angel Island's landmarks** (owner): the **Point Blunt** and **Point Stuart** lights (`angelLight`: a white fog-
     signal house with a red hipped roof and a white tower carrying a black lantern, on the highest ground), and the
     Immigration Station's **Detention Barracks** (`detentionBarracks`: long, two-storey, white clapboard on a raised
     concrete base, rows of double-hung windows in green trim, a grey hipped roof, the end stair to its porch). Fort
     McDowell, Camp Reynolds, the rest of the Immigration Station and the Ayala Cove quarantine station take the
     #527 restyles (army white/red tile, white clapboard). No cars on the island.

529. **Straight on along the Embarcadero at Washington** (owner: "very hard to go straight"). OSM's southbound
     Embarcadero jogs west into the junction to meet Washington Street and jogs back out. So its continuation leaves
     at ~108° by the first 8 m `exits` reads, while Washington, straight across, leaves at 46°. Worse, the
     continuation was folded away as a duplicate of Washington's east arm (within 20°), so it wasn't offered at all.
     `rideExits` (city/ridegraph.ts) now:
     - sorts the arrival street's own continuation first, so the dedup keeps it;
     - where exactly one exit carries on the street the rider is on (same trunk name) and runs within 70° of the
       arrival judged over 30 m each way (past the jogs), makes that exit 'straight', first in the list, at its true
       angle; anything else that was 'straight' becomes a turn by its side.
     This is at the choice only; the baked graph is unchanged (ride files rebuilt, ridefile test passes). Measured:
     arriving southbound, the offers are now Embarcadero straight, Washington right, Washington left (was:
     Washington right, Herb Caen Way left, no Embarcadero). A first attempt that moved the merged junction node
     onto a named street's axis moved it only 2 m and was reverted.

526. **Sailboats off Ocean Beach** (sf/boats.ts): a five-boat route 700–900 m offshore.
